This is **E-device-only** applied to the gate itself, and it is remedy 3 of [`pr-gate-budget.md`](../tests/agent-e2e-mobile/flows/pr-gate-budget.md) — "move a claim down a tier", which that document calls the correct first move rather than the last resort.

**Twelve minutes stands, and is now measurable.** The number was derived, never observed: it priced the four reusing journeys at ~6 minutes, and run 33573882728 measured them at ~9 — the ten-surface tour alone was 187s of it. The suite is therefore fitted to the budget rather than the budget to the suite: the tour moves down a tier, and six Maestro spawns (four reuse-mode gateway configures, two restarts) at ~9–15s each fold into their neighbours' chunks. The four device lanes now upload `ledger/durations.json` as `mobile-run-ledger-<lane>`, so the re-derivation the budget owes after three real runs reads data rather than arithmetic.

**The five ran as two parallel legs, and twelve minutes was per leg.** Ruled 2026-09-02 and **superseded the same day** by **G-merge-diet** ([#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915)), which makes the smaller cut: `native-v0-resilience` moves to rung 3 and the gate keeps three members at 8 minutes warm on one emulator. The finding below is why the split existed and is unchanged. Even fitted to the budget the five price ~795s in sequence against a 720s deadline — run 33582899886 measured `pairing-canary` 187s, `notes-library` 106s, `native-v0-resilience` 267s, `cold-start` 145s, and `photos-permissions` starved by the clamp at 24s where it needs ~90s — so the last member is starved on every run. `mobile-device-gate` is one job with a two-leg matrix — paired (`pairing-canary`, `notes-library`, `photos-permissions`) and resilience (`pairing-canary`, `native-v0-resilience`, `cold-start`) — on two emulators, each pairing itself, each under the unchanged twelve-minute deadline. The gate's wall time is its slower leg. The accepted cost is one extra pairing per run, ~190s of runner time paid twice instead of once. **This is not the budget raise `pr-gate-budget.md` forbids**: the bound a human waits on is unchanged, no member left the gate and no assertion was loosened — runner minutes are the currency the shape spends, and they are the one thing nobody is waiting on.

### The merge queue: evaluated, and NOT adopted yet

_Re-examined and re-dated 2026-09-02 — see [The merge queue: still NOT adopted](#the-merge-queue-still-not-adopted-re-dated-2026-09-02) below, which supplies the numbers this section was waiting for and a substitute (the candidate pointer) for what a queue would have bought._

PRs validate against the merge state at trigger time, so two individually-green PRs can merge into a red `main` — and a red `main` was the state that prompted this audit. A GitHub merge queue fixes that **by construction**, and it would also give the per-merge canary tier a native home (mutation full-seed, `gateway-package`, possibly the device gate, run once at queue time instead of on every push of every PR).

**It is not adopted in this change, and the reason is ordering rather than doubt.** A merge queue re-runs the required set against the queued merge commit, so its cost and its flake exposure are both a direct function of how long that set takes and how often it fails on the first attempt. Both of those numbers are what #892 just changed and neither has been measured on the new shape: `verify` was split, coverage was sharded four ways, the mutation full-seed moved off the PR loop, and `lane-health` (which reports first-attempt pass rate) has not yet run once. Enabling a queue before those numbers exist would be choosing a merge strategy from the arithmetic of a lane set that no longer exists.

**The decision this defers is bounded, not open.** Revisit once `lane-health` has three weeks of data. Adopt if the required set's p95 is under ~20 minutes and no required lane is below 95% first-attempt pass; if either is false, fix that first — a merge queue in front of a slow or flaky required set converts every red into a queue stall for everyone.

Meanwhile the gap is narrowed rather than left open: `mutation-canary` and `mobile-canary` both attribute a post-merge regression to one commit, and `lane-health`'s chronic-red rule bounds how long a red `main` can be treated as normal.

### `governance.yml` and branch protection

`governance.yml` is exempt from the only-`ci.yml`-listens-on-`pull_request` rule because it is kit-managed (`# governance-kit:managed`), which `scripts/lint-workflow-pins.mjs` skips by design — its policy lives upstream. The consequence is easy to miss and worth stating: **it is not part of the required `check` aggregate**, so unless the repository's ruleset lists `governance` as a required status check of its own, a red governance run blocks nothing.

That entry is repository configuration, not repository content, so no gate in this tree can assert it — the honest thing is to name it here rather than to pretend a linter covers it. The required set should be exactly two entries: `check` (which aggregates every `ci.yml` lane) and `governance`.

## Portable export custody (#630)

Ruled 2026-09-02. The portable vault bundle shipped `custody/seal-key.bin` — the vault's data-encryption key, in the clear, in the same unencrypted zip as the sealed ciphertext it opens — so every locker secret, connection token and passkey was one `unzip` away from anyone holding the file. The current decision, in four parts:

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **PX-ciphertext** | **A bundle never carries a key in the clear.** Sealed cells travel as ciphertext; the DEK leaves only inside `custody/recovery-kit.json`, password-wrapped, and only when the owner supplies a passphrase. Without one the manifest says `sealed: "ciphertext-only"` and the bundle opens no secrets anywhere. The header comment's claim that "no plaintext reaches the surface" is now literally true. |
| **PX-onewrap** | The wrap is the backup recovery kit's own scrypt(N=2¹⁷)→AES-256-GCM machinery, extracted to `@centraid/backup`'s `password-wrap.ts` and shared. **One** implementation, so there is one KDF cost, one AAD discipline, and one place the "no unwrapped acceptance path" rule of [#568](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/568) lives. |
| **PX-reseal** | Import **re-seals** every sealed cell under the TARGET vault's own DEK; it never installs the source's. The target's key file, its custody and its rotation history stay its own, and the same sweep serves `key rotate` and portable import. |
| **PX-refuse** | An artifact carrying sealed values with no key is **refused before the first row is written** — as is a kit with no passphrase, a sealed value in a column the re-seal sweep cannot reach, and any bundle still carrying a plaintext `custody/seal-key.bin`. The source vault's `core_vault.settings_json.seal_key` stamp is never copied: the stamp a restored vault ends up with names the key it actually sealed with, which is why it now reopens instead of failing `SealKeyError('missing')` after reporting success. |

## Ontology v0 close (#916)

Ruled 2026-09-02 by [#916](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/916), which re-reviewed the whole v0 ontology — every CHECK value against its writers, every foreign key's delete rule against its siblings, every table's timestamps, and twelve adversarial scenarios driven through the real gateway. Three seams that earlier waves had filed as settled turned out to be settled on nothing but a prior ruling, so the first ruling here is about reviewing. Engineering state, the census and the drift register are [vault-ontology.md](vault-ontology.md).

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **ONT-review** | **A citation is not a justification.** In a model review, a `#NNN`-ruled seam is re-judged on its merits for the product as it is now; the ruling explains why it was chosen, not whether it should stay. Mechanical sweeps and an adversarial run against the real gateway are part of a review, not extras — reading alone found none of the reproduced purge and merge bugs. Recorded as a repo convention in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md). |
| **ONT-onefile** | **One file.** `journal.db` is gone; its append-only **audit band** and the conversation **ledger band** live in `vault.db`. A receipt is written in the same transaction as the mutation it describes, the bands are append-only by TRIGGER, and each declares its own retention window (audit 365 days, ledger 90) with an archival pass that proves custody before it prunes. Export, device replica and support bundle exclude both bands BY BAND, not by table list. **Supersedes** the published page's "Physical layout — two files, split deferred": the split traded atomicity and referential integrity for a size problem retention already answers. The backup protocol follows: a manifest carries exactly one `db` entry and the WAL stream is addressed by generation with one tick marker per tick — the capture order, the coordinated break and the pair marker existed only to keep two files in step. |
| **ONT-entity** | **One supertype.** Every entity row is also a `core_entity(entity_type, entity_id)` row, and every `(type, id)` pointer is a composite foreign key with `ON DELETE CASCADE`. A row that is a _part_ of an entity — a split, a memory member, a phash — is declared a **projection**, keeps only its parent key, and mints no id in the entity namespace (11 of them; `locker.item_alias` is one by decision, because an alias word must not occupy the entity-id namespace). Deliberate non-keys are enumerated with a written reason each (13): the audit outlives its subject; grant containers are not canonical rows; sync staging and replica change are history; policy scope levels are not rows; demo seed markers may name ext-band tables. **Supersedes** [#272](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/272) (links end-dated on purge — they cascade now) and [#441](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/441)'s poly-ref registry and its sweep. |
| **ONT-dormant** | **A mechanism with no producer and no consumer leaves.** [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883)'s O-domains test, applied evenly: `health.*`, `finance.*`, the observation spine, the learn loop (`agent.correction` / `agent.judgment`), `consent.app_view`, `consent.export_job` and `schedule.availability_rule` are dropped. `social.thread` / `message` **stay** — they have writers. Intent belongs in an issue, never in dormant DDL. |
| **ONT-version** | **One version scheme.** The ontology version is a property of the FILE (`PRAGMA user_version`) and of the command CONTRACT (`agent_command.ontology_version`); `ONTOLOGY_VERSION` is `"1.0"`. No row carries a stamp — `core_party.ontology_version` is dropped. |
| **ONT-self** | **The vault's own party is the `self` party** (`core_vault.self_party_id`): the person as data. It confers nothing; authority stays gateway-side (owner + device + `share.authority`). The seam #726 left unnamed now has a name on the page and in the glossary. |
| **ONT-lifecycle** | **Lifecycle is declared per entity** — `append-only`, `mutable`, `trash` or `machinery` — and a test holds the schema to it. `created_at` / `updated_at` have ONE shape everywhere (`NOT NULL DEFAULT` + touch trigger), packs and machinery bands alike. The ledger band is the single exemption, with its reason (an engine epoch-ms clock) recorded in `lifecycle.test.ts`. |
| **ONT-star** | **Favorite is the flags-scheme tag, anchored on the entity the app shows.** `media_asset.favorite` is deleted; Photos' star anchors on `media.asset`, Docs' on `core.document`. **Supersedes** the single-writer mirror ruled in [#419](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/419) / [#441](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/441): a mirror is two truths, and a postcondition asserting they agree is a test for a problem that need not exist. |
| **ONT-purge** | **A party is trashed and purged like every other kind.** The purge is one DELETE the supertype cascades; the engine refuses it while money or authority still names the person (RESTRICT), and attribution yields (`SET NULL`). Standing authorities about a purged subject are revoked by trigger in the same statement, retained as history with `revoked_reason`. The foreign-key walk that deleted whatever it could reach — other people's expense splits included — is gone. |
| **ONT-revisions** | **One revision mechanism with per-entity retention.** `core_entity_revision` is the only history table; `locker_item_history` is dropped and the locker pack declares `retain: "forever"`. A revision records the invocation that caused it (`invocation_id`, `ON DELETE SET NULL` — the archive pass retires invocations and a revision outlives the record of it). |
| **ONT-rows** | **Structure that identity or the purge cascade must see is ROWS, not JSON.** A recurring template's division is `tally_recurring_expense_split` (**supersedes** O-recur's "`splits_json` is the one deliberate JSON seam"); an overriding occurrence's attendees are `schedule_recurrence_exception_attendee`. Party ids inside a blob are invisible to merge and unreachable by the cascade. |
| **ONT-access** | **The `consent` plane is renamed `access`.** The plane answers "may this actor reach this data"; consent is one of the answers it records. Every scope, tombstone and policy names ONE dotted entity (`media.asset`) instead of four encodings of "which entity". The rename is physical and logical: `consent_*` tables to `access_*`, `consent.*` scope strings to `access.*`, `gateway/consent.ts` to `gateway/access.ts`, `evaluateConsent` to `evaluateAccess`, `GatewayError("consent", …)` to `GatewayError("access", …)` and the wire code it derives, `VAULT_CONSENT` to `VAULT_ACCESS`. Deliberately **kept**: `providerEgressConsent*`, `enrich.record_consent`, the `consent.required` wire code and the client's own `ConsentDecision` — those name the member's act, not the plane that decides. |
| **ONT-currency** | **Money states its currency**: on `tally_group` (the ledger everyone in it reads), on `tally_expense`, and on `tally_settlement`, with triggers holding a grouped row to its group. Splits, payers and line items inherit by construction — they are shares of a stated amount. |
| **ONT-recur** | **Recurrence is honest about time.** An unsupported RRULE part is **refused** at the write and import boundary, never silently dropped (`packages/core/src/time/rrule-support.ts`). Exceptions key on the series' wall-clock start plus its `recurrence_semantics`, so moving a series' zone no longer orphans them. |
| **ONT-sql** | **One door means one door.** Raw SQL on vault tables from outside `packages/vault` fails `bun run lint:vault-sql`, whose allow-list carries a reason per entry and fails on stale entries. The three life-data readers that had grown outside the gate (daily brief, due reminders, semantic search) are behind it — the first two through `gateway.read`, semantic search through the vault's `rankLivePhotoEmbeddings` — and none of them was allow-listed. |
| **ONT-ladder** | **A baseline, then rungs.** Rung one is the #916 baseline, stated rather than reconstructed because v0 had no files in the field; it is history now and does not grow. [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929) was the first change that had to reach an existing file, so it added **rungs two through four** — the subscription seat's tables with the purge trigger re-cut without the rail's grant table, then `share_delivery_config` re-cut with the rail's `departure_policy`, then the drop of `core_share_origin`. [#972](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/972) added **rung five**: `share_authority_request` and `share_authority_use` had been composed into rung 1 after the #929 golden froze at `user_version` 2, and a climbed rung never runs again. A fresh vault stamps `user_version = 5`. A rung already climbed never runs again, so a shape change made after a release is its OWN rung or it reaches nothing. The commons rail's own tables are dropped by a DATA pass on open, not by a rung: its rows become standing answers first. The golden corpus is re-frozen as `packages/vault/tests/golden/issue-929`. |

Portable-export custody was ruled in the same wave and is recorded separately as [PX-ciphertext / PX-onewrap / PX-reseal / PX-refuse](#portable-export-custody-630).

## The Quality Ladder (#915)

Ruled 2026-09-02 by [#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915), which audited the QA architecture against 30 days of run history and found the doctrine sound and the machinery outgrown by it: **0 of 30** scheduled nightlies green since 2026-08-03, a PR gate observed at ~26 minutes against a documented 12.3, and thirteen auto-filed nightly tracking issues closed as noise. Two variables govern every ruling below — confidence a regression cannot reach a user, and minutes between "done" and "merged".

Six rungs, one question each: 0 commit, 1 push, 2 merge (`ci.yml`), 3 candidate (`candidate.yml`), 4 nightly (`e2e.yml`), 5 weekly. Lane identity is the GitHub **job id**.

### The ladder's rungs 2-5

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **G-candidate** | **Post-merge produces a candidate; nightly, weekly and release consume it.** `candidate.yml` runs on every push to `main` and, on green, moves the git ref `refs/candidates/latest`, writes `artifacts/candidate.json` and publishes `test-report/candidate.json` + a 200-deep `test-report/candidates.json` history to gh-pages. On red the pointer **stays**, which is the whole value: the deep rungs keep testing the last build somebody would have handed to a device instead of following `main` into whatever broke. Every consumer opens with a `resolve-candidate` job whose fallback chain — dispatch input → the pointer → the last green `ci.yml` run on main → the run's own SHA — is printed to the step summary, so the page always says which link answered rather than quietly restoring the old behaviour. |
| **G-merge-diet** | **A check belongs on a rung only if it sharpens that rung's answer.** `client-e2e` (web + desktop Playwright), `gateway-package`, `desktop-e2e-macos` and the Android **resilience** leg all left rung 2. None was deleted or loosened: the first three run on every promotion as `web-e2e-linux`, `desktop-e2e-linux`, `lane-gateway-package` and `desktop-e2e-macos`, where they now **gate promotion** — strictly more than the advisory status `desktop-e2e-macos` had on the PR loop. What stays on the merge gate for the phone is the smallest set that can falsify "this diff broke it": pairing canary, one home-app journey, cold start, 8 minutes warm. |
| **G-cold-cache-is-a-lane-failure** | **A pipeline fact is reported as a pipeline fact, in seconds.** `mobile-device-gate` used to absorb a cold apk restore by compiling a release build inside the job — 16m21s of a 27m job on 0655a1b7 and again on 60732d30 — and every PR behind it paid the same. The apk key folds the native **and** JS fingerprints, and `candidate.yml`'s `mobile-canary-android` banks that exact key on every push to `main`, so a miss means that lane has not run for this fingerprint or did not bank. The gate now fails immediately with `::error title=mobile-device-gate cold cache::` and an evidence case id `cold-cache`, and its outer timeout falls 60 → 20 minutes. **Superseded by G-reconstruct-on-miss below**: the apk key names the CONTENT of `apps/mobile/src`, the four workspace packages and `bun.lock`, and the only lane that banks it runs post-merge — so a PR touching mobile JS produces a key no pre-merge run could have banked, and fail-on-miss was unpassable by construction for exactly the PRs it existed to check. |
| **G-reconstruct-on-miss** | **A cold apk is rebuilt, and the rebuild reuses everything a compile already produced.** `mobile-device-gate` no longer fails on an apk miss; it compiles the shell and banks it under the key the restore missed on, so the next push on the branch is warm. What made that affordable is a caching restructure across `ci.yml`, `candidate.yml` and `e2e.yml`, moved together under one `android-gradle-v3-*` key namespace because a lane that saves a shape another lane does not restore is worse than no cache. Three things were wrong at once and each alone still left a full native compile: `org.gradle.caching` was unset, so `~/.gradle/caches` held dependencies and transforms but no task **outputs**; the autolinked React Native / Expo libraries build under `node_modules/<lib>/android/build` with CMake intermediates in `node_modules/<lib>/android/.cxx` (and the app's own in `app/.cxx`), and none of it was cached — which is why a warm cache still recompiled `react-native-quick-crypto`'s C sources for twenty-five minutes on run 33405430819; and `apps/mobile/android/.gradle` was not cached either, so gradle's stale-output cleanup **deleted** the two build directories that were, because its execution history had no record vouching for them. The `restore-keys` fallback stays on this cache and stays refused on the apk cache: a partial match restores directories whose task inputs gradle re-checks, not a binary. The one task whose declared inputs do not cover everything that changes it is the JS bundle — React Native scopes `BundleHermesCTask.sources` to `apps/mobile` while the bundle also contains the workspace packages — so `android-emulator-install.sh` forces it with `--rerun` rather than trusting it, and no restored directory can smuggle another commit's Hermes bundle into a freshly packaged apk. The gradle/native save is gated on `gradle_ran`, not `built`: an apk-cache hit used to re-bank a barren directory, and because `restore-keys` resolve to the most recently created match, that entry then shadowed the warm one — which is why the cache never self-corrected. |
| **G-burn-in** | **A new test runs three times, alone, before it is trusted.** `author ≠ auditor` was enforced for the product and not at all for the tests: a newly written test ran exactly once before merging, inside a full suite sharing a worker pool with a thousand siblings. `new-test-burn-in` (in `check.needs`) runs every added or modified Vitest `*.test.*` / `*.spec.*` file three times in isolation from its own package. **Any disagreement across the three is red**, not just a failure — the file that passes twice and fails once is the expensive one and the one a single run cannot see. Playwright and Maestro specs are skipped with a printed reason rather than dropped. |
| **G-mutation-cap** | **Per-PR mutation keeps its audit and loses its tail.** The affected-seed set is a function of the diff, so one PR paid 90 seconds and the next nineteen minutes — a rung-2 lane answering a rung-3 question. `scripts/ci/mutation-cap.mjs` caps the run at 8 minutes (`MUTATION_PR_CAP_MS`), and over the cap it kills the run, writes a `deferred` evidence case, leaves **one** PR comment (rewritten in place through a hidden marker) and exits 0. Nothing is skipped: `candidate.yml`'s `mutation-full` runs every seed with floors on the merge commit. What moves is when the answer arrives, not whether. A deferral is never recorded as a pass of the seeds. |
| **G-suite-rung3** | **The serial suite is a promotion lane, not a merge lane.** The first time the rung-2 wall-clock gate actually ran it read **24.2 min against 15**, and `verify`'s `bun run test:suite` was ~19 of them — a serial, uninstrumented re-run of the same projects the four `coverage-shard` legs already run in ~7 min each. On the merge gate the shards are the pass/fail answer (a failing test fails its shard) as well as the floors, so the serial run left `verify` and became `candidate.yml`'s `suite` lane, on every push to `main`, gating `promote`. It took two things with it that only a single unsharded runner can produce honestly: the suite wall-clock ceiling — [#905](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/905) measured the per-file span sum at 3,824 s sharded against 2,370 s serial for the same files, so scoring it on the shards would fail the runner, not the suite — and a second copy of the collection tripwire (the merge gate keeps its own on the merged report). What changes is _when_ a test flood bites: at promotion, minutes after the merge and attributed to one commit, rather than before it. What does not change is the number: `tests/budgets.json#suiteWallClock` is untouched and still tightens only. |
| **G-security-rung3** | **CodeQL and the Rust supply chain gate a promotion, not a week.** Both moved from `security.yml`'s Sunday cron into `candidate.yml`; `security.yml` is deleted because nothing remained in it. A weekly SAST run attributes a finding to a week's worth of commits and lets six days of promotions happen in front of it; on rung 3 the finding names one commit and blocks the pointer. |
| **G-parks** | **A park is a deadline, never a mute.** [`tests/quarantine.json`](../tests/quarantine.json)'s `lanes` entries carry `issue`, `expires` and `why`. A parked lane still runs and still writes evidence; the writer records `verdict: parked` instead of `failed`, the nightly verdict is computed over unparked lanes only, and the day the expiry passes the lane counts as red again. More than three parked lanes, or any park longer than 30 days, is a report-level `HOLD`. Seeded with `mobile-e2e-android` and `mobile-e2e-ios` (#870, expiring 2026-09-16) and `web-e2e-cross-browser` (#915, whose first green run on a candidate is its baseline rather than a regression). |
| **G-rolling-issues** | **One issue per lane, rewritten in place; never a comment, never a re-creation.** The nightly filed a single `[nightly] e2e lane red — tracking` issue and commented on it every morning; thirteen were closed as noise, and its `### Job results` block was a hand-maintained list of ten lanes that covered neither `fuzz-parsers` nor five others. `file-tracking-issue.mjs --update` matches by **exact title** (the fuzzy `in:title` search also matches `…-ios-smoke`, and editing the wrong lane's body destroys it) and rewrites the body, so `[nightly] lane red — <lane>` always states that lane's current condition and closing it means the lane is green. The red-lane list is `toJSON(needs)` through jq — a lane added to `needs:` is covered without a second list. Rung 3 files the same shape as `[candidate] lane red — <lane>`. |
| **G-lane-rules** | **Promotion and demotion are counted, not remembered.** `scripts/ci/lane-rules.mjs` applies the issue's table mechanically: a rung-2 lane below 99 % first-attempt pass files `[lanes] demote <lane>`; two escapes in the window file `[lanes] promote <lane>`; three consecutive reds on candidates demand a park; an expired park and a p95 over the rung budget (15 / 45 / 90 / 300 minutes) are red, the latter naming the minutes to cut. The escape count is a **stated over-approximation** — nothing in the Actions API carries case ids across workflows, so it counts a deep-rung red on a SHA whose rung-2 gate was green. It over-counts and never under-counts, which is the right direction for a signal whose action is "consider promoting"; do not tighten the rule without replacing the approximation with case ids from the evidence files. |
| **G-pr-gate-budget** | **The rung-2 budget is measured on the run that spends it.** `tests/budgets.json#suiteWallClock` gains a `pr-gate` lane at 900,000 ms, scored by `scripts/ci/pr-gate-wall-clock.mjs` inside `check` (which therefore holds `actions: read`) as `max(completed_at) − min(started_at)` across `check`'s `needs:`. The **span**, not the sum: summing the lanes would punish the parallelism that makes the gate fast. Both lanes of that file are ratcheted by `ratchet-floors.mjs`, so widening either needs a changed `approvedDeviation`. |
| **G-turbo-inputs** | **A build hashes only what a build reads.** With no `inputs:`, turbo hashed every non-ignored file in a package, so one line in `packages/core/src/blob/cbsf-properties.test.ts` moved 11 of 16 build hashes — including `@centraid/tunnel#build`, a release cargo compile measured at 278 s of a 349 s cold `build:ci`, in each of the five lanes that build. `turbo.json` now declares `$TURBO_DEFAULT$` minus test/spec modules and markdown; re-measured, a test edit and a README edit move **0** hashes and a real `packages/core/src` edit still moves the same 11. `coverage-shard` and `coverage` also gained the `cargo-cache: verify` preset `verify` already had, so an identical miss degrades to an incremental compile rather than a cold one. `bun run build:ci:floored` enforces `--min-hit-rate 0.15`: the deepest legitimate single-package change still leaves ≈ 23 % cached, so only a whole-graph miss falls below. |
| **G-turbo-floor-waiver** | **The floor's one exception is computed, not offered.** A change to a global-hash input (`bun.lock`, the **root** `package.json`, `turbo.json`, `.npmrc`, `bunfig.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `.node-version`, `rust-toolchain*`, `.github/actions/setup/**`) misses every task by design, so enforcing the floor there would red every dependency bump — a gate that is always red on the PRs that need it least is a gate that is off (principle 2), and it would blow the scorecard's ≤ 2 % PR false-red target on its own. [`scripts/ci/turbo-floor.mjs`](../scripts/ci/turbo-floor.mjs) therefore reads the diff first and downgrades the failure to a `::warning title=Turbo cache floor waived::` that **names the file that moved the hash**, writing the reason to the Job Summary. A package-local `package.json` is deliberately not a mover — that is the case the floor exists to catch. **There is no flag and no environment variable that turns the floor off**: the waiver is derived from the diff, which is what makes it an exception rather than a hole. An unreadable diff waives loudly for the same reason it is not a red — reding a lane for its checkout depth is a false red the author cannot fix. Widen `GLOBAL_HASH_INPUTS` only against a run that proved the path moves the global hash; the `### Turbo cache` summary prints `globalCacheInputs` for exactly that. |
| **G-release-candidate** | **We ship builds somebody promoted.** Release SHA selection was implicitly "whatever `HEAD` is when `publish.mjs` runs", and every release lane **builds** the tree rather than testing it, so a tag on an unexercised commit would have been green. `release:prepare` now refuses a HEAD that is not `refs/candidates/latest`, `release:classify --require-candidate [--sha]` offers the same check to a caller that only classifies, and `release.yml`'s `require-candidate` job is the backstop — accepting the current pointer **or** an entry in the gh-pages promotion history, because cutting a release takes minutes while `main` keeps moving. `--allow-uncandidated` passes and prints the reason it was used. |

### The mobile roster and the iOS shell (Wave 2)

Ruled 2026-09-02 by [#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915) Wave 2. These extend **G-merge-diet** and **G-device-only-gate** rather than replacing either: what moves is where a mobile claim is declared and which rung answers it.

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **G-one-roster** | **One roster, one runner.** [`tests/agent-e2e-mobile/roster.json`](../tests/agent-e2e-mobile/roster.json) is the single source for what the phone must do: `suites` carry the ordered member list, the aggregate `budgetMs`, the rungs and the platform; `flows` carry the claim, the status and a per-journey marginal budget; `lanes` carry a `rung`. The seven `run-*-suite.mjs` files that each held a `const FLOWS` and a `const BUDGET_MS` literal are one `run-roster.mjs --rung <n> --platform <p> [--suite <s>]`, and `tests/agent-e2e-mobile/lib/roster.mjs` is its only reader — `lint-e2e-wiring`, `lint-mobile-testids`, `lint-e2e-claims`, `check-mobile-suite-budgets` and the report all go through it. **The flags are the wiring**, deliberately: `lint-e2e-wiring` derives what a lane schedules by reading the invocation the shipped YAML or shell script contains, so a runner that picked its suite from an environment variable would make every lane look identical to the gate whose `promoting` and `exploratory` rules depend on telling them apart. The six one-line shims that bridged the swap were deleted with the last workflow line that spelled their paths, so `run-roster.mjs` is the only runner on disk. |
| **G-roster-ratchet** | **A ceiling cannot be laundered by moving it or by renaming it.** Suite budgets stay TIGHTEN-ONLY across the move out of the runner files: `check-mobile-suite-budgets.mjs` reads the merge base's `roster.json` first, then the suite named by `supersedes`, then the retired `run-<id>-suite.mjs` literal, and only an exhausted ref list means "new suite, no base". The p95-slack rule (1.5x the observed p95 once three samples exist) is unchanged and still a no-op, because `ledger/durations.json` holds zero records. A flow's own `budgetMs` is a second, different ceiling — its **marginal** cost with no fresh pairing in it — which is why a suite's members may sum past the suite's own number; `validateRoster` refuses only a member that cannot fit its suite at all. |
| **G-rung-tags** | **Rung 2 keeps the smallest set that can falsify "this diff broke the phone".** `pr-gate` (pairing canary, `notes-library`, cold start; Android; 8 min warm) is the only mobile suite on the merge gate, and **zero iOS minutes** are spent there — a #915 non-goal in as many words. Rung 3 is `resilience` (the retired second leg's three members) plus `home-apps` on Android and `ios-smoke` on iOS. Rung 4 is `probes-suite`, `photos`, `home-apps` again, `sharing` and `promoting-suite` on Android and `ios-depth` on iOS. `sharing` exists because `sharing-reach` ran as a **bare** `node …/flows/sharing-reach.mjs` line with no aggregate ceiling — the one member of the roster nothing priced, which is the shape #890 W0 wrapped six other journeys to end. |
| **G-ios-shell** | **Cache the iOS shell by its NATIVE fingerprint and make the JS current.** The `.app` cache key drops its `js` component (`ios-shell-<os>-xc<toolchain>-fp<native>`), and [`apps/mobile/scripts/ios-simulator-install.sh`](../apps/mobile/scripts/ios-simulator-install.sh) re-exports the SHA's bundle into the banked `.app` instead of rebuilding it — the "pay packaging, not compilation" path Android has used since #905, worth ~32 minutes of a 51-minute job on a JS-only commit. The injection runs **both** commands the Xcode build phase runs, because `expo export:embed` emits plain JavaScript by design and Hermes will happily run it: skipping `hermesc -emit-binary` produces a green lane measuring an engine path no member has. `hermesc` is banked beside the `.app` under the same key (Pods are deliberately uncached, so it is otherwise absent on the warm path), the injected bundle is asserted to carry the Hermes magic `0xC61FBC03`, and the branch itself lives in `ios-shell-cache.mjs` with a unit suite — a wrong rebuild costs minutes, a wrong reuse reports green over another commit's JavaScript. See [docs/traps/ios-shell-injection.md](traps/ios-shell-injection.md). |
| **G-candidate-ios** | **Every candidate carries an iOS verdict, on every push to `main`.** The issue's open decision 3 asked whether the macOS minutes are worth it; they are, because the iOS `.app` is a SEPARATE ARTIFACT — a different binary, a different toolchain, a different Maestro driver — so "it paired and reached a usable Home on Android" is not evidence about it, and until this lane the first iOS answer of the day came from the nightly against a tree that had already absorbed a day of merges. `candidate.yml`'s `mobile-ios-smoke` runs the three-member `ios-smoke` suite in ten minutes WARM and gates `promote`. What makes the price payable is **G-ios-shell**: the shell is restored by native fingerprint and this SHA's bundle is injected into it, so a JS-only candidate pays packaging, not a thirty-two-minute compile. The lane is `blocking: false` in the roster because rung 3 is not a merge gate — it blocks the promotion, not the merge. |
| **G-state-variety** | **State variety never goes to a device.** The eight apps' seven canonical designed states are `tests/integration-mobile/`'s: it boots the shipped gateway and a real native replica session and arranges each state as a boot condition, on Linux, in about two minutes, covering 52 of the 56 cells. A simulator minute costs roughly 600 Vitest seconds, so the same claim on a device is the same claim at 600x. `lint-e2e-wiring`'s RULE `state-variety` refuses any `appStates` cell whose owner is under `tests/agent-e2e-mobile/`. The four uncovered cells (`docs.parked`, `notes.parked`, `photos.parked`, `tasks.parked`) are a **product** gap, not a test gap — no action those apps ship routes to a `confirm: true` command — and are recorded as deliberate n/a with that reason rather than closed by faking a park. |
| **G-launcher-await** | **A journey that opens an app waits for the launcher, not for the band.** `HOME_READY_MARKER` is the Home band's label and the band renders over `DayOne` too, so a flow that waits only for it walks into an empty-vault Home and fails on its own tile selector — which is #870: twelve journeys reporting `Element not found: Open <App>` while the app was correct and the corpus had simply arrived after the phone cloned. `AWAIT_LAUNCHER` waits for `home-grid`, which `LauncherGrid` alone publishes. `lint-e2e-flows`'s RULE `launcher-await` requires it in every chunk that reaches a launcher tile, with an `# e2e-lint-allow: launcher-await` escape hatch for the flows that deliberately face an empty vault. |
| **G-flow-crash** | **A broken journey is a third failure class.** `lib/failure-class.mjs` had two: `infrastructure` (retried once) and `product` (never retried, the default). A flow whose own script throws — `flows/share-intent-in.mjs` used `shQuote` without importing it and died with a `ReferenceError` after six assertions had passed — was neither, and defaulted to `product`, so the nightly said the app was broken when the journey was. `harness` is matched on the error type plus a stack frame inside `tests/agent-e2e-mobile/` (excluding `*.test.mjs`), is never retried because `retry-policy.mjs` retries `infrastructure` alone, and states that no claim was exercised. |
| **G-alarm-weekly** | **The E2E alarm runs weekly, from quarterly.** #915's open decision 4 reads "monthly now, weekly once the roster is single-sourced"; Wave 2 **is** that single-sourcing, so the cadence goes straight to weekly — Sunday 03:00 UTC, before the 04:00 slot, so the answer to "can this suite go red at all" arrives before the weekly lanes report. `mobile-alarm-test.yml` still runs the rung-2 suite against a build with Home blanked and still **fails on a green suite**; nothing about its inversion, its pins, its timeout or its deliberate absence of an apk cache changed. |

### Superseded by the rulings above

- **G-mutation-tiers** (#892) — the tier split stands; the names moved. `mutation-canary` is now `candidate.yml`'s `mutation-full`, and the PR tier gained the 8-minute cap and the rung-3 deferral of **G-mutation-cap**.
- **G-device-only-gate**'s two-leg matrix (#905, 2026-09-02) — superseded the same day by **G-merge-diet**. Its finding is unchanged and is why the split existed: the five priced ~795 s against a 720 s deadline, so the fifth member starved. #915 makes the smaller cut instead of buying a second emulator — `native-v0-resilience` is a question about a build, so it moves to rung 3 with the rest of the Android depth, and the gate keeps pairing canary, `notes-library` and cold start at 8 minutes warm. The rest of **G-device-only-gate** (the covers-open claim held at the rendered tier; the ledger uploads) stands unchanged.
- **G-lane-health** (#892) — extended, not replaced. First-attempt pass rate and the chronic-red rule stand; **G-lane-rules** adds the demote/promote/park/budget table and the `HOLD` verdict, and rung 3 gets its own invocation.

### The merge queue: still NOT adopted, re-dated 2026-09-02

Re-examined by [#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915) and **still deferred**, for a reason that is now stronger rather than weaker. #892 deferred it pending `lane-health`'s first numbers; those numbers arrived and say the required set was ~26 minutes with a nightly that had not been green in a month, which is precisely the state in which a merge queue converts every red into a queue stall for everyone.

What #915 changes is that the deferral now has a **substitute** rather than only a condition. A merge queue's value was that two individually-green PRs cannot merge into a red `main`; `candidate.yml` answers the same question one step later and without the stall — the pointer simply does not move, and the deep rungs keep testing the last good build. That is weaker than a queue (a red `main` still exists) and much cheaper (nobody waits).

**Revisit condition, unchanged in substance and re-dated:** adopt when the rung-2 p95 is under ~15 minutes over 20 runs (the `pr-gate` budget now measures exactly this) and no rung-2 lane is below 99 % first-attempt pass (the demote rule now files an issue when one is), **and** PR volume exceeds a handful a day. Until all three hold, a queue buys ordering nobody is waiting for.

### Gate and ledger diet

Ruled 2026-09-02 by [#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915) Wave 4. Every gate in `check:push` now carries a class and a reason in [`scripts/ci/gate-classes.json`](../scripts/ci/gate-classes.json), enforced by `scripts/ci/gate-classes.test.mjs`.

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **G-gate-class** | Every gate is **product** (it falsifies a user-visible claim), **contract** (it falsifies a repo-internal wiring or shape claim), or **hygiene** (it is a tighten-only ratchet over the _test suite's own_ quality). The class decides the rung: product and contract at rungs 1–2, hygiene at rung 5. A gate with no class is a gate nobody has decided about, so the register is a test, not a document. |
| **G-product-bundle** | The 38 contract/product gates that each run in under a second are one gate name, `lint:product` ([`scripts/lint-product.mjs`](../scripts/lint-product.mjs)), not 38. They are not 38 decisions a developer makes. The bundle spawns each member exactly as `run-gates.mjs` did and keeps per-gate buffered failure output, so attribution is unchanged; it deliberately does **not** host them in-process, because doing that means monkey-patching `process.exit` per gate and a bundle that can swallow a failure is worse than the ~3 s it saves. |
| **G-weekly-hygiene** | The seven hygiene ratchets — comment density, assertion matchers, fixed sleeps, skips, environment-red sites, the type floor, the schema/export fingerprint — run weekly in [`hygiene.yml`](../.github/workflows/hygiene.yml) against `main`, with one rolling issue on red. This is a **third tier**, not the "enforced by the pre-push hook is enforcement in name only" regression [#782](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/782) ruled against: that ruling's objection was that nothing but a developer's hook ran the gate, and here CI runs it, on a schedule, and files an issue. `test:comment-density` had no CI job at all before this, so it gained enforcement. What makes the move safe is that all seven are **standing** checks over the whole tree — a count, an inventory, a fingerprint — never diff-scoped, so a weekly run on `main` sees exactly what a per-push run would have seen. The price is detection latency, push → within 7 days, and nothing else. |
| **G-weekly-tradeoff** | Two of the seven are named as hygiene by #915 but are not purely meta-quality, and the tradeoff is recorded rather than argued away. `lint:type-floor` is the one gate in the class whose red **is** user-visible — an 11px label ships — and DESIGN.md owns the floor as its own invariant; it moves because #915 lists it, and because it is a pure standing source scan that a weekly run catches in full. `lint:schema-export` couples the vault schema to the portable export; its enforcing comparison is a standing fingerprint-vs-source check, so weekly detects the same drift, and only the diff-scoped "touch the export owner in the same PR" hint is lost. If either ever catches something in the weekly lane that a push would have caught a week earlier, promote it back to rung 1 — the classification is a measurement, not a doctrine. |
| **G-push-17** | `check:push` names **17** gates. It had drifted to 59 while [dev-environment.md](dev-environment.md#the-local-gate-loop) still claimed 25. Nothing was dropped to get there: 38 bundled, 7 moved weekly, and `check:mobile-native-state` (30.5 s) fell to rung 2, where ci.yml's `mobile-smoke` job already ran it on exactly the diffs that matter. The wall clock is still bounded by `test:affected`. |
| **G-rung0-deferral** | `receipt-per-issue` runs at pre-push, not pre-commit. It is repo-wide by construction and digest-locked, so its `hook:` field cannot be moved in-tree and the kit exposes no `hook:` overlay — the deferral therefore lives in `.githooks/*`, driven by `.governance/conf/srikanth235/centraid/pre-commit-deferred.conf`. **Scope is unchanged:** `.governance/run.sh`, which CI invokes, runs every directive regardless of `hook:`, so this moves _when_ a directive runs locally and never _whether_ it runs. A directive belongs on that list only if it is repo-wide by construction and a material share of the budget. **The budget half of that test no longer holds.** The ruling's original arithmetic was `repo-hygiene` + `receipt-per-issue` at 86.3 s against a 5 s rung-0 budget; governance-kit audit 0.11.0 retired `repo-hygiene` and rewrote `receipt-per-issue`, and the whole 12-directive suite now runs in ~4 s in one process. Whether to keep any deferral is open — it survives here as the unchanged mechanism, not as a re-argued ruling. **2026-09-09 — SUPERSEDED by [#1005](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1005):** the deferral is removed and the conf file is deleted. Two things settled it. The four vendored directives are now ESLint rules under `.governance/law/`, so `hook:` is no longer out of reach and the repo-wide half runs from one generated record in under a second. And the mechanism had already stopped working: under governance-kit 0.15.0 nothing in `.githooks/` reads `pre-commit-deferred.conf` — the dispatchers select on the `hook:` field alone — so `receipt-per-issue` was running at pre-commit while this row and CONSTITUTION.md both said it ran at pre-push. |
| **G-file-ceiling** | **The 625-line file ceiling is an oxlint rule, and its exemptions are a ledger row.** governance-kit audit 0.11.0 retired `repo-hygiene`, which had owned the ceiling; `max-lines` in [oxlint.config.ts](../oxlint.config.ts) replaces it at the same raw-line count for ~0.4s against the directive's 51.2s. The 131 files predating the rule are exempt by name in `tests/inventory.json#fileSize` rather than by inline `oxlint-disable` comments, because a suppression is free to add and invisible in review while a row must survive the section's down-only `_budget`. The other four `repo-hygiene` sub-checks — the 5 MB tracked-file ceiling, merge markers, committed build artefacts, debug statements — were deliberately **not** replaced. |
| **G-governance-timeout** | `governance.yml` gets no `timeout-minutes` from this repo. It is digest-managed (`.governance/install.yaml`), the kit exposes no timeout setting for the generated workflow, and no `governance` CLI is vendored here to regenerate it — a hand edit would fail `managed-tree-integrity`, which is the directive that exists to catch exactly that. Its bare `pull_request:` listener and its missing timeout are legal by the same mechanism: `lint-workflow-pins` skips `# governance-kit:managed` files whole. The supported path is a kit update; until one ships this is a named gap, not a hand-patched one. |
| **G-retirements** | `scripts/ci/retry.mjs` and `tests/helpers/factories.ts#buildTestGateway` are retired — both had zero callers, and TESTING.md had already documented the latter as a retirement candidate. #915's third named retirement, a "stale TESTING.md reference to `golden-vault.test.ts`", was **not** performed: the premise is wrong. Both paths TESTING.md names resolve (`packages/vault/src/golden-vault.test.ts` and `packages/vault/tests/golden/v0-baseline/`), and the same pair is cited correctly at **G-golden** above. |
| **G-four-ledgers** | **Twenty tighten-only ledgers under `tests/` are four, behind one validator.** [`tests/floors.json`](../tests/floors.json) (up-only), [`tests/budgets.json`](../tests/budgets.json) (down-only), [`tests/inventory.json`](../tests/inventory.json) (down-only with an issue and an expiry) and [`tests/quarantine.json`](../tests/quarantine.json) (tests and lane parks, down-only with an expiry), validated by `bun run lint:ledgers` ([`scripts/check-ledgers.mjs`](../scripts/check-ledgers.mjs)) — a rung-1 **contract** gate inside the `lint:product` bundle. The five discovery scanners keep their own detection and their own `--write`; the validator owns the shared shape. Twelve files were deleted, none of their rules were. |
| **G-waiver-scope** | **Merging the files did not merge the waivers.** Each section carries its own `approvedDeviation`, and a widen is waived only by a note that CHANGED in the section being widened. Seven files previously scoped seven waivers; one file-level note would have let a reviewed widen of the desktop cold-start ceiling silently waive a coverage-floor drop riding the same PR — the exact failure [#781](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/781) found when it ruled that mere presence never waives. |
| **G-rename-base** | **A ledger rename may not widen anything, including the rename's own commit.** Every comparison reads the merged file at the merge base first and falls back to the section's pre-merge path. Without the fallback each ratchet would go silent for exactly one commit, which is the commit best placed to lower a floor unwatched. `scripts/check-ledgers.test.mjs` proves it by committing the pre-merge tree and lowering a floor in the merged one. |
| **G-mirrors** | **A number lives once; a second copy is a derived mirror, asserted equal.** `floors.minimumTests` mirrors `tests/claims.json#flows[].minimumTests` and `budgets.mobileSuites` mirrors the mobile roster's `suites[].budgetMs`; `lint:ledgers` fails on any difference and `--write` refreshes them. The issue asked for `minimumTests` to MOVE into the floors file; it did not, because the up-only rule for a flow rename reads that flow's surface, dimension and tier, which are claims data — moving the number alone would have split one record across two files, and moving the record would have duplicated the other three fields. The mirror gives the ledger the numbers without giving the repo two sources of truth. |
| **G-experience-reference** | ~~**`budgets.experience` names the per-surface files rather than absorbing them.**~~ SUPERSEDED by [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927): the per-surface files and the query-count file are absorbed into [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json), keyed `surface / journey / volume / hardware`. The consumer count was the argument for leaving them, and it was the wrong one — the keys said which surface a ceiling belonged to but not the volume it held at, so several ceilings measured on an empty vault read as year-3 budgets. `inventory.naCells` stays a reference to `tests/claims.json#naCells`; that reasoning is untouched. |
| **G-inventory-deadlines** | **Every inventory exception carries an issue and an expiry; every inventory population carries one for the whole section.** A named exception (a skip, an env-red guard, an advisory step, a park) is attributable, so it takes `issue` plus `expires` — or, for env-red, the `revisitTrigger` event that retires it, which is a sharper deadline than a guessed date. A population budget (sleeps, assertion hygiene, comment density) counts a measured population rather than listing waivers, so the deadline sits on the section. The 25 skip sites had an issue and no date; they now carry #915 with a 2026-12-01 expiry, as do the three population sections. This is a tightening — nothing that was enforced stopped being enforced, and three registers that could stand forever now cannot. |
| **G-section-watch** | **A watch on a merged ledger names a SECTION, not the file.** `MUTATION_GLOBAL_WATCH` watched `tests/mutation-floors.json`, whose successor also holds the coverage floors; watching the path would have run all 24 Stryker seeds on every coverage-floor edit. `scripts/mutation/run.mjs` expands `tests/floors.json` into `tests/floors.json#<section>` tokens for the sections that actually differ from the merge base, so the list stays as narrow as [TESTING.md](../TESTING.md) says it is. |
| **G-not-ratchets** | **A ledger that is not a ratchet stays its own file.** `design-grammar-matrix` (a structural design contract), `mobile-resource-evidence` (observations with per-row tolerances), `diff-coverage-deviation` (a waiver slot whose whole point is the ABSENCE of a key), `path-filter-ledger` (the CI filter inverse register), `schema-export-fingerprint` (a sha256 coupling gate, neither up- nor down-only) and `tests/quality/classification-ratchet.json` are not budgets, and filing them under one would say something false about each. `design-token-css-budget` DID merge, as `budgets.designTokenCss`, and is still deliberately empty — [docs/traps/design-tokens.md](traps/design-tokens.md) forbids repopulating it either way. |

## Snappier blueprint apps (#922)

Ruled 2026-09-03 by [#922](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/922), the first-principles review of what one user action on any of the eight blueprint apps costs through the engines all eight share. Two pairs of rulings had grown to contradict each other and four seams were held only by citation; those are settled here, in wave 1, ahead of the code waves that land them, so no later wave inherits a deference nobody re-judged. **A ruling in force whose code has not moved yet names the wave that lands it** — until that wave merges, the code still reads the old way and the doc says so.

**Four of these rows are superseded by [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996), which deleted the store they rule.** **SB-text**'s per-entity text ceilings and lazy columns, **SB-replica-sync**'s per-seat pragma split, **SB-overlay-1**'s settle-on-observation as written against the shaped applier, and the `synchronous=FULL` row of the register below all describe the shaped replica store, its `replica_row` table and the second database beside it. A seat now holds `vault.db` itself, the outbox is a table in that same file, and the pragma question is [#456](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/456)'s vault ruling — see [One vault, every seat](#one-vault-every-seat-996) R1, R24 and W5-D1. The properties they kept are restated there; the mechanisms are gone. Everything else in this section stands on its own merits: the worker pool, the warm thread, the SSE frame and Tally's one balance engine are all about the gateway and the apps, which #996 did not touch.

| Id | Current decision | Property it keeps, or the finding it files | Lands in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **SB-payload** | **The SSE `change` frame carries the projected batch and the client applies it.** A pull happens only for catch-up — `hasMore`, reconnect, or a cursor gap. The doorbell-only client feed is the losing side of the contradiction and its pull-on-every-nudge path is **deleted** in the same slice, so the wire is never paying for both. [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929) subscribes over this frame. | **One hop from commit to a subscribed device.** The status quo — server projects a full payload, client discards it and pulls, which re-projects outside the hub memo — is the one answer ruled out: it pays for both sides and uses neither. | **landed** (wave 3, A1). The projection behind the frame is computed **once per commit per authorization**, not per subscriber and not per device: `projectReplicaPage`'s per-device branch is deleted and a device's own intent outcomes are layered on afterwards by `applyReplicaIntentOutcomes`, so 31 further identically-authorized subscribers cost 0 projections and 0 prepared statements per commit. Stated at N = 32, the `SSE_MAX_SUBSCRIBERS` ceiling; a larger fan-out is refused by the cap, not measured. |
| **SB-text** | **Deferred text is a per-entity declared ceiling, and text rides in full.** Only genuinely binary/blob values stay deferred; a fetch-on-demand route exists only for columns an entity explicitly marks lazy. The "oversized list stored, never read" state is deleted either way. | **A note the phone cannot show offline defeats the replica.** The "binary is never eager" ruling this rode on was about blobs; a long note or document text is text, and today it is simply absent on the device with no indicator and no consumer of the deferral list. | **landed** (wave 1, 0b): the ceiling is declared per entity in `packages/vault/src/schema/entity-catalog.ts` (`core.content_item` and `core.content_derivative` at 1 MiB), `enrich.embedding.vector` is the one column declared lazy, and no fetch-on-demand route was built because nothing renders a vector — both clients name the absence instead (`fieldNotOnThisDevice`). |
| **SB-pool** | **The warm-pool size has one source: `CONSTRAINED_WORKER_POOL_SIZE = 1` in `packages/server/src/engine/handlers/worker-pool.ts`.** The `conserve` preset's `workerPoolSize: 0` in `packages/server/src/serve/hardware-profile.ts` and the boot-time override in `packages/server/src/serve/build-gateway.ts` are **deleted**. | **A constrained host is where a cold boot hurts most** ([#659](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/659)) — the reason `worker-pool.ts` already gives at the constant. The [#528](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/528) hardware-budget reading had no dependent property; two numbers ruled opposite ways and the preset silently won at boot. | **landed** (wave 2, B3): `BUDGET_PRESETS` in `hardware-profile.ts` no longer carries `workerPoolSize` at all, and `build-gateway.ts` writes `CENTRAID_WORKER_POOL_SIZE` **only** when the resolved source is `prefs` — the owner's durable override from Settings → Resources, which `resource-summary.ts` publishes as the `workerPoolSize` P1 knob and has no other way to reach the engine. The pool's own two constants are the default: `CONSTRAINED_WORKER_POOL_SIZE = 1`, `DEFAULT_WORKER_POOL_SIZE = 2`. |
| **SB-reuse** | **A handler worker is a reused thread that gets a fresh realm per run.** One unit for first-party handlers and automations alike; a worker that finished cleanly returns to the pool and is terminated only on timeout, error, or a resource-limit breach. | **The thread boundary plus a hard timeout** is what the isolation ruling ([#404](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/404)) actually buys — a hung or crashing handler must not take the gateway down. Disposal after every run was the implementation, never the property, and it costs a TS-loader boot and a transpile per invocation. | **landed** (wave 2, B3): a clean worker returns to the pool and `terminate()` is reached only from the timeout, error and resource-limit paths. Measured on this container over the golden vault, handler p50/p99: standard warm 217.6/303.9 ms → **4.3/67.4 ms**, constrained warm 273.9/333.7 → **3.8/56.8 ms** (#922 receipt, `## lane 5`). |
| **SB-tally** | **Tally's "one balance engine" ruling is superseded.** The engine is the pure module pair `packages/blueprints/src/tally-balance.ts` / `tally-simplify.ts` **the web query handlers already import, which the phone imports directly from wave 4 (E7); until then the phone reaches the fold only through the gateway RPC this ruling supersedes.** From E7 the phone reads balances from the replica through that module, not from gateway RPCs. The [mobile-offline.md](mobile-offline.md) Tally carve-out and [blueprint-seats.md](blueprint-seats.md)'s amended seat row revert with the code. | **One derivation of "who owes whom"** — unchanged, and now kept by importing the module rather than by forcing a tunnel round trip. Reading the same entities on-device through the same fold is not a second derivation; a phone that shows a stale ledger offline over rows it already holds is the cost the old reading charged for nothing. | wave 4 (E7) |
| **SB-replica-sync** | **The replica's sync mode is ruled per seat, and only one seat's change is unconditional.** **Web/desktop** — the wasm replica store runs `synchronous=NORMAL` (derived, rebootstrappable state; the outbox is IndexedDB, `packages/client/src/replica/intent-store.ts`, outside this pragma (**superseded on the premise, not the ruling** — [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R24 moved BOTH seats onto one `SeatIntentStore` in the seat's own vault file, `packages/client/src/replica/seat/seat-intent-store.ts`; `packages/client/src/replica/intent-store.ts` no longer exists. The pragma ruling is unaffected — the outbox is still outside it, now because it is bracketed rather than because it is a different store)) — lands in wave 3 (C2), unconditional. **Mobile** — the replica store **stays `synchronous=FULL` today**; a change there is **conditional on B4's fsync-per-offline-intent measurement on a phone** (measurement before optimisation), because under a rollback journal the saving is one fsync per transaction and E1 (the store core off the JS thread) is the responsiveness win. If the number justifies it, the preferred mechanism is **WAL + `NORMAL` for the replica with each outbox transaction bracketed at `FULL`** — under WAL, `NORMAL` can lose the last commits but cannot corrupt, so the bracket is sound and the outbox keeps its file; this requires re-judging the seam "the attached second reader depends on `journal_mode=DELETE`" (`store-core.ts:1006`), which is **a citation, not a property**, and is C2's first task. Only if that dependency proves real does the outbox move to its own SQLite file/connection at `FULL`, with a one-shot lossless migration of existing outbox rows (red-first) and the Android backup rules and storage accounting updated. Either way C2 lands a power-loss case in the convergence suite proving no acknowledged outbox write is lost and a corrupted replica file re-bootstraps. | **A member's write is fsynced before the enqueue returns; replica page applies are not.** That is the durability the outbox owes and the replica does not — the replica is derived, rebootstrappable state whose lost commits are re-pulled from the cursor. [#456](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/456)'s `FULL` ruling is about **the vault** and is untouched by this. | **web/desktop landed** (wave 3, C2): `wasm-sqlite-driver.ts` declares `synchronous = "NORMAL"` and a driver that declares nothing gets `FULL`, so a new driver takes the safe answer. **Mobile is unchanged and its condition is unmet**: no phone fsync-per-offline-intent number exists, so the replica store stays `FULL`, the outbox keeps the replica's file and connection, and nothing moved. The `journal_mode=DELETE` seam was **re-judged and holds on the phone** — `op-sqlite-driver.ts` runs two live handles per vault file (per-vault writer, gateway-scoped multi-ATTACH reader) and sets a 5 s busy timeout for exactly that reason. It does **not** hold on web, where there is no second handle, and `store-core.ts` sets `PRAGMA journal_mode=DELETE` for both seats from the shared core anyway — a spelling both seats inherit rather than a property either one keeps. |
| **SB-session** | **A replica session lives as long as the tab or window**, and closes on hide or memory pressure instead. | The [#599](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/599) 30-second idle close saved **one SQLite handle and one worker, and nothing consumes that saving** — while the reopen it cost was paid on every return to an app. Closing on hide/memory pressure keeps the memory property and drops the tax on switching. | **landed** (wave 4, C6) as `replicaScopeDisposition(page, refs)` in `shell-session.ts`, and it is narrower than "lives as long as the tab": a scope a screen still holds is **never** closed under it (`hold`), an unheld scope on a visible page keeps the 30 s grace (`warm`) so leaving an app and coming back pays no second open, and an unheld scope on a `hidden` or `frozen` page closes **at once** (`close`) — the browser freezes a hidden page precisely when it wants its memory back. |
| **SB-instrument** | **F1 (per-request gateway phase timing) is absorbed into [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927)'s gateway trace slice** — one instrument, not an interim standing beside its replacement. F2 and F4 are **closed as superseded** by #927's journey ledger and device rung. The interim numbers this umbrella cites are F3/F5 plus #927's work counters, until #927 wave 3 lands the ledger. | **Deletion with replacement, applied to instruments.** A second phase-timing seam shipped a wave before the trace would have had to be deleted by the trace's own slice; citing #927's trace and counters from wave 1 costs one wave of numbers and no duplicate instrument. | #927 (trace); wave 1 (F3/F5) |
| **SB-loader** | **ADOPT — the blueprint `queries/*.ts` are Metro-loadable today, so the phone runs the same query handler the web seat runs.** The wave-1 spike pulled `packages/blueprints/apps/tally/queries/dashboard.ts` into the mobile graph through a DOM-free native ctx adapter and exported it: **+4 modules, +31,494 B (+0.39 %) of Hermes bytecode, `expo export` exit 0**, with `apps/mobile/metro.config.js` **untouched** (and `packages/blueprints/package.json` unchanged — the export map already resolves `queries/*`). The issue's premise that a bundler change was the blocker is therefore stale; what is not loadable is `app-inline.tsx`, which pairs the queries with a React DOM `Root`, and that is an entry file, not a bundler limit. Indicative handler time on the native session over a node-sqlite replica driver: **11.1 ms median at N=40** expenses (160 splits, 40 payers) and **188 ms at N=2,000** — the query's own declared window — on a 4-core / 15 GB host. Consequently the per-app projection fork is **deleted app by app** (E3/E7, **Tally first**), not maintained behind a parity oracle. Both preconditions are DONE: **(a) in wave 1**, **(b) in E7**. **(a) One ctx builder**: `packages/client/src/replica/inline-query-ctx-core.ts` (new) holds everything both seats shared — `guardedRow`, `receiptIdFor`, `inlineReadsFor`, `buildInlineCtxCore`, `runInlineQueryCore` and `ctx.time` — DOM-free by construction, re-exported through the **`@centraid/client/replica/native`** subpath React Native already consumes from source, so it does not drag `gateway-client-core.ts` into a Metro bundle. Both seats became thin callers keeping only _what one row becomes_: the adapter shrank **238 → 81** lines and the web `inlineQueryCtx.ts` **294 → 173**, and duplication between them went **93 of 238 lines (39.1 %) → 0 blocks, 0 lines (0.0 %)** against both each other and the core. The hand-rolled online-only guard was deleted in favour of `packages/client/src/replica/online-only-guard.ts` — a third copy the same finding surfaced. Evidence: this issue's receipt, `## w1 Metro-loader spike — ctx-core de-duplication`. **(b) No `__centraid*` provenance is handed to a handler** — the phone's mounted read plane decorates rows with `__centraidScopeId` / `__centraidScopeLabel` / `__centraidCanWrite`, which a handler that spreads a row (Tally's `recurring`) then emits. Because both seats import one builder, the strip landed in **exactly one place — the native seat's `inlineReadsFor` row callback**, as `withoutScopeProvenance` in `apps/mobile/src/lib/replica/inline-query-ctx.native.ts` — and the phone's payload now equals the web's without filtering. E7 delivered the tail of this ruling, app by app: **Tally runs the web query handlers on the phone** (`tally-reads.ts`, the seven `queries/*.ts` modules over the mounted replica), its seven gateway read RPCs and their 10-minute stale clock are **deleted**, and the read fork they were is gone; **Locker's list, shelves and search followed** (`apps/mobile/src/apps/locker/locker-reads.ts`, the `items`/`search`/`trash` modules over the same plane) once **#928** ruled that `locker.item` visibility is the app grant's and authentication gates permits and `reveal` only — its `ctx.vault.authenticate` gate is deleted from `queries/items.ts`, the Watchtower and count decorations became `optional` invocations that answer undecorated rather than refusing, and its own 10-minute stale clock went the way Tally's did. `reveal`, `authenticate`, permits and the secret-bearing writes are unchanged and still online-only, and `ONLINE_ONLY_ACTIONS` is untouched. Parity is held phone-vs-web on the same rows by `tests/integration-mobile/tally-balance-parity.integration.test.ts` and `tests/integration-mobile/locker-rows-parity.integration.test.ts`. | **One query handler per app, on both seats.** The ~2.5k-line phone projection fork (`people-model.ts`, `docs-projection*.ts`, `notes-model.ts`, `timeline-model.ts`, `useTasks`) stops being a second derivation of every app's reads, and the 1,000-row drift it carries goes with it; Tally's seven gateway RPCs and their 10-minute stale clock become a local read. Refusing would have kept the fork honest with a parity oracle and paid for two derivations forever — strictly worse, and the oracle exists either way (`apps/mobile/src/lib/replica/inline-query-ctx.native.test.ts`). | wave 1 (spike, adopted); E3/E7 (wave 4) |

**The re-judged register.** Every seam the review's first pass accepted "by ruling" was asked one question: does a user-visible or security property depend on it, for the product as it is now? Recorded in full so no wave inherits the deference.

| Seam | Ruling cited | Property that depends on it now | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Worker spawned and **terminated** per handler invocation | isolation ([#404](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/404)) | a hung/crashing handler must not take the gateway down — that needs a thread boundary and a timeout, **not disposal after every run** | **finding** (B3) |
| Warm pool `0` on the `conserve` preset | hardware budget ([#528](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/528)) | none; `worker-pool.ts` rules the opposite ("not zero (#659): a constrained host is where a cold boot hurts most", `CONSTRAINED_WORKER_POOL_SIZE = 1`) and `build-gateway.ts` overwrites it from the preset at boot | **contradiction** (B3) |
| SSE frame projects the full shape; client discards it and pulls | doorbell-only feed (client) vs payload frame (server) | none — two rulings, each side built for the other's absence; the server pays a projection nobody reads | **contradiction** (A1) |
| `synchronous=FULL` on the client replica store | durability ([#456](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/456), a ruling about the **vault**) | none: the replica is derived and rebootstrappable; the outbox that must survive a crash is a separate store | **finding** (C2) |
| `journal_mode=DELETE` on the phone's replica | same | **real**: the attached second op-sqlite reader handle depends on it | holds on mobile; re-judge on web where no second handle exists |
| 200 ms kit debounce / 120 ms phone coalesce applied to the user's **own** overlay invalidation | delta-storm suppression | real for remote batches; **nothing for a local echo** | **finding** (D1) |
| 8 ms group-commit window with one write queued | fsync amortisation ([#659](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/659)) | real under load only; with an empty queue it is pure latency | **finding, fixed** (B7): an idle `GroupCommitQueue` commits on the next microtask, which still gathers every write issued together at no added latency; the window opens only for the turn after a batch larger than one committed, and its size is `groupCommitWindowMs(storageFsyncMs)` from the boot probe rather than the unmeasured `NORMAL ? 8 : 5` pair. Lone write through the plane's queue, p50 **12.29 ms → 7.33 ms**. |
| Replica session closed 30 s after last holder | memory ([#599](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/599)) | one SQLite handle and one worker; nothing consumes the saving | **finding** (C6) |
| 1,000-row silent default cap, both layers | bounded reads ([#262](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/262)) | bounding is right; **silence** has no dependent and produces a wrong screen | **correctness hole** (Part 0) |
| 64 KB deferred columns with no fetch path | "binary never eager" | real for blobs; **text has no consumer of the deferral** | **correctness hole** (Part 0) |
| One intent per HTTP round trip on drain | outbox order ([#880](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/880)) | **order** is the property, not one-request-per-intent | **finding** (A6) |
| `awaiting-change` until the feed returns the device's own change | convergence truth | the intent response already carries the committed cursor | **finding** (A6) |
| ~~~4 durable commits per intent~~ | crash replay ([#916](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/916)) | **REFUTED by measurement (B8, 2026-09-04).** The cost is **3.00**, not ~4, and one of the three is already the folded canonical transaction: `invokeBatchSettled` opens one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` per arrival window carrying the mutation, the invocation row and the audit write together. Each of the other two protects a named crash property. **Admission (`sending`, before dispatch)** is the only durable carrier of the device-visible intent identity — device, app, action, payload hash — which no canonical transaction can reconstruct; it is also the latch that makes a redelivered duplicate conceal instead of dispatch, and the row a parked command transitions days later. **The terminal outcome (after dispatch)** is the ACTION's outcome, not the invocation's: an action may span several canonical commits and may end contradicting one. Two `replica-intent-route.test.ts` cases pin it — "a post-invoke denial is durable and does not re-dispatch on retry", and a canonical write that stays durable while its journal finalisation aborted and is repaired on the next retry without re-entering the handler. Folding either would turn a member's committed write into a rollback. | **fold refused, with evidence**; the seam holds |
| Store core synchronous ⇒ op-sqlite `executeSync` on the JS thread | one core for web and native | the property is **code sharing**, not which thread runs it | **finding** (E1, reframed) |
| First mount awaits tunnel + `/info` before local files open | gateway compatibility wall | real for the wall; **nothing about opening a local file depends on the answer** | **finding** (E9) |
| Hash-chained receipt per `gateway.read` | "what looked at my data" | **real** for grantee, agent and `reveal` paths (sharing dashboard "last used", Locker access history); **no consumer found for an owner device reading its own vault** | holds where consumed; owner-read receipts are settled by [#928](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/928) A4; commit off the read path (B1) |
| Order guards as window functions | replica order = canonical order ([#262](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/262)) | **real**; the property does not require the implementation | holds; C3 stays |
| Five perf designs "evidence-gated, not adopted" ([Performance and Rust byte plane](#performance-and-rust-byte-plane)) | evidence gate ([#456](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/456)) | the gate can never open: no instrument measures open-to-paint, tap-to-echo or reconnect-to-converged, and statement-count budgets are satisfied by the worst offenders (a 170 ms window-function read is one statement) | [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927) is the precondition, not the last wave |

**One premise in that register does not survive the code**, and the ruling above rather than the register is the design: the `synchronous=FULL` row says "the outbox that must survive a crash is a separate store", which was true on web/desktop (IndexedDB) but **not on mobile**, where the outbox tables share the replica's file and connection. It is now true on NEITHER: [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R24 gave both seats one `SeatIntentStore` in the seat's own vault file, which is what lets an executed answer clear its overlay in the transaction that carries its commit (`docs/mobile-offline.md`). `SB-replica-sync` therefore rules per seat, and only one seat's change is unconditional. **Web/desktop:** the wasm replica store runs `synchronous=NORMAL` (derived, rebootstrappable; the outbox is IndexedDB, outside this pragma (**superseded on the premise, not the ruling** — [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R24 moved BOTH seats onto one `SeatIntentStore` in the seat's own vault file, `packages/client/src/replica/seat/seat-intent-store.ts`; `packages/client/src/replica/intent-store.ts` no longer exists. The pragma ruling is unaffected — the outbox is still outside it, now because it is bracketed rather than because it is a different store)) — wave 3 (C2), unconditional. **Mobile:** the replica store **stays `synchronous=FULL` today**, and a change there is conditional on B4's fsync-per-offline-intent measurement on a phone — measurement before optimisation, because under a rollback journal the saving is one fsync per transaction while E1 (the store core off the JS thread) is the responsiveness win. If the number justifies it, the preferred mechanism is **WAL + `NORMAL` for the replica with each outbox transaction bracketed at `FULL`**: under WAL, `NORMAL` can lose the last commits but cannot corrupt, so the bracket is sound and the outbox keeps its file. That requires re-judging "the attached second reader depends on `journal_mode=DELETE`" (`store-core.ts:1006`) — **a citation, not a property** — which is C2's first task; only if the dependency proves real does the outbox move to its own SQLite file and connection at `FULL`, with a one-shot lossless migration of existing outbox rows (red-first) and the Android backup rules and storage accounting updated. Either way C2 lands a power-loss case in the convergence suite proving no acknowledged outbox write is lost and a corrupted replica file re-bootstraps. The property kept is unchanged: a member's write is fsynced before the enqueue returns; replica page applies are not.

**Pending-write overlay (SB-overlay-1 … SB-overlay-9).** Part G of [#922](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/922), re-imagined against this program's own changes (A1/A6/C2/D1/E1, [#928](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/928)'s one plane, [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929)'s subscription) under the same rule. The read law `replica ⊕ outbox` **holds**, as does the durable outbox and the Pending changes sheet as the guaranteed place — a pending insert that sorts outside the current window is correctly absent from the list and present in the sheet. What did not survive is how the overlay is dropped, named and presented.

| Id | Current decision | Property it keeps, or the finding it files | Lands in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
