| **SB-overlay-1** (G1) | **Settle by observation, not by the answer.** An `executed` outcome carries the resulting row versions; a row's overlay is dropped only once the replica holds that row at or above that version, so `awaiting-change` becomes per row. **SB-overlay-8** (G8) rides this row: a payload-frame echo of an already-applied row causes **0 re-reads**, because rowVersion equality short-circuits the invalidation. | One rule covers four cases that otherwise each need their own: the vanishing-row frame the shaped applier had (it settled intents before rows applied), the wider gap A6 opens by clearing on the answer before the pull lands, C2's power-loss case (a `FULL`-bracketed settlement survives, a `NORMAL` row apply is lost), and #929's member write whose answer arrives from the origin one hop before the row. | wave 3 (folded into the A6 slice) |
| **SB-overlay-2** (G2) | **Client-minted row ids.** The projection mints the row's UUID, the input carries it, the origin honours or refuses it; the trace contract already makes the intent id the trace id. `stablePendingRowId`'s `pending:` grammar, the revision-identity probe, `SYNTHETIC_PENDING_ROW` and the `pending:` guards are **deleted**. Preceded by a wave-2 **probe** (test-only, reported and not landed if red): no code on the server or in the post path rewrites a synthetic parent id inside a child write, so an offline create-group followed by add-expense either posts the literal `pending:` id or is withheld per app. | Executed means "the same row lost its badge". The server-as-sole-id-minter seam kept nothing — the server already refuses id reuse by payload hash — while the id swap re-keys lists, closes editors, and forces `pending:` special cases in `intents.ts` and `centraid-inline.ts`. | wave 2 (probe); wave 4 (red-first slice, after #928 wave 4 and B8, because it touches the origin's id policy in `execution.ts` / `replica-intent-shape.ts`) |
| **SB-overlay-3** (G3) | **Pending as a sidecar, one key in the row.** Only the pending key stays a row column; status, reason, attempts, enqueued-at and versions ride a per-result map keyed by intent id, read at render time as today. | Rows stay schema-pure for a query handler that runs on both seats, so **SB-loader**'s precondition (b) shrinks to "strip nothing but the key". The badge itself still needs a row-level signal, which is what the one key is. | wave 3, after the A6 slice and before E3 |
| **SB-overlay-4** (G4) | **One "waiting on" vocabulary.** `parked` gains a structured `waitingOn` of kind owner, origin or gateway plus a label from the link; the owner's phone sees the member's write in Approvals through the replica feed; `apps/mobile/src/lib/replica/steward-label.ts` is **deleted** with the commons rail. | Three vocabularies (`parked`, the steward label, #929's member intents) were held by [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883)'s known-duplication note, which keeps nothing once #929 deletes commons. Recorded here because `pending-overlay.ts` is this umbrella's file. | #929 S2 (wave 3) and its wave-4 deletion |
| **SB-overlay-5** (G5) | **Real states and structured reasons.** `conflict` and `expired` become `IntentState`s (ALTER-only widening on the phone store, additive on IndexedDB); a conflict whose base row is gone gets its own verdict instead of "expected 4, found undefined"; `denied` carries a structured `denial` (grant, principal, subject, scope, reason) that #928 wave 4 fills from the one plane, so the row can offer ask-again versus discard; a shape removal (#929 S5) settles that shape's queued intents as `expired` with "no longer shared with you". | "Schema unchanged" was the whole justification for persisting a conflict as `failed` plus detail and for `expired` being presentation-only — a citation, with no property behind it, and a user reading a parser artefact. | wave 3 (client half + protocol field); #928 and #929 halves in their own waves |
| **SB-overlay-6** (G6) | **Delete-projection tripwire**, the same shape as #928's static tripwire: for every action whose handler deletes or soft-deletes, the pending projection must project `op: delete` or set `deleted_at`; registered in `tests/claims.json`. First output is a count across the eight apps of excluded, patch and delete projections per destructive action. | Projecting a delete as a patch was a per-app choice with nothing depending on it, and it leaves the row on screen after the user deleted it (Tally `delete-expense`). | wave 2 (`w2-tw`) |
| **SB-overlay-7** (G7) | **Overlay cost moves with the store core.** C1's in-memory pending set and E5's per-generation memoisation stand; when E1 moves the store core off the JS thread, composition goes with it, and the receipt reports overlay rows composed per read from #927's counters rather than a new instrument. | Enumerating the overlay per mounted read was held by "one core"; the property that ruling buys is **code sharing, not which thread runs it** (E1, reframed). | wave 4 (E1's contract) |
| **SB-overlay-9** (G9) | **Age on the badge, no expiry.** After 24 h the copy reads "saved on this phone since 3 Aug", from `enqueuedAt`; queued intents never expire on their own. | **Real, and re-judged as still real**: member data is never dropped. The gap was that a week-old queued write is indistinguishable from a second-old one, which the age closes without an expiry. | wave 2 (`w2-age`) |

**The overlay seam register.** Same question, asked of every seam of the pending-write overlay.

| Seam | Held by | Property that depends on it now | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Overlay dropped when the device's own change returns via the feed | convergence truth | none once the answer carries the versions | **finding** (SB-overlay-1) |
| Synthetic `pending:` ids for inserts, real id minted by the gateway | server as sole id minter | none: the server already refuses id reuse by payload hash; the swap re-keys lists, closes editors, and forces `pending:` special cases in `intents.ts` and `centraid-inline.ts` | **finding** (SB-overlay-2) |
| Badge fields injected as row columns | one law for both seats | real for the badge, but it collides with **SB-loader**'s "strip `__centraid*` before a handler" | **finding** (SB-overlay-3) |
| Three "waiting on" vocabularies (`parked`, steward label, #929 member intents) | [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) known duplication | none after #929 deletes commons | **finding** (SB-overlay-4, [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929)) |
| Conflict persisted as `failed` plus detail; `expired` presentation-only | "schema unchanged" | none — a citation | **finding** (SB-overlay-5) |
| Delete actions projected as a patch (Tally `delete-expense`) | per-app choice | none found; the row stays on screen after the user deleted it | **finding** (SB-overlay-6) |
| Overlay enumerated per mounted read on the JS thread | one core | code sharing, not the thread (E1) | **finding** (SB-overlay-7) |
| No expiry on queued intents | member data is never dropped | **real** | **holds** (SB-overlay-9 adds age) |

**Considered, not now:** one device's queued writes visible on the owner's other seats (offline is physics; a count in the heartbeat is a later idea); merging the intent, placement and upload outboxes (re-judged after #929 wave 4 deletes the commons rail).

## One authority plane (#928)

Ruled 2026-09-03 by [#928](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/928), which re-judged the half of the consent system [Grants v2](#grants-v2--one-authority-plane-883) left alone: the app grant plane, its purposes, its policy table and the evaluator that ran on every non-owner read. A personal vault has exactly **four kinds of principal that can be told yes or no, and first-party apps are not one of them** — they are the owner's own screens, running the owner's own code, on the owner's own device. These rulings land ahead of #928's code waves, so no wave is built over a guess; where a sentence below describes a mechanism, it states the ruling in force and the wave that lands it.

| Principal | Subject | Enforced at |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the owner's device | the vault, with a trust tier | identity, one statement (already so) |
| automation or assistant | pack or entity, read or act, attenuated per run | the execution clamp, plus a standing answer row |
| person or circle | a shareable subject | delivery into their vault (already so) |
| external service | a capability and egress class | the enrichment gate (already so) |

All four fit `share_authority`. Two already live there; this ruling moves the other two in and deletes the plane they came from.

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **AP-principals** | The four kinds above are the whole list, and enforcement locus follows the kind (as [V-locus](#grants-v2--one-authority-plane-883) already rules). A first-party app is not a principal: it holds no credential, is told nothing, and cannot be revoked, because revoking the owner's own screen is not a thing the owner wants to do. `principal_kind` gains `automation`; `app` stays **reserved and unwritten** against a future third-party door. |
| **AP-apps-declare** | **Apps declare, they are not granted.** Each app keeps a build-time entity manifest (the existing `vault.scopes` block, minus purpose, minus row filters and field masks): the entities and columns its screens read and the commands it invokes. It is used for exactly two things — **replica shape composition**, which becomes a static function of the manifest and the sealed-column registry (no evaluator, no purpose, no grantee join, no `readGrantees`), and a **static tripwire** in the style of `one-computation.test.ts` that fails the build if an app's `queries/*`, `actions/*` or mobile hooks touch an entity the manifest does not declare. Minimisation is kept at build time, with a reviewable diff, at zero runtime cost. **Amended by AP-clamp-vocabulary**: four `vault.scopes` entries still carry a `rowFilter` or `fieldMask` (Locker ×2, People, Tally) and they are **live** — the shape composer ignores them, but the per-call execution clamp reads them, which is where they do real work. What is dead is the one surviving `purpose`, in `packages/blueprints/apps/people/app.json`: nothing reads it. |
| **AP-clamp-vocabulary** | **Row filters and field masks are the EXECUTION CLAMP's vocabulary, not the manifest's** (ruled 2026-09-04, closing lane 1's finding (a)). They were kept in the replica shape until wave 4 because the shape was composed by the evaluator; once composition became static, the only reader left that needs them is the per-run clamp, where they do real work — `intersectFieldMasks` and the pin-conflict rule in `packages/vault/src/gateway/access.ts`, and `scopeCovers` in `packages/vault/src/scope-extent.ts`, which requires an outer row filter to be IDENTICAL and an outer mask to be a superset before a run is admitted. A first-party app's own row filter is ALSO re-expressed as a `WHERE` clause in the query that wanted it, because the clamp filters after the window and the query must filter before it — Locker's access history is the worked example (AP-locker-boundary): without the SQL `WHERE`, a busy vault's newest 200 receipts could all be another app's and the clamp would hand the screen an empty history. |
| **AP-owner-direct** | **Owner-app reads are owner-direct.** A handler invoked for an owner device reads **as that device**: the app bridge's `kind: "app"` credential and `evaluateAccess`'s app path go, and an owner-device read of the owner's own vault runs zero grant statements. `access_app` survives as the **install register** (name, label, its signing key, status). `access_grant`, `access_grant_scope`, `access_policy`, `access_scope_tombstone`, `access_scope_request`, `access_seed_row`'s dependence on grants and the install top-up are **deleted** — none appears in any DDL, and `evaluateAccess` names three answers: owner-direct, the assistant riding the acting owner, and an automation's row. `Identity.surface` survives beside it as **attribution only**: it names which first-party surface carried an owner-direct call, so a receipt and a log can say so; it is not a principal, carries no credential and grants nothing. |
| **AP-automation-principal** | **Automations and the assistant are principals in the one plane.** An automation's compiled manifest mints `granted` rows per (pack or entity × read \| act), and the owner's refusals are `declined` rows; a widened manifest still parks as a decision, and both the request and the answer are rows Settings → Access shows. The per-run execution clamp is unchanged. **The assistant holds no standing grant** — its reads and writes ride the on-behalf-of identity with the acting owner's trust tier, receipted as exercises — and a scheduler-fired automation with no owner behind it acts under its own `automation` row rather than uncapped. |
| **AP-one-id-space** | **One id space, one receipt shape.** Every receipt references `authority_id` from the one authority table; `access_receipt.grant_id` — which today names four different id spaces — is renamed and typed accordingly, and `purpose_concept_id` leaves the receipt with the purpose vocabulary. Receipts record exercises of an answer, reveals, denials and the owner's own high-risk acts; **owner-device reads of the owner's own vault are not receipted**, because nothing consumes them. "Last used" on the Access dashboard becomes computable, and is implemented. |
| **AP-attenuations** | **Attenuations move to the principal they attenuate.** Companion `grant_profile_json` becomes `device`-principal rows with the surface as subject — a device answer with a device as its subject, rather than a surface mask parked on an enrollment — and `outbox_grant` becomes `automation`-principal rows. Each moves in the wave that deletes its old store, never beside it. |
| **AP-egress-rows** | **An egress answer is a row with `subject_type = 'egress'`** (ruled 2026-09-04, realizing AP-attenuations). `outbox_grant` is deleted with its readers: the standing "always allow" for an external write becomes a `share_authority` row whose subject is the destination and whose verb is the semantic capability — a capability plus an egress class, which is what the enrichment gate decides on. The principal is whoever acts: `automation` for an automation, `device` for the owner's own surfaces, which reach the outbox on the device credential. `outbox_item.grant_id` becomes `authority_id`, a real reference into the one table. **Property**: the review feed, "last used" and the purge cascade see one id space instead of a side table, and an automation cannot mint its own standing egress answer — the `granted_by` CHECK refuses a row without the member who approved the item it was minted from. |
| **AP-companion-projection** | **The companion attenuation rows are the source of truth; the gateway holds a projection of them** (ruled 2026-09-04, realizing AP-attenuations). A Companion device's surface set is `share_authority` rows, principal `device`, subject type `app.surface`, verb `use`. Because the gateway authorizes a Companion request **before** any vault is open, `gateway.db` keeps a per-(device, vault) projection of those rows, rebuilt whenever the vault mounts or the answer changes; `devices.grant_profile_json` is replaced by a single `attenuated` flag and has no reader. **Property**: the failure mode of companion authorization stays CLOSED — an attenuated device whose projection is missing is refused until the vault opens, rather than widened to full reach because a file could not be read. |
| **AP-give-residue** | **The give-plane residue is deleted.** `share_edges`, `share_effects`, the coordinator, the edge store, the edge routes, the peer-plane sweep's edge half and the retire pass go. The verb retired in [#825](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/825) and the machinery now serves only same-owner placement, which is a **command**: project then delete, one transaction. `share_access_receipts` is kept as history only. |
| **AP-locker-boundary** | **Locker's boundary is restated where it holds.** The sealed-column registry, the one-shot permit, `ONLINE_ONLY_ACTIONS` and the `reveal` verb are untouched — those are what protect a secret. The `access.receipt` **row filter** was never the boundary: it guarded a first-party screen against itself, so it becomes a `WHERE` clause in Locker's own history query, and `reveal` stays a distinct verb on the sealed registry rather than a grant scope. |

**v0 stance, unchanged from [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883).** A superseded store is deleted in the wave that replaces it, never carried beside its replacement. The only compatibility target is member data: app grant rows are derived state and are not migrated, while automation grants and tombstones migrate one-shot into authority rows, demonstrated red before landing.

**Two of #928's open questions are answered here.** **Third-party apps**: `app` stays a reserved and unwritten `principal_kind`; the static manifest becomes the install-time consent artefact if and when a door exists, and no door is designed now. **A scheduler-fired automation's principal**: **one row per automation**, not one per pack — the pack is shared machinery and the automation is what the owner said yes or no to. Two are deliberately not decided here, each with the place that decides it: the raw SQL door (`sqlAsOwner`) is **deleted** — its callers were enumerated in wave 3 and were the assistant's, which now reads through the same plane as everything else; whether owner-direct reads stop being receipted entirely or are sampled is decided with [#922](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/922)'s B1.

### The rulings, re-judged (#928)

The register #928 built by applying [ONT-review](#ontology-v0-close-916) to every seam of the consent plane. A row is kept only where a concrete product or security property depends on it **for the product as it is now**; a row that survives on a citation alone is a finding.

| Seam | Ruling cited | Property that depends on it now | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| "Installing IS the consent" | [#306](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/306) | none — all eight apps are bundled and installed by default, and the owner is never asked; the only consent moment an app grant produces is a widened manifest on upgrade | **finding** — the consent is fiction; the manifest becomes a build-time declaration (AP-apps-declare) |
| App grants as app-strategy machinery beneath manifests | [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) V-split | none — SECURITY.md disclaims protection against an installed app with broad grants, and there is no third-party app door | **finding** — kept by citation; deleted with the evaluator (AP-owner-direct) |
| Purposes and the DPV vocabulary | [#306](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/306) | none — 8 apps and 29 automations declare exactly one purpose, `dpv:ServiceProvision`; the second party the vocabulary was held for arrived in [#825](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/825) and sharing did not use it | **finding** — deleted, receipt column included (AP-one-id-space) |
| `access_policy` purpose / minimization / retention | [ONT-06](#ontology-v0-close-916) kept three kinds of four | none — zero non-test writers; the table has been seeded empty since #916 and `evaluateAccess` consults it twice per non-owner read | **finding** — deleted (AP-owner-direct) |
| Row filters and field masks on app scopes | [#306](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/306) | none — 5 of 277 manifest scopes carry one, and every one is a first-party app reading its own undo history or its own receipts | **finding** — each is re-expressed as a `WHERE` clause in the query that needed it, before the evaluator goes |
| Locker: "the rowFilter is the boundary" | [#873](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/873) L-access | none — the filter's counterparty is Locker itself, a first-party screen; the properties that hold are the sealed-column registry and the one-shot permit, and neither is a grant | **finding** — the sentence is superseded; the boundary is restated where it holds (AP-locker-boundary) |
| Tombstones and scope requests | [#308](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/308) | **real for automations**: an automation authors its own manifest, so an owner's "no" must survive as data or the next compile silently re-asks | **kept, re-homed** — `declined` rows in the one plane (AP-automation-principal); irrelevant for bundled apps and deleted there |
| The assistant's standing `act` over every command schema | the code's own comment — "more privileged than any app, contained only by confirm-parking" | none — on-behalf-of already carries the acting owner's trust tier, which is the cap that should apply | **finding** — the standing grant is deleted; any command that only worked under it is itself a finding |
| `sqlAsOwner` with purpose `owner-assistant` | [#306](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/306) purpose vocabulary | none — a door outside the plane whose purpose string is decorative | **finding** — resolved in wave 3 once its callers are enumerated (deleted if only the assistant uses it) |
| Per-read receipts keyed by a polymorphic `grant_id` | [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) V-receipts | none as built — V-dashboard promises "last used" and four id spaces share the column, so it is uncomputable | **finding** — one id space, and the promise is implemented (AP-one-id-space) |
| The give-plane residue (`share_edges`, `share_effects`, coordinator, edge store, edge routes, sweep half, retire pass) | [#726](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/726), retired as a verb by [#825](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/825) | none — it now serves only same-owner placement, which is a command | **finding** — deleted; placement becomes one vault command (AP-give-residue) |
| Companion `grant_profile_json` | [#726](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/726) device attenuation | **real**: a companion device is attenuated to a set of surfaces, and that attenuation is what keeps an autofill companion from reaching the rest of the vault | **kept, re-homed** — it is a device-principal answer wearing a surface as its subject, so it becomes rows of the one plane, with a gateway-side projection for the pre-open request path (AP-attenuations, AP-companion-projection) |
| The spine: one owner per vault; `share_authority` with immutable `granted` / `declined` rows; the `share.*` pack as sole writer; delivery as enforcement for people; the sealed-column registry and Locker permits; the demo-seed exclusion for autonomous readers; the per-run execution clamp | [#726](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/726), [#825](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/825), [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883), [#873](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/873) | **real**: each is the only thing standing between a principal and data it was never told yes about — an auditable answer, one writer, enforcement at the boundary a principal cannot cross, and a per-run cap an autonomous reader cannot widen | **holds** — untouched by this umbrella, and the shape every ruling above is folded into |

**What the shape of the landed plane is, said once.** Three mechanisms that the rulings above imply but do not name, so nothing has to be re-derived from code:

| Mechanism | Shape |
| --- | --- |
| A parked ask | `share_authority_request` — deliberately **not** a `share_authority` row, because that table records what the member SAID and a parked ask is not an answer. One open ask per automation (`share_authority_request_open`, partial unique on `decided_at IS NULL`); a re-publish replaces its scope set; deciding it stamps `decided_at` and writes the real answer next door. |
| "Last used" | A row of its own, upserted beside the receipt that cites it — not a column on `share_authority`, whose row is immutable except for `revoked_at`, so stamping it per use would rewrite the member's answer and push a replica change every time an automation read anything. Absent row = never used, which is what the dashboard draws. |
| A same-owner placement | `placeItemsInVault` — read the closure from the origin outside any transaction, hardlink the blobs (idempotent), then ONE transaction in the audience vault. The origin is never written, so no two-database transaction exists; a `move` releases the source set after the projection commits. `share_access_receipts` is the exactly-once anchor and the only history kept. |

The one-shot backfill w3a landed to carry prior app grants and refusals into the plane is **gone with the plane it read from**: there are no app grant rows left to migrate, so `packages/vault/src/grant/automation-authority.ts` holds only the live path — `automationSubjectsOf`, `automationAnswers`, `recordAutomationAnswers` (revoke-then-insert, so a changed answer is a new immutable row rather than an edited one) and `revokeAutomationAnswers`.

One earlier open question closes with this ruling: `locker.item` list visibility does **not** depend on `ctx.vault.authenticate` — the list was authorised by the app grant alone (and an owner device bypasses grants), and authentication gates permits and reveal only.

## Perf and scale infrastructure (#927)

Ruled 2026-09-03 by [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927), the first-principles review of the perf and scale machinery built under [#404](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/404), [#456](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/456), [#532](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/532), [#659](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/659), [#873](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/873) and [#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915). The job of that machinery is to answer, for every change and every release, **whether the owner's experience on their device, at their volume, got worse, by how much, and where**; measured against that job it mostly measured machine costs, on shared Linux runners, at empty or synthetic volume. The rebuild has five principles: **P1** the product emits a local-only trace and the tests consume it; **P2** deterministic work counters separate work from time and gate the merge rung with no retry and no history; **P3** time on a shared runner is measured as a paired candidate/PR run with a confidence interval; **P4** measurement happens at year-3 volume on the hardware the owner has, including a device rung; **P5** one ledger keyed `surface × journey × volume × hardware` in which every entry names its spans and its consumer.

| Id | Current decision | Property it keeps, or the finding it files | Lands in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **PS-trace** | **The trace and work-counter contract is landed and is the one shape every emitter and consumer imports**: `packages/core/src/protocol/trace.ts` — `TRACE_FORMAT_VERSION = 1`, the closed nine hops (`seat`, `tunnel`, `gateway`, `handler`, `sqlite`, `commit`, `sse`, `apply`, `render`), four seats, the nine journeys, `TraceSpan` / `TraceRecord`, the nine integer `WORK_COUNTER_KEYS`, a strict `validateTraceRecord`, a pure `waterfall`, and deterministic sampling that is **off by default**. For a write the trace id **is** the replica intent id. Traces are sovereign: they live in the owner's diagnostics, are purged with the vault, and are never egressed ([logs.md](logs.md)). | **A regression answers _where_ as well as _whether_.** A journey budget is a query over spans, so rigs shrink to "drive the journey, read the trace"; the always-on half is integers, so the merge rung pays no wall clock for it. | **landed**, contract and emitters: [`packages/core/src/protocol/trace.ts`](../packages/core/src/protocol/trace.ts) is imported by the gateway (`packages/server/src/serve/gateway-trace.ts`, `trace-store.ts`), the web/desktop seat (`packages/client/src/replica/trace.ts`), the phone (`apps/mobile/src/lib/replica/native-trace.ts`) and the counter gate (`tests/perf/work-counters.perf.test.ts`) |
| **PS-922-instruments** | **[#922](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/922)'s F2 and F4 are closed as superseded** — F2 by this umbrella's **journey ledger** (P5) and F4 by its **device rung** (P4). F1 is absorbed into the gateway trace slice. The ledger has landed as [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json); F3/F5 plus the work counters were the wave-1 interim, and **every #922 receipt from its wave 2 onward cites before/after numbers against a ledger entry** (the map from #922 section to entry key is that receipt's `## Ledger citations (close pass)`). | **Deletion with replacement, applied to instruments.** A second phase-timing seam or a second mobile-number source would have shipped one wave before its own replacement deleted it; one wave of interim numbers costs less than a duplicate instrument. See [SB-instrument](#snappier-blueprint-apps-922). | #927 waves 1–3 |
| **PS-evidence-gate** | **The "five perf designs are evidence-gated, not adopted" row is superseded as practice.** The principle stands; what did not is the claim that the gate could open — no instrument measured open-to-paint, tap-to-echo or reconnect-to-converged, and statement-count budgets are satisfied by the worst offenders (a 170 ms window-function read is one statement). **#927 is the precondition, not the last wave**: the gate for the dependency-aware read cache, compiled consent decisions, incremental materializations, verified boot snapshot and lazy vault mount is the **journey ledger**, and none of the five is adopted until an entry in it says so. [Performance and Rust byte plane](#performance-and-rust-byte-plane) keeps the designs and their boundary; this row replaces its gate. | **An evidence gate with no instrument is a permanent refusal wearing the word "later".** Naming the ledger as the gate makes the condition checkable, and keeps the eager-mount correctness ruling (a missed automation wakeup is a correctness failure) untouched. | **landed**: the ledger is [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) and [§ Performance and Rust byte plane](#performance-and-rust-byte-plane) names it as the gate |
| **PS-diet** | **A deleted rig is deleted with its entry, and the deletion list is reviewed before it executes.** #927 deletes the actions-cache history, the 30-sample drift rule, the 3× catastrophe rule, the retry-once step, `client-query-counts.json` and the serial-pool requirement in `vitest.perf.config.ts` / `vitest.scale.config.ts`, plus every rig measuring a machine cost no journey entry cites. **The list of those rigs is produced by wave 1 and approved by the maintainer before wave 5 executes it — it is pending, not produced.** `validate-nightly-wiring.mjs` keeps failing on an unregistered rig throughout. | **Deleting rigs could lose coverage nobody listed.** The review step is what keeps "no journey entry cites it" from being the only argument, and pairing each deletion with the entry that would have cited it is what makes the claim checkable. | list: #927 wave 1 (pending); execution: wave 5 |
| **PS-device-rung** | **The device rung exists as a lane, and it is the only rung whose mobile numbers may become ceilings.** Ruled 2026-09-04 by [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927) W4. Three cells in [`.github/workflows/e2e.yml`](../.github/workflows/e2e.yml) at rung 5: one **low-end Android** and one **iPhone** leased from a cloud device farm, and one **Pi-class gateway** on a self-hosted runner labelled `centraid-pi`. Each is gated on its own secret or variable and is **SKIPPED, never failed**, when that is absent, while `device-rung-gate` — which always runs — writes each absent cell's evidence as `parked` against [`tests/quarantine.json`](../tests/quarantine.json), so the report shows the debt with a date rather than a silent gap. **Cost**: two leased device-hours a night at the suites' 45-minute and 25-minute ceilings, plus one always-on Pi; the two cloud cells are the only recurring third-party spend any lane in this repository incurs, which is why they are behind secrets a repository owner must choose to set rather than on by default. **The [#870](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/870) parks on `mobile-e2e-android` and `mobile-e2e-ios` are DELETED rather than moved here**: their 14-day date expires 2026-09-16 and the fix they named ([#915](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/915)'s MOBILE slice) has landed, and a park moved onto a cell that is skipped until somebody buys a farm would be a mute rather than a date. If either lane is still red on the next nightly the answer is a new park with a new date and a named fix, never the old one restored. | **No emulator number may become a ceiling.** `ci-android-emu` and `ci-ios-sim` have neither a phone's thermal envelope nor its flash, so every mobile row in [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) stays `_intended*` and gates on nothing **until the first device run** — which is a statement about hardware, not a delay tactic, and this rung is what ends it. The same argument names `pi-arm64-4c` beside `ci-linux-x64-4c-constrained`: pinning the constrained profile on a cloud x64 runner changes SQLite's durability mode and nothing about the disk underneath it. | #927 wave 4 |

## Sharing as subscription (#929)

Ruled by [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929), which re-judged the commons rail [#731](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/731) built and [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) left standing beneath the one authority plane. A share had two engines: the replica, which already moves rows to devices, and a per-grant command log with its own cursors, hash chain, replay, recovery, sweep and intent surface. The second one existed because the first was thought not to cross a gateway. It does.

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **SS-subscribe** | **A share is a replica subscription.** The audience vault subscribes to a **grant-keyed replica shape** the origin serves, over the four peer doors `/centraid/_peer/replica/{bootstrap,changes,blob,intents}` (peer protocol 2). A co-hosted audience takes the loopback route; a cross-gateway one takes the peer plane; the shape and the grammar are identical, which is the point. `share_subscription(shape_id, audience_vault_id)` is one row **on both seats** — the origin reads `cursor_seq` as the audience's acknowledgement, the audience as how far it has ingested — deliberately with no foreign key to `share_authority`, because the audience never holds the origin's answer. |
| **SS-one-writer** | **The origin is the single writer; a member's edit is a signed replica intent.** The member's vault signs a canonical envelope with its Ed25519 identity key, the origin verifies it against `share_party_vault_binding` and invokes the ordinary vault command, and the receipt names the **member**, not the owner whose credential executed it. Which commands may write into a container, and which input key carries the container id, are DECLARED (`packages/vault/src/share/container-routing.ts`); a routed-but-undeclared command is refused BY NAME. A confirmation-gated write parks and is decided from the phone. |
| **SS-re-origin** | **Steward transfer is re-origin.** The receiving vault BECOMES the origin of the container it already held, so a migrated group keeps every member and every ledger row instead of being rebuilt from a log. A departure has never been a reason to rewrite history. |
| **SS-lineage** | **Provenance is keyed by SHAPE, not by row.** A row-keyed table can name only one sender, so two grants over the same photograph left the second invisible and revoking the first purged a row the second still delivers. `share_subscription_lineage` is the many-to-many the model actually has: a row survives a purge while any other live shape claims it. It carries `origin_row_version`, which is how a member's phone knows to drop a pending write — its replica holds the origin's answered version — rather than inferring it from its own log. |
| **SS-settle-on-ack** | **A revocation settles `removed` only on the audience's cursor acknowledgement**; a share that was never delivered settles with the "nothing had been delivered" detail. Revoking also settles that shape's queued intents as `expired` with "no longer shared with you", so no pending row survives over a purged shape. |
| **SS-waiting-on** | **One "waiting on" vocabulary.** `parked` carries a structured `waitingOn` of kind `owner`, `origin` or `gateway` plus the label from the link, on both seats. The three vocabularies it replaces — `parked`, the steward label and the commons member intent — were held by [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883)'s known-duplication note, which kept nothing once the rail went. |
| **SS-delete-the-rail** | **The rail is deleted, not gated.** `share_commons_*`, `share_circle_grant`, the peer commons rail, its sweep, recovery, chain, replay and intent surfaces, `steward-label.ts` and `core_share_origin` are gone. The tables are dropped by a **DATA pass on open** (`subscription-migration.ts`), not by a schema rung — a rung cannot read rows, and their rows have to become standing answers and subscriptions first. |
| **SS-one-ceiling** | **One size ceiling per grant.** The three that had accumulated collapse to `share_delivery_config.max_size_bytes`, keyed by the authority row it serves: a ceiling is a property of how a subject is shipped, not of whether the member said yes. |

**Vocabulary.** `commons`, `steward`, `compile` and `edge-retire` are **retired**; `subscription`, `origin` and `re-origin` replace them ([glossary.md](glossary.md#sharing-the-grant-plane-subscriptions-links-and-the-peer-plane-726-731-825-929)). Two wire paths still spell the old word — `/centraid/_gateway/commons/intents/<id>/{cancel,decide}` in `packages/core/src/protocol/routes.ts` — and are left alone deliberately: renaming a path is a compatibility act, and the plane behind it is already the new one.

**The ladder is five rungs**, and this is where #929 met it: rung one is the #916 baseline and is history; #929 added rungs two through four (the subscription seat's tables, `share_delivery_config` re-cut with `departure_policy`, and the drop of `core_share_origin`); [#972](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/pull/972) added rung five so `share_authority_request` and `share_authority_use` reach files frozen at `user_version` 2. A fresh vault stamps `PRAGMA user_version = 5`. See [ONT-ladder](#ontology-v0-close-916).

## One vault, every seat (#996)

Ruled 2026-09-06 by [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996), which re-judged the replica plane [#406](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/406) / [#417](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/417) built, the mount plane [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) D1 added and the share transport [#929](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/929) landed, against the same test [#928](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/928) applied to the consent plane: **a citation is not a justification**. Per-app shapes existed to minimise a replica to an app's consent grant; #928 deleted the grant, and what the mechanism produces now is eight stored rows, eight FTS entries and eight wire payloads per shared content row on every seat. The mounted multi-vault reader was built for a household reading several people's vaults on one phone; #929 made a share a projection into the audience's own vault, so the cross-owner case has no consumer left.

**Every seat holds the vault.** One SQLite file per enrolled vault, the gateway's schema, bootstrapped by file copy and kept current by a log the gateway captures with SQLite's session extension. There are no shapes, no per-app composition, no mount plane. The gateway stays the single writer. **A subscriber is a seat with a predicate**: a device consumes the log under _not private_; an audience gateway consumes the same log under _in the closure of grant G_, with membership tracked as explicit state. **And the vault the seats replicate is an executable agreement about identity and behaviour**, closed in wave 0 before any replica engine is built on it.

These rulings land ahead of #996's code waves, so no wave is built over a guess; where a row describes a mechanism it states the ruling in force and the wave that lands it.

| Id | Current decision |
| --- | --- |
| **R1** | **Full replica, same schema.** Every seat holds `vault.db` whole — no row filters, no field masks, no app composition. _An index is not data_: B-tree indexes and FTS are built locally by whichever process owns the file. |
| **R2** | **Single writer.** The gateway is the canonical writer; a seat is a full read replica plus a durable outbox of intents. No multi-master, no CRDTs. |
| **R3** | **Private state is a table list, not a second file — and a table with replicated references is split, not dropped.** Credentials and key material, enrichment job machinery and peer-link state stay on the gateway. `access_device` and `access_agent` split: a non-secret identity projection (`device_id`, label, kind, status) stays replicated so `core_content_item.origin_device_id` and `media_asset.camera_device_id` resolve, and the key material moves to private sibling tables. On a seat `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is asserted over the replicated schema; relationships into excluded tables are asserted separately by the snapshot test. The audit and ledger bands ship whole (**OQ-1**) — `agent_command_invocation` in particular is **replicated**, because five replicated tables key into it (`access_receipt`, `agent_invocation_check`, `agent_evidence`, `agent_explanation` and `core_entity_revision.invocation_id`), so making it private would break the one property R3 exists to keep. Exclusion is per **table**, and per **column** where a table is not split (`access_device.public_key` / `sync_cursor`, `access_agent.enrollment_key`, the Locker sealed columns). |
| **R4** | **The snapshot carries the schema, and is physically sanitised.** Bootstrap is `VACUUM INTO`; in the copy, `PRAGMA secure_delete=ON`, drop every trigger except FTS sync, drop the private tables, truncate the log leaving its cursor, then a final `VACUUM` — a dropped table's bytes otherwise stay in freed pages. The snapshot test plants a private canary and asserts it absent from the file's **bytes** and from retained FTS content; reading `sqlite_schema` is not enough. A seat runs no DDL generator, no triggers except FTS sync, and `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` always: the constraints are still declared and the data still satisfies them, and what is off is re-checking — a mirror cannot reject what the writer committed, the applier writes a commit's rows in table order rather than dependency order, and the log already carries every cascaded delete as its own row. Indexes a screen needs are added to the **gateway's** DDL, never as seat-only DDL. FTS sync triggers become plain SQL on every seat: `vault_content_text` is retired by storing decoded body text in a 1:1 side table at write time (R8), because expo-sqlite cannot register a SQL function and a trigger must index a column. |
| **R5** | **Session capture, JSON everywhere, with a defined replay contract.** The gateway records each commit with `node:sqlite`'s session extension and decodes the changeset to JSON row images **inside the originating transaction** — an UPDATE changeset omits unchanged columns, so the decoder reads each touched row before commit to produce the full image. JSON carries BLOBs as base64 and 64-bit integers as strings, and distinguishes SQL `NULL` from an absent column. `replica_log(seq, schema_epoch, ddl_version, commit_id, rows_json, made_at)` commits atomically with the data; `ddl` rows carry additive migrations. Two numbers, not one: `schema_epoch` is compatibility (a mismatch is a re-bootstrap), `ddl_version` orders the additive migrations a seat applies. No compaction; a retention floor, below which a seat re-bootstraps. The applier uses `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, never `INSERT OR REPLACE` (REPLACE deletes the old row and fires delete triggers only under `recursive_triggers`, which desynchronises FTS); applies one transaction per commit **with the cursor in the same transaction**; is idempotent under duplicate delivery; and resumes or restarts an interrupted bootstrap cleanly. The epoch gate refuses a row whose `schema_epoch` is not the file's — a schema mismatch is otherwise a silent no-op. The gateway bounds producer commits: enrichment and bulk writes are chunked to at most N rows per commit, so no log row exceeds the deferral threshold and a deferred span leaves the seat consistent behind it. The decoder is one module on Node, differential-tested against `applyChangeset`. |
| **R6** | **`row_version` on every mutable table**, bumped by the existing `touch_updated_at` triggers; an intent carries the expected version and the gateway's conflict check compares against the column, not a shape-derived `MAX(seq)`. **The rows an intent's base versions must cover are declared by the domain operation, not invented per app**: an expense includes its splits and payers, a document edit its current revision, a task completion its series state, and the checker refuses an intent whose supplied set is short of the declared one rather than returning early on an empty list. The comparison is plain equality; **a member's own offline chain passes it through predecessor references the gateway resolves (R23), never through a looser check**. |
| **R7** | **Bytes.** A ~2 KB thumb per media item in a 1:1 side table, canonical and replicated. Previews and originals are content-addressed files under a per-seat policy: desktop everything, phone what it captured plus an LRU cache with a "keep offline" pin, PWA on demand. `blob_presence(sha, seat_id)` rows are written by seats as intents, with tombstones a seat acknowledges on purge. **"Backed up" means the gateway's CAS holds the sha, verified**; a seat's presence claim is never by itself the durability answer. The owner sees two states — backed up, or not — plus a cache bit. |
| **R8** | **Structure instead of lint; measured work instead of plan words.** Wide columns (bodies, decoded body text, thumbs, transcripts) live in 1:1 side tables so `SELECT *` on a hot table is harmless. The read runtime owns `LIMIT`, and pagination is **keyset** — a stable `(sort_key, pk)` cursor, never `OFFSET`. A committed `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` snapshot per handler is the derived manifest, reviewed as a diff; it is **not** the performance gate, because an outer `LIMIT` does not bound an aggregate or a sort, an efficient ordered traversal can print as `SCAN`, and plan text changes between SQLite versions. The gate is measured work at year-3 scale in the journey ledger — rows visited, sort steps, elapsed, memory — from [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927)'s work counters. [AP-apps-declare](#one-authority-plane-928) is restated: _apps' reads are their query plans_. |
| **R9** | **One handler, plain SQL, paged.** `queries/*.ts` runs on every seat and on the gateway over real tables. The read-plan compiler, census probes, deferred values, text ceilings and `replica_row` go. Screens are notified by the applier's `(table, pk)` sets per batch; nothing polls. |
| **R10** | **Sharing is the same log under a closure predicate, with membership as explicit state.** The origin keeps `share_subscription_member(authority_id, table, pk)`; at each commit touching a grant's closure it diffs the closure against that set and emits **three outputs** per subscription — **enter** (the full row image for a row newly in scope: an existing photo added to a shared album has no log entry of its own), **update** (retained rows that changed) and **leave** (a removal for a row no longer in scope, even if the row itself is unchanged, and for a deleted or purged row). `structure_digest` is superseded by the member set, not deleted without a successor. A new subscriber bootstraps from a closure snapshot (`readShareClosure` becomes the snapshot builder) and tails from the origin's seq; rows are re-keyed through lineage at ingest. **The grant _is_ the shape**: `share_subscription` and `share_subscription_lineage` are keyed by `authority_id`, and the grant-keyed shape composer is deleted **after** the predicate transport lands. **Derived rows never project** — with R20's representation split an owner's typed title is authored metadata and a generated caption is a derived row, so captions, OCR and embeddings are the receiving vault's job and no vault-private reference can leak, by construction. **Projected rows are read-only in the audience vault**: an `edit`-grant edit is an intent forwarded to the origin, committed there and returned through the subscription, and the recipient's own enrichment writes only derived rows keyed to the projected row. Devices never see a foreign vault. Sharing is a copy, not a lease: revoke scrubs the projection and ends updates; it cannot claw back. [SS-settle-on-ack](#sharing-as-subscription-929) and [SS-re-origin](#sharing-as-subscription-929) are unchanged. |
| **R11** | **Enrollment is full trust.** Owner-direct reads; the companion tier and `grant_profile_json` go; a device belongs to one person. |
| **R12** | **Multi-vault per owner.** N files on a seat, one open for reads, all syncing over the multiplexed connection. A vault is a world (Agenda's birthdays come from the People in the same vault); a vault switcher replaces vault-free views. |
| **R13** | **Locker v0, with the unlock boundary named per seat.** One random `K` per vault in the gateway's `keys/`. `secret = AES-GCM(K, value, aad = row_id ‖ key_id)` with a fresh random 96-bit nonce stored beside each ciphertext and `key_id` stored beside it as well as in the AAD; only secret values are ciphertext — title, URL and username stay plaintext so lists and search work offline without unlocking — and attachments are encrypted under `K` directly. `locker_key(key_id)` in the vault carries the id only. A seat **fetches** `K` after it is enrolled, over the authenticated key door (`GET /_vault/seat/locker-key`) — never over the pair ticket, which is a base64url payload a camera reads off a screen, is seen by whatever is pointed at that screen, survives in a photo roll, and is validated before a device exists to be a principal (amended 2026-09-11 by [#1015](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1015) R-NY-19 to the design the code has; the rest of R13 stands). **Storage is not authorization**: a non-extractable WebCrypto key stops export, not use by app code, and Electron's `safeStorage` encrypts at rest without any prompt, so the unlock boundary is explicit per seat and demonstrated before the existing gate is deleted (**OQ-10**). Reveal decrypts locally after that boundary; offline works; the reveal receipt is a device intent the gateway stamps. Revoke = rotate, in a defined order across two stores that share no transaction: write `K′` to `keys/` as a new key file, commit the re-encryption of every secret and attachment plus the `key_id` bump in one DB transaction, then delete the old key file — after a crash both files exist and the DB's `key_id` says which is live. Surviving seats see the delta and re-fetch `K′` with their device credential from one authenticated endpoint; the revoke screen lists what was readable on that device. An intent under a stale `key_id` is refused with "re-enter this secret". Share = decrypt at origin, re-encrypt under the audience's `K` at ingest. Locker is enabled on every seat, PWA included. The permit, the sealed registry and the `authenticate` gate are deleted once each seat's boundary is demonstrated ([AP-locker-boundary](#one-authority-plane-928) superseded). **The recovery kit carries every live key file — `K`, and `K′` while a rotation is in flight — and the backup snapshot never does**: long-lived key material stays out of every snapshot by construction (`packages/server/src/backup/backup-sources.ts`), so key custody rides the one passphrase-wrapped artefact the owner exports deliberately ([SECURITY.md](../SECURITY.md)). Restoring ciphertext without its key file is refused with the reason. The one honest trade: rotation protects what comes after. |
| **R14** | **expo-sqlite replaces op-sqlite** on the phone: sessions compiled in, `useSQLCipher` (phone at-rest encryption is that flag), `withSQLiteVecExtension` (on-device vector search is a later proposal; the flag is set so the build does not change again), `enableFTS`. Desktop and web at-rest file encryption is a later driver swap over the same file format, not a design item. |
| **R15** | **The PWA is a cache of the vault** and says so (Safari's OPFS ceiling). A storage-estimate probe at bootstrap chooses full or rows-minus-FTS (**OQ-2**). The seat vocabulary becomes _promises the file_ (phone, desktop) versus _caches it_ (PWA). |
| **R16** | **The gateway stays on Node** (`node:sqlite`; Bun has no session API). |
| **R17** | **The authority plane has three principal kinds across four CHECK values.** `person` and `circle` are the two audiences of a share (subject: a shareable subject; verb view/edit; enforced by the closure predicate on the origin's log); `automation` is pack or entity × read/act under the execution clamp; `harness` is an egress class under the enrichment gate. `device` **leaves** the `principal_kind` vocabulary, with `companion-surfaces.ts`, `device-trust.ts`, `device_surface_projection`, the tier words and any enrollment UI offering a companion tier; `share_fulfillment`'s five states are derived from the origin-side `share_subscription` row (cursor vs head, `removed_at`, link ended) and the table goes; `access_app` goes, its enabled bit to prefs; `share_authority_use` goes for an index over receipts unless `evidence.ts` names a property it cannot serve; `share_access_receipts` moves to the audit band or is deleted. **Row-level scoping exists in exactly one place in the system: the closure of a shared subject** — everything else is all-or-nothing. Settings → Access shows three kinds, and those are the product's three consent moments. [AP-principals, AP-attenuations and AP-companion-projection](#one-authority-plane-928) are superseded to this. |
| **R18** | **Egress answers do not travel with a share.** A shared item is enriched by the receiving vault under the **recipient's** egress answers, because a copy is the recipient's, and the share sheet says so (**OQ-6**). Seats write receipts as intents and the gateway stamps the principal from the authenticated session, never from the payload; the chain verifier runs on the gateway only. |
| **R19** | **A forced re-bootstrap loses nothing the seat owns.** Pending intents, offline captures, upload progress and offline pins survive a file replacement, including on the phone where the outbox shares the replica file. **The preserved set includes dependency edges, base references, reconciliation state and staged bytes referenced by pending intents.** The **cutover sequence**: prepare the new file while local work continues; briefly pause mutation admission and applier / upload state changes; transfer the seat-owned state; drain and close the handles; the recoverable file switch; reconstruct pending projections over the new snapshot; resume. The seat checks it has room for both files before starting, resumes an interrupted download by range, and states what it will do on prolonged cellular use and low storage rather than stalling silently. A snapshot may already contain an intent whose acknowledgement was lost: the seat resolves that intent's durable outcome before retrying it or showing its effect twice. |
| **R20** | **Identity is never taken from a value.** Five rulings of one shape. (a) **A revision is an occurrence, not a content id**: `core_entity_revision` is the one history mechanism and gains an immutable per-document / per-note revision row pointing to content and, where needed, a parent revision; the wrapper points to its current revision; a restore is a new revision even when its bytes already exist; `recordRevision`'s content→content edges and the restore walk over them are deleted; two documents with identical bytes have independent histories, A→B→A→B is valid, and restoring another object's revision is refused. Document-history retention is decided apart from the 20-snapshot capture default (**OQ-11**). (b) **Bytes are not an interpretation**: `core_content_item` is bytes (sha, size, uri); a representation row owned by the document, note or asset carries `media_type`, charset and interpretation; the authored title lives on the wrapper and a generated caption is a derived row (**OQ-9**); byte dedupe is preserved. (c) **A provider-local id is scoped to its source**: the sync map's `(connection_id, external_id)` is authoritative, the global `UNIQUE` on `core_transaction.external_id` and the publisher's global probe go, `accountFor` keys on an institution/account identifier rather than a display name, and a cross-source match is explicit evidence producing a reviewable result (**OQ-12**), never inferred from equal reference strings. (d) **A concept's label is not its identity**: a provider concept id or an opaque stable id, a Unicode-preserving normalised key with collision handling where only labels exist, the original label kept with a language tag, and the 64-character slug given collision cases. (e) **Current preference is not history**: the primary-identifier index is partial on `valid_to IS NULL` like the value index, retirement and replacement are one atomic operation, interval ordering is a CHECK, and an issuer/namespace column is added where a short handle is not globally unique. |
| **R21** | **One invariant boundary for every writer.** Domain commands, imports, Atlas and — in W9 — the seat's local apply all pass through the same domain operations, and Atlas's pre/postconditions stop being empty. Simple invariants are CHECKs and partial indexes; aggregate ones are the operations: content write is one operation over hash, size and bytes; a task hierarchy is acyclic and a section agrees with its task's project; **task completion is one operation** (`complete` / `reopen`, never a state-dependent toggle) shared by People, Tasks and automations, with a stable recurring-task series identity, occurrences identified separately, and the relationships that belong to the series (the `about` link) declared and inherited by the successor; **the recurrence occurrence key is one typed value** (series identity plus recurrence-local identity and semantics) that the core matcher takes directly and every reader consumes through one adapter, so `original_start_local` / `original_start` and `tz` / `time_zone` cannot drift again; **temporal meaning is validated at the boundary** with the core rule parser and date validators, distinguishing an instant, a local date, a floating local datetime and a yearless month-day, and an unsupported imported rule is retained with an explicit support state rather than as an executable rule. Acceptance is a matrix: the same invalid mutation through every supported writer is refused by each or transformed identically by each, Atlas included. |
| **R22** | **Contracts between storage and consumers are typed.** **Money keeps its currency**: balances are `(party, currency) → amount_minor`, group results stay in the group's currency, a base-currency overview is an explicit valuation carrying rate, effective time and source with an unavailable state when a rate is absent, and a shared Money type in the query output contract makes a bare amount unrenderable as a balance; settlements, obligations, exports and the mobile consumers use the same helpers. **Deletion is declared by relationship role** — owned child, attribution, participation, durable record — beside each reference, with the purge behaviour tested per role; the FK census by referenced table becomes a census by role, and a parent-owned projection is told apart from rebuildable derived data. **Machine assertions link to their evidence**: a machine tag and a document classification carry a link to the `enrich_derivation` and input revision that produced them, competing confidences are representable, and the preferred assertion is derived explicitly — machine tags and classification only, no universal claim engine. **A storage ruling is enforced by a reader test**: every shared concept has at least one test that writes through a real command and reads through a real app query across two app surfaces, and the drift register gains the category [_reader-side drift_](vault-ontology.md#drift-register). |
| **R23** | **Offline work is durable, visible and a causal chain.** _Durable before "saved"_: every supported mutation gets a stable intent id; an object created offline gets a stable id later intents reference; the intent, its dependency edges, its base references and what its pending projection needs are persisted before the write reports saved on this seat; projections are reconstructed from the outbox after restart, never held only in component state; the replica tables hold confirmed state only, pending changes are applied when the visible result is composed, and list, detail, filter, ordering and aggregate views agree on them — today's contract in [mobile-offline.md](mobile-offline.md), restated so the cutover cannot lose it. _Ordering_: intents drain one at a time in outbox order and a transport failure holds the head ([#880](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/880), kept); local creation order renders pending rows deterministically and device timestamps never decide canonical order. _Dependency_: intent B depends on A when B's input names a row id A's projection minted and A has not executed — the seat **derives** the edges from the minted ids the outbox already recognises, never hand-declared by an app and never inferred from a value (R20), and carries them on the wire as intent ids. _Base references_: each row in the operation's declared read-set (R6) is referenced either as a canonical `row_version` the seat observed or as _the version intent I_k produced for this row_; the gateway executes an intent only after the intents it names have executed, resolves predecessor references from their durable outcomes (the outcome table, never a walk over `replica_log`) within the idempotency window (R24), then compares by plain equality, and never substitutes the latest version. So a member's successive offline edits do not conflict because an earlier one advanced the version, an intervening edit from another device still conflicts, a failed or conflicting prerequisite parks its dependents with its reason, and unrelated intents continue. _Coalescing is declaration-only_: the `revisions` pairs an app declares merge into one unsent intent, nothing undeclared is combined, and later coalescing is operation-specific, limited to unsent intents and preserves dependencies and effects. **Rejected**: seat-side substitution of predecessors' versions before send (three outboxes each carrying a rebase the gateway cannot tell from an observed version); a gateway walk over `replica_log` (retention); rebasing to latest (hides the real conflict). |
| **R24** | **Settlement is idempotent and tied to replica progress.** Every executed outcome is durable at the gateway and carries the canonical commit position (`commit_seq`) and the `(table, pk, row_version)` set its commit produced, on every path. A retry reuses the intent id and an identical payload and receives the existing outcome without re-execution; a different payload under the same id is refused; dependency resolution and the conflict check run inside the canonical execution boundary. Transport state and reconciliation state are separate: `executed` with the commit still arriving is `awaiting-change`. The seat keeps the pending projection until its applied cursor reaches `commit_seq` — where the outbox and the replica share a file, clearing the projection and recording the reconciliation commit in the transaction that advances the cursor, with notifications firing after that commit — so acknowledgement-before-delta and delta-before-acknowledgement both converge with no flicker and no doubled effect. Absence reconciles too: a rejected offline creation leaves no pending row, an accepted deletion leaves no stale row. The **idempotency window** is stated in the protocol (**OQ-13**). |
| **R25** | **Offline failure preserves the work, and every operation declares its offline contract.** A transport failure leaves the intent retryable; a conflict keeps the attempted change and both versions for edit / retry / discard; denied and failed are shown apart from pending; dependents park with the prerequisite's reason; resolving a conflict mints a new intent against a newly observed base and never rewrites a submitted payload; dismissing or replacing a pending operation handles its descendants explicitly — they settle `expired` naming it. Each domain operation declares whether it supports offline submission, its pending behaviour, its conflict scope (the R6 read-set) and its byte or connectivity prerequisites; apps invent none of this. An attachment-dependent intent names the content hashes it needs, the gateway executes it only once those bytes are uploaded and verified, and the seat protects a capture referenced by pending work from cache eviction (R7's LRU cannot evict it). An action that needs fresh gateway authority — Locker's online-only set — is explicitly **unavailable** offline, not queued. |

**An index with no reader is a superseded store too.** Ruled 2026-09-07 on the same pre-1.0 stance: `share_subscription_member_row` was created for one function, `shareGrantsClaimingRow`, and that function turned out to answer a question nothing asks — the leave a purged member owes each audience is that grant's own `before ∖ after`, computed per grant by `diffShareClosure` (R10). Both are **deleted from the baseline DDL** and the golden corpus is **re-frozen in the same commit**, so `golden-vault.test.ts` proves the new baseline rather than the old. There is no migration rung for it and no compatibility path: pre-1.0 there are none, and a rung spent carrying a dead index forward is a rung spent making the wrong thing survive.

**Two of the five code enumerations came back with opposite answers.** Ruled 2026-09-07 in wave 8, by reading the consumers rather than the intent. **OQ-7 — `vault_links.permissions_json` goes.** Nothing consumed a permission out of it; the one key ever read was `commonsPartyIds`, a `vaultId → partyId` map, which is link identity and not authority — and since [#903](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/903) a link is a channel, not a permission slip. The column is `party_ids_json`, typed `Record<string, string>` and narrowed at the parse, because the far side's hello used to be spread into it verbatim. **OQ-8 — `share_authority_use` stays.** R17's escape clause fires, though not where it was expected: `writeAuthorityReceipt` stamps the use row from the same input as a receipt bearing the same `authority_id`, and `idx_receipt_authority(authority_id, occurred_at)` already exists, so for a live receipt the index is exactly as good. The property is the receipt's **lifetime** — the audit band is retained 365 days, its `journal-archive` duty deletes the rows it seals out of `access_receipt`, and the band rides no canonical portable walk, while the use row has no history to age and is a registered `share.*` entity. "Granted a year ago and nothing has used it since" is the fact that makes a stale answer visible on Settings → Access, and it is precisely the case an index over receipts answers "never used". R17 is amended to that extent; the rest of its diet is unchanged.

**v0 stance**, unchanged from [#883](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/883) and [#928](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/928): a superseded store is deleted in the wave that replaces it, never carried beside its replacement. Wave 0's schema changes and wave 1's land under **one `schema_epoch` bump**, taken when W1 merges; each later store cutover is one bump and one forced re-bootstrap; there is no compatibility shim for the shipped client. **Device evidence precedes irreversible deletion**: no store is deleted before the phone's measured rows exist.

**There is no seat-store flag** (W4-D1, ruled 2026-09-07 in wave 4). `seat/flag.ts`, `?seatStore=1` and the remembered `centraid.seatStore` preference are **deleted**, and the seat store is unconditional on web and desktop. The flag existed so a member's browser could run either store while both existed; the owner opened wave 5 — the old store's deletion — while wave 4 was in flight, so one of the switch's two positions is being removed and a switch with one position is not a switch. The consequence is ruled with it: **there is no flag-OFF path to serve**, so the gateway does not grow a second paged-handler host for a store that the next wave deletes (this narrows review sweep F2 further — `gateway.read` keeps its window for automations and the server's own callers, and gains nothing for apps). The old store's own files stay until wave 5 removes them, unreachable in the meantime.

**An app handler's statement never runs on the gateway as raw SQL** (W4-D2, ruled 2026-09-07 in wave 4). On a seat that holds the vault file, a handler's statement-as-data runs on the seat. For a seat that holds no file — the browser that turned "Keep an offline copy" off, which R9 allows — the gateway serves the **same** statement through the **paged door** (`packages/vault/src/gateway/paged-door.ts`, `Gateway.page`, the `page` op on `bridgeFor`), which executes it under the caller's own principal with `evaluateAccess`, the R17 field mask and the manifest row filters applied, never bypassed. The door parses the statement against a small grammar rather than escaping it: to apply a field mask it has to know which columns are projected, and to apply a row filter it has to know which tables are read, and a string of SQL hides both — so a subquery, a second statement, a comment, an unlisted function, an unresolvable table or a sealed column is **refused**, not repaired. `ctx.vault.page` picks the seat or the door by whether the session holds the file; `ctx.vault.read` and the declarative read vocabulary die with their last caller. Locker reads are seat reads like every other app's — the seat holds the vault whole, and the permit tier is deleted. This supersedes W4-D1's "the gateway does not grow a second paged-handler host": W4-D1 was right that a host for the **flag-OFF store** was throwaway work, and wrong that no host was needed at all — R9's remote-only seat is not a flag position, it is a member's standing choice.

**A shelf is a filter, and an item created inside it belongs to it** (W4-D3, ruled 2026-09-07 in wave 4). A task captured on the **Today** shelf is stamped **due today**, whether or not the member touched a When chip; a task captured anywhere else is undated, and an **undated task is an Inbox task** (`inboxGroup`: open and unfiled) that Today never draws. The rule lives in the app — `quickAddInput`/`shelfDue` in `packages/blueprints/apps/tasks/quick-add.ts` — so the web capture and the phone's answer it once, and the raw write rail (`window.centraid.write`) stays what it is: a rail with no shelf, whose undated task lands in the Inbox. This answers `apps/web/tests/e2e/tasks.spec.ts:325`, which had been asserting page-wide visibility for a task added with no date from the Today shelf: the spec now captures through the shelf's own overlay, so it asserts the ruled behaviour rather than the absence of it. Rejected alternatives: an "Added just now" group on Today (a shelf that shows what it does not contain), and requiring a date before the add can fire (capture that argues with the member).

**Search runs on the seat, not the gateway** (W5-D1, ruled 2026-09-08). Wave 2 kept the FTS sync triggers and rebuilds the index after the bootstrap copy, so a seat's file already carries the vault's shadow tables; local search is therefore a statement-as-data page over them (`packages/client/src/replica/seat/search-page.ts`), not a round trip. The ruling also fixed the ORDER of the cut: the remaining declarative call sites converted to pages **before** the old plane was deleted, in their own commits, so the deletion itself stayed one atomic commit with nothing half-converted behind it.

**The §293 sealed-column class survives the Locker gate's deletion** (W6-D1, ruled 2026-09-07). R13 supersedes the Locker _gate_, not the column class. What goes is what the vault key `K` replaced — permits, the `authenticate` op, the permit screens. What stays is `SEALED_COLUMNS` and the machinery around it, because `sync.connection_credential`'s broker-token columns, the ext band's per-app declared `sealed` lists, and the journal redaction and error-text scrub each depend on it and none of them is a Locker reveal. Deleting the class to satisfy a scope line would have turned a gateway that must inject OAuth tokens into a gateway that stores them in the clear. The Locker entries stay in the registry for the redaction half: `key_id` and the ciphertext must still be hash-not-value in the append-only journal.

**Blueprint code never holds `K`; the unlock boundary is a shell door** (W6-D2, ruled 2026-09-07). R13 puts the unseal on the seat; this says which part of the seat. The shell holds `K` behind the member's unlock and an app gets **the plaintext of one row per receipt**, never the key — because one `fetch` in a blueprint would put a vault key on someone else's server with no receipt recording it, since nothing was revealed. The door is `window.centraid.locker` on the kit surface, owned by `packages/client`: `reveal({ rowId })`, which writes the reveal receipt through the same path the gateway's door used, plus `state()` and `subscribeLock()` so screens render the shell's Lock surface instead of drawing their own. There is **no seal door and no `unlock()`** — writes stay intents carrying the secret, and a locked `reveal` returns a typed refusal rather than prompting, because a door that can raise the passphrase prompt is a door that can be used to phish it. `gateway.reveal` survives for the sealed-column class W6-D1 keeps and refuses the `locker` schema outright, for every principal including the owner on their own device.

**The Companion fills through that door and never holds `K`** (W6-D3, ruled 2026-09-07). The browser extension is a client of the desktop shell: browser fill is one row's plaintext per fill through `window.centraid.locker.reveal`, its receipt awaited, with a typed `locked` refusal when the seat's unlock boundary has not been crossed. It holds no key and no permit. **The wiring is a named seam, not built**: the door is a client surface inside the shell's process and the Companion reaches the gateway over the peer transport, so connecting them needs a shell-side listener the product does not have — native messaging or a localhost bridge — plus its origin binding and its gesture proof. On a seat with no shell, `autofill-item.ts` returns the match plus the stated reason.

**W5 opened before the emulator gate measured the phone** (owner ruling, 2026-09-07). The four `mobile/*` journey rows were opened on Linux-measured numbers plus a linking Android release build with Maestro running; those stand as v0 evidence, and the rows stay **open ledger rows with provenance `emulator`** rather than being closed on a projection. Recorded here because it is the substitute evidence a slice ran on, not a measurement it satisfied.

### Questions settled with the rulings (#996)

Eight of the issue's thirteen open questions are answered here as dated decisions; the other five (the big-batch threshold, desktop reads, Node 24's bundled SQLite, `vault_links.permissions_json`, `share_authority_use`) are measurements or code enumerations and are answered by the wave that makes them, not by a ruling — the last two by wave 8, above.

| Id | Question | Ruling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **OQ-1** | Do the audit band and the ledger band (the assistant's `conversation ⊃ turn ⊃ item` transcript) ship to every seat? | **Yes.** They are the owner's own history; only credential and gateway-machinery tables are private, and the audit band's `agent_command_invocation` could not be private in any case — five replicated tables key into it. Realized by R3's private-table list, which is the one place the answer is written, table by table: a band-level "ships" is the default, and any table whose contents no schema constrains is ruled there on its own. |
| **OQ-2** | PWA contents: rows minus FTS, or rows plus FTS under Safari's OPFS ceiling? | **Rows minus FTS on Safari, full on Chromium**, decided by a storage-estimate probe at bootstrap rather than by a hard-coded browser check (R15). Search on a Safari PWA is online. |
| **OQ-6** | Does an egress answer travel with a share? | **No** (R18). A copy is the recipient's, so the recipient's egress answers govern it; the share sheet says so. The alternative — one flag on projected items honoured by the recipient's enrich gate — is **rejected for v0**: it would make one vault's answer enforceable only by another vault's good behaviour. |
| **OQ-9** | Where do generated captions live? | **A derived row keyed to the representation** (R20(b)), never in the owner-authored title column. The owner **may promote a caption to the title with one action**; the promotion writes the wrapper's authored title, and the derived row stays what it was. |
| **OQ-10** | Desktop and PWA unlock. | **A local PIN or passphrase that wraps `K` on that seat** for v0, with WebAuthn user verification used where the platform can gate the key, plus a session lock with a timeout (R13). Each seat's boundary is **demonstrated** before the permit and the `authenticate` gate are deleted. |
| **OQ-11** | Document-history retention. | **Unbounded for authored edits**, with a size-based prune the owner triggers — separate from the 20-snapshot capture default and from the undo window (R20(a)). |
| **OQ-12** | Cross-source transaction matching. | **A proposed-match row the owner accepts, never automatic** (R20(c)). Equal reference strings across two sources are evidence for a proposal, not an identity. |
| **OQ-13** | The idempotency window. | Outcomes are retained **not shorter than the log's retention floor, and never pruned while a device's cursor is behind them**. **Recovery**: an unknown or expired outcome for a `sending` intent **parks** it with that reason and the member sees the attempted change and decides — never a blind re-execution of a payment, a recurring-task completion or any other consequential operation (R24). The window's number lands in [protocol.md](protocol.md) at close. |

## Governance as a constitution (#1005)

Ruled 2026-09-09 by [#1005](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1005). The governance kit answered "is this file well-formed?" one shell script at a time; the constitution answers "is this _change_ lawful?" with a rule catalog, and says which half a machine settles and which half the owner does.
