The rules under [`.governance/law/`](../.governance/law/README.md) observe; the host — GitHub branch protection and the reviewers [`.github/CODEOWNERS`](../.github/CODEOWNERS) names — enforces. Nothing an agent can edit is the authority for what an agent may do.

| Id | Current decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **G-ledgers-retired** | `COSTS.md` and `STEERING.md` are deleted, with their `doc-integrity` overlay rows, their `oxfmt` and `format-check` exclusions and their `install.yaml` seeds. Token cost per arrival is a front-page number read back from the touched receipt's `## Accounting` section; when the author recorded none the front page prints "token cost: not recorded". | Both were append-only ledgers that nothing enforced. They stopped at #238 and #240 while the work reached #1003, and no gate, review or agent noticed for seven hundred issues — a register nobody is required to write is a register that records the beginning of a habit and nothing after it. Keeping them would have meant adding a directive to enforce two files whose content no consumer reads; retiring them and making the one number that mattered _generated_ is the smaller, honest answer. |
| **R-1005-11** | The door selects which rules run; the pack row sets severity. `error` blocks, `warn` is a front-page finding, `off` repeals a rule without deleting it. | Porting a blocking shell directive into a warning would be weakening policy without editing it, and a repeal that deletes the rule loses the record that it existed. |
| **R-1005-12** | Every module under `.governance/law/lib/` and `arrival.mjs` is under the managed-tree digest; `digest.mjs --record` re-records exactly those. | A rule is only as trustworthy as the generator that wrote the record it reads, and splitting a file must not be a way to move half the generator out from under its own digest. |
| **R-1005-13** | Three estates: `law` (the union of every pack's `lawPaths`), `registry` (`receipts/**`, `CHANGELOG.md`, this file, `QUALITY.md`, `.governance/law/docket.json`) and `territory` (everything else). **Amended 2026-09-09 (R-1005-19):** the registry estate is `docs/**` whole, not `docs/decisions.md` alone — a doc records what is and never changes what the next change may do, so filing the tree as territory made every rule-with-its-documentation commit an estate-separation finding and taught the honest shape to look like the violation. The generator tags every changed file with its estate and fills `gates` with the tighten-only ledgers a change moved and which way. | A constitution that cannot say what it governs governs nothing in particular. `registry` is tested first because the docket also matches the law glob `.governance/**` and a register of exceptions is evidence, not a rule. |
| **R-1005-14** | `estate-separation` at the hook door, pack severity `warn`. The hook door is fatal for every rule it runs; the window door reports at the declared severity — the catalog's one asymmetry. At the hook only the staged set is judged; in the window every commit of the range is, and so is the aggregate. | At the hook the author still holds the change, so a rule that cannot refuse is a rule for nothing — and a finding about a commit already made would block a commit no edit to it could fix. In the window a red gate must not stand in for the owner review that would actually enforce this: branch protection over the law estate is owner-enabled and **not confirmed enabled**. |
| **R-1005-15** | `registry-completeness` at the window door, severity `error`, entirely event-driven: a changelog line when a change lands, a line here when a touched receipt records a ruling, a ruling or a changed `approvedDeviation` when a gate widens, a docket row when a waiver is spent. That a change carries a receipt at all stays `receipt-per-issue`'s question. | Its host backing is real — `governance` is a required check on the default branch ([The PR gate loop](#the-pr-gate-loop-892)) — which is why it is `error` where `estate-separation` is `warn`. Event-driven is the whole design: a rule demanding a changelog line from every commit is a rule people satisfy with an empty bullet. |
| **R-1005-16** | `.github/CODEOWNERS` is generated from the union of pack `lawPaths` by `node .governance/law/codeowners.mjs --write`; `--check` exits 1 on drift and a test pins the generation. | The file did not exist, so nothing the host reads named a reviewer for the rules. Generating it closes the gap where a law path is added to a pack and quietly owned by nobody. It does **not** turn branch protection on and cannot check that it is on. |
| **R-1005-17** | The repeal above, landed in one commit with the two `doc-integrity` waivers in its body and this row as the reason. The 2026-06-12 Evolution Log line naming both files stays verbatim — it is frozen history, and the deletion is recorded by a new line, not by editing the old one. | Documentation describes current state and history is cited, never rewritten. A repeal that edited the log entry that created the thing would erase the evidence the repeal is answering. |
| **R-1005-18** | #1002's squash range is replayed against the catalog and the findings are pinned in `.governance/law/fixtures/replay/1002.json`. | A rule catalog that has never been pointed at a real, already-merged change is a catalog of opinions. The replay is what makes "this would have caught it" a fact rather than a claim. |
| **R-1005-19** | `docs/**` is the **registry** estate, not territory. | A doc records what is; none of them changes what the next change may do, which is the one thing that makes a path law. Filing the tree as territory made every rule-lands-with-its-documentation commit an `estate-separation` finding and taught the honest shape to look like the violation. Amends R-1005-13 above. |
| **R-1005-20** | `.governance/law/docket.json` is the register of standing exceptions — id, rule, path, reason, authority, who filed it, issue, expiry — and `waiver-docket` holds every waiver against it: a matching row, already on the trunk at the merge-base, whose authority is not `pending owner grant`. | A law with no escape is a law people route around with `--no-verify`, so every rule can be waived; what is not deliberate is a waiver nobody revisits. The self-granting clause is the load-bearing half: an agent can write any row it likes, but it cannot put that row on the trunk before the change that needs it. This umbrella's own waivers therefore report as self-granted, which is the correct output. |
| **R-1005-21** | `constitution-coverage`: every principle ends with `— rule: <id>`, `— decision: <anchor>` or `— owner question: <anchor>`; every `### <id>` under `## Directives` names something in the catalog, and everything in the catalog has a section. | **No unwritten law.** An agent is held only to what was published before it acted, so a principle nobody can resolve is unenforceable in the only sense that matters. The reverse held too: four enforced directives (`format-check`, `lint-check`, `pre-push-gate`, `law`) had no section at all while the document said a directive without an enforcing test is a wish. What cannot be made a statute becomes a question — Q-1005-1..3 below — never a silence. |
| **R-1005-22** | `doctrine-citation`: packs declare doctrine domains (`{id, paths, decision}`); a change touching one cites that decision or its issue in a touched receipt or a commit body, and a ruling recorded in a touched receipt with nothing cited in its paragraph is a finding. Window door, `warn`. | The expensive failures here are not changes that broke something; they are changes made in a settled area by somebody who did not know it was settled. The ruling half is sharper: [#1002](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1002) merged a receipt carrying **W6-D1** and **W6-D2** with nothing behind either — rules invented in a document nobody would look at for rules. `warn`, because whether a citation is APT is a reading and a red gate must not stand in for one. |
| **R-1005-23** | `amendment-pairing`: a rule changed without its cases, a rule added with no pack row, or a severity/door moved without a `CONSTITUTION.md` change in the same arrival is refused at the hook door. | The cardinal rule made mechanical. The document opened for four months with "amendments must land in the same commit as the change to their enforcing test, no exceptions" and nothing checked it; a cardinal rule enforced by attention is enforced by nobody. Severity and door **are** the policy ([R-1005-11](#governance-as-a-constitution-1005)), so moving one alone is a repeal nobody announced. The prose callout is retired into a citation of the rule. |
| **R-1005-24** | `node .governance/law/brief.mjs` generates the doctrine digest a worker brief carries; `run.mjs --brief-digest <hex>` (or `GOVERNANCE_BRIEF_DIGEST`) reports what moved in the law since the brief was stamped. The base→HEAD "law changed" line is capped at twelve paths. | A digest typed by hand is wrong the first time somebody amends the law, and an agent held to a rule that was not in its brief is being held to unwritten law. The agent is still held to HEAD — the stamp does not excuse anything, it names the gap. A stamp no commit in the range carries reports as "unknown commit" rather than as "nothing changed". The cap is because the line printed sixty-odd paths and an unread line is the same as no line. |
| **R-1005-25** | Every row in [`scripts/ci/gate-classes.json`](../scripts/ci/gate-classes.json) carries a `door` in the rules' vocabulary — `hook` at rungs 0–1, `window` at rung 2, `owner` above — asserted against its rung by `gate-classes.test.mjs`, and `readGateDoors()` refuses a value outside it. `governance` and `governance:law:test` are classified. | "Where is this answerable, and who can act on it" is one question whether the answer is a lint rule or a test suite, and two vocabularies for it is how a gate ends up enforced somewhere nobody looks. [#576](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/576)'s cost arithmetic stays a tiebreaker WITHIN a door and is never a reason to move one; [dev-environment.md](dev-environment.md#the-local-gate-loop) says so. `governance:law:test` is rung 1 because it rides `scripts:test`, which `check:push` names and `ci.yml` re-runs — no new CI wiring was invented for it. |
| **R-1005-26** | The #1002 replay is extended: `estate-separation`, `registry-completeness`, `doctrine-citation` (both uncited rulings, by receipt line) and `waiver-docket` (all seven waivers it spent against a register that did not exist). | A rule catalog that has only ever seen its own fixtures is a catalog of opinions. Four rules failing on a change that already merged is what makes "this would have caught it" a fact. |
| **R-1005-27** | The arrival record is a function of exactly two inputs: the range `(base, head)`, and `pending` when a commit is being written. A `--range` run reads nothing from the working tree or the current branch — the receipt corpus, `registries.docket` and `managedTree` are read at `head`, and at the hook door all of them are read from the **index**, so the commit being written is judged on what it stages. `registries.receipts.change.branch` is removed; `change.completed` derives from `range.hasBase`, or from `range.onDefaultBranch` (a `git symbolic-ref` taken only for a pending run) when a commit is in flight. | A fixture that pins the checkout is green on one branch and red everywhere else — a passing gate that enforces nothing. The record claimed to be deterministic from a git range and was not, and the four regenerations that claim had already cost were the evidence. Reading the corpus at `head` rather than narrowing it to the range keeps the one thing `receipt-per-issue` inherited from the shell directive: uniqueness across every receipt, not just the ones a change touched. |

### Open questions for the owner

Recorded by [#1005](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1005) lane D. `constitution-coverage` requires every principle to resolve to a rule id or a decision anchor; a principle that can be made neither is recorded here as a question and cited, because an agent is held only to what was published before it acted and silently dropping a principle is how a doctrine stops applying without anyone deciding that it should.

| Id | The question | Why it is open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Q-1005-1** | The docs doctrine — "docs are load-bearing, stale docs and broken internal links are bugs" and "documentation describes current state; history is cited, never narrated" — has no statute. Should it get a rule (a link checker plus a narration tripwire over `docs/**`), a decisions row of its own, or stay a reviewer-judged principle? | Both principles were adopted as principles on purpose: [#767](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/767)'s Evolution Log entry defers "a narration tripwire" as a future warn-only sweep lane, and the link half lost its enforcing directive when the vendored `governance-kit/docs` pack went. Neither is `grep`-decidable in the form written, and the honest options — a real rule, a narrower rule, or an acknowledged reviewer-only principle — differ in cost by an order of magnitude. |
| **Q-1005-2** | The performance principles — "every user-facing interaction has a perceived-latency budget" and "nothing whose cost scales with vault size runs synchronously on the request path or the event loop" — have no statute and no decisions section of their own. Should [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) become their statute, so an interaction with no ledger entry is a finding? | [#659](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/659)'s own Evolution Log line says the failures are shape-level and not `grep`-decidable, and points at [docs/coding-standards.md](coding-standards.md) for the doctrine. Since then [#927](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/927) built the journey ledger keyed `surface / journey / volume / hardware` ([Perf and scale infrastructure](#perf-and-scale-infrastructure-927)), which is the first artefact that could carry the budget half mechanically. Whether every user-facing interaction must own an entry is a scope call with a real cost, and it is the owner's. |
| **Q-1005-3** | `estate-separation`, `doctrine-citation` and `waiver-docket` are declared `warn` because their host backing — branch protection over the `law` estate and the reviewers `.github/CODEOWNERS` names — is owner-enabled and unconfirmed. Are those settings on? | The repository cannot assert its own host configuration, and a rule that claimed its own enforcement would be the exact failure `COSTS.md` was. If branch protection and code-owner review are enabled, the three rows move to `error` in one commit with this file; if they are not, the `warn` is the honest severity and should stay. |

### Deliberate non-goals

- **The law does not re-adjudicate the ledger validator.** `gates` reports that a knob moved and which way; whether the move is allowed is [`bun run lint:ledgers`](../scripts/check-ledgers.mjs)'s answer, read from the one direction table both share. Two answers to the same question is how the quieter one goes stale.
- **The law does not claim the host is configured.** Every doc says branch protection is owner-enabled and unconfirmed. A rule that asserted its own enforcement would be the exact failure `COSTS.md` was.

## Replication, offline and sharing (#1014)

Ruled 2026-09-10 by [#1014](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1014), the umbrella over an adversarial run against the shipped product (thirty findings) and a full read of the engine beneath it (152 more). Twelve questions had to be answered before the first wave could start, because every one of them decides what a later wave is allowed to assume: which log is the log, what a refused write looks like on a second phone, what a seat's identity is, and what a revoked device does with the writes it never sent.

**These rulings are provisional.** Each row adopts the recommendation [#1014](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1014) argues for; the owner was not available in the run that recorded them, and the work proceeds on them so that no wave is built over a guess. Confirming or reversing them is [**Q-1014-1**](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014) below, and it is a release blocker rather than a follow-up. A ruling in force whose code has not moved yet names the wave that lands it — until that wave merges the code still reads the old way, and this section says so.

| Id | Current decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **R-1014-1** (OQ1, G7) — **landed** | **One log.** `replica_change` is retired **as a transport**: the SSE change feed and the OCC candidate lookup both read `replica_log`, `replica_meta.floor_seq` has exactly one owner (`pruneReplicaLog`), and the trigger log with its per-entity triggers is deleted. `v0-no-legacy` applies — seats re-bootstrap once at the cutover (rung ten, `epoch_reason = 'one-log'`) rather than the log being migrated across. | Two capture mechanisms over the same tables, with two retention floors and no relationship between them, is how a prune moves one cursor past a row the other side has not applied. The feed needs only `(table, pk, seq)`, which the session-captured log already carries, so keeping the trigger log buys a second floor to reason about and nothing else. [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) already ruled the trigger log replaced; it was never removed, and both logs are live in the tree — the ruling is restated here with the wave that executes it. |
| **R-1014-2** (OQ2, R26, re-judging [#922](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/922)'s G5) | **A refused intent keeps its overlay and the row is marked refused, on every surface that draws it.** `conflict`, `denied`, `failed` and `expired` stay in `OVERLAY_STATES`, and no surface may draw the overlaid value without the refusal mark; the pending-changes sheet is surfaced from Home (R11). Two phones may show different text **only** while one of them shows a visible "not accepted" mark. Lands with waves C and G. | The write is still the member's to act on, so dropping it silently would lose their words — that half of #922's ruling survives re-judgement. What did not survive is the composition: with R11 (the sheet unreachable) and R1 (nothing ever leaves `awaiting-change`), the product-level result is a household where two phones disagree about a caption forever and nothing on either screen separates a value the gateway took from one it refused. The mark is the missing half, not a reversal. |
| **R-1014-3** (OQ3, R14) | **Member-facing screens name no gateway host, transport or sync-engine state.** Those words belong to Settings, Devices, Backup health and Onboarding, and nowhere else. The lightbox pill is action-only and the Home lockup drops the gateway name. Lands in wave G. | A member owns a vault, not a fleet. Naming the host on a photo screen asks them to hold an operational model to read their own library, and it puts a machine name where a failure would need one — which is what Settings and Backup health are for. |
| **R-1014-4** (OQ4, M3) | **A guest vault gets a second device through the guest's own phone.** Mobile Settings gains "Add another phone", which mints a pairing ticket for the vaults **this device** holds. The `owner_only` refusal at the landlord door stays exactly as it is. Lands in wave G. | The ownership rule is right and re-judged as right: a landlord minting a ticket for someone else's vault is the thing `owner_only` exists to refuse. The defect is that the rule had no door behind it — the only surface a guest could reach offered "Pair another desktop" — so the correct rule read to the member as "you cannot". |
| **R-1014-5** (OQ5, B10) | **Derived data is not replicated data**, in scope for wave F: `enrich_embedding`, `enrich_derivation` and `enrich_request` leave the replicated set. **Per-commit log coalescing is its own proposal**, not part of this umbrella. | ~19 `replica_log` rows per title edit, plus every embedding on every seat, is two problems with two different answers. Removing rederivable bytes from the replicated set is a scope call this umbrella can make and test; coalescing a commit's rows into one is a change to the log's own semantics, which every wave here builds on, and it should not ride under a bug umbrella. |
| **R-1014-6** (OQ6, G8) | **The wiring gate is a law rule.** An exported prune, rotate, report or checkpoint mechanism in `packages/vault/src/replica`, `packages/client/src/replica`, `packages/server/src/serve/enrollment-store.ts` or `apps/mobile/src/lib/replica` with zero non-test importers is a finding. Each pending item is a docket row naming the wave that wires or deletes it, and the docket is empty at the umbrella's close. Lands in wave 0d. | Nothing ships as "bounded by X" or "pruned after Y" unless X or Y has a production caller with a test that exercises the caller. A per-mechanism caller-test convention only holds for the mechanisms someone remembered to enrol; a rule over exports catches the next one, and the docket keeps a deliberate gap visible instead of turning it into a TODO. |
| **R-1014-7** (OQ7, X2) | **The seat file is keyed with the Locker key door**, as [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R13 designed, and **revoking a device rotates the key**. The decision and its wiring are in scope; a new key-management design — threshold custody, a second factor — is out and is a follow-up proposal. Until X4 lands, V13's recall gap is a **recorded** drift rather than a silent one. Lands in wave S. | The seat holds the whole vault, so "OS data protection is the posture" would mean the vault's confidentiality is whatever the platform default happens to be on the device the member actually owns — and SECURITY.md does not say that. Keying it is the design already ruled; the honest part is admitting that recall of a key already handed out is not solved by this umbrella, and recording that gap where a reader will find it. |
| **R-1014-8** (OQ8, X3, G14) | **For v0 the audit and ledger bands stay on the seat, and SECURITY.md is corrected to say so.** `access_receipt.detail_json` and command outputs are **stripped from what replicates**, now. Moving the bands off the seat is a later proposal. Lands in wave S. | [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) OQ-1 already ruled that both bands ship — they are the owner's own history, and on-phone conversation history breaks without the ledger band. What is wrong is not the ruling but the document, which claims a narrower seat than the code builds. Correct the doc, and remove the one thing in those bands that is a real leak: receipt detail and command output, which no seat surface reads. |
| **R-1014-9** (OQ9, B9) | **The no-backfill class is declared on the recipe, never inferred.** Per-occurrence automations — `reminders`, `renewal-reminders`, `materialize-recurring-expense`, and any recipe whose manifest declares `backfill: "each"` — run once per missed occurrence after an outage; metric and digest recipes keep no-backfill. Lands in wave F. | The two classes fail in opposite directions: a missed reminder that never fires is the member's forgotten obligation, and a missed metric that fires forty times is forty rows of noise about a window that has already passed. Inferring the class from the recipe's shape would make the answer change when someone edits a handler; a manifest field is the recipe author saying which failure they are choosing. |
| **R-1014-10** (OQ10, S2) | **A link between two vaults on one gateway takes a local path.** `pullShape` and `forwardProjectedEdit` short-circuit through the gateway's own vault handles when `peerForVault` is null and both vaults are local. Same-gateway links are **not** modelled as something other than a link. Lands in wave D. | The relationship is a link whichever host the other vault sits on; giving the same-gateway case its own concept would put two vocabularies over one thing and leave the peer path untested for the configuration most households actually run. A null peer is a transport fact, and the transport is the only thing that short-circuits. |
| **R-1014-11** (R25, G9) | **Seat identity is `(gatewayId, vaultId)`, with no `manual` fallback.** A mount either resolves its gateway id before it opens a file, or it refuses. A snapshot carries its vault id and the seat refuses a mismatch **before** touching the destination. Lands in wave 0c. | Identity by mutable value is what let a Personal seat end up holding Family's epoch with its `seat_state` and `seat_outbox` gone, `integrity_check` still reporting ok, and a 135 KB snapshot fetch looping every six seconds against a vault the member had removed. A fallback that names a path and moves the file later is the same bug with a friendlier name: an identity that can be guessed is one the recovery paths will guess wrong. |
| **R-1014-13** (R-1014-1's design question) — **landed** | **The log carries an update's prior image as a DELTA, not a second image.** `replica_log.prior_json` holds the OLD values of the columns a statement changed; the prior image the feed decides filtered-shape membership from is `row_json` overlaid with it. An insert has no prior and a delete's `row_json` IS its prior, so the column is set on updates alone. A gateway-private table that needs a doorbell position gets a key-only `local` row in the same log rather than a second table. | The feed's consumers need, per row, `(table, pk, seq, op)` and whether the row ENTERED or LEFT the client's shape; only the last needs history. The two candidate designs were (a) capture a whole prior image for updates and (b) reconstruct membership from the previous log row for the same key. (b) was rejected: a row updated for the first time since the floor has no previous log row, so the answer would be "unknown" — a re-bootstrap — in the ordinary case. (a) is right, and the delta is (a) done honestly: a session changeset already carries the old value of exactly the columns the statement touched, and the untouched columns are identical in both images, so a whole second image would be bytes spent restating `row_json`. Measured on the change this wave's suites exercise, the delta is the changed columns alone — for the one-field edits the log is dominated by, a handful of bytes against a full row. |
| **R-1014-12** (the 1 ↔ S seam) | **One revocation policy on both hosts.** A revoked seat **exports or holds** its unsent outbox before purging its copy, and never deletes it silently; the gateway keeps the idempotency ledger for a revoked device **across re-enrolment**. Lands in waves 1 and S. | Revocation is about the device's future access, not about the member's past writes — a queued intent is data they authored and were told was saved. Two hosts each deciding what "revoke" means is how one of them ends up destroying it, and dropping the ledger at revoke is what lets a re-paired phone replay work the gateway already did. |

### Deliberate non-goals (#1014)

- **The HEIC/libvips decoder and the `ftypavif` sniff** stay where they are, as [QUALITY.md](../QUALITY.md) entries under [#1011](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1011).
- **No redesign of the share transport.** Wave D fixes what does not relay; the wire itself is not re-argued here.
- **No QR or deep-link pairing door.** The 524-character ticket is an instrument problem for the simulator and a real ergonomic problem for a human, and it is a proposal of its own.
- **No threshold or second-factor recovery kit.** X13 gets a passphrase strength floor here; the rest is a follow-up ([R-1014-7](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)).
- **No per-commit `replica_log` coalescing** ([R-1014-5](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)): it changes the log's semantics under every wave in this umbrella and belongs in its own proposal.

### Open questions for the owner (#1014)

| Id | The question | Why it is open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Q-1014-1** | Every **R-1014** ruling above adopts [#1014](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1014)'s own recommendation without an owner reading. Confirm or reverse each before release. | The alternative was to hold twelve waves until an owner was available, and a wave built over an unrecorded guess is worse than one built over a recorded provisional ruling. Four of the twelve change what the member sees or what the product promises — the refusal mark ([R-1014-2](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)), the guest's second phone ([R-1014-4](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)), the seat at rest ([R-1014-7](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)) and the bands on the phone ([R-1014-8](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)) — and those are the owner's calls, not an agent's. |

## Mobile UX consistency (#1015)

Ruled 2026-09-10 by [#1015](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1015). A nine-lane simulator audit of the Expo app found that the nine mobile surfaces share no header, back affordance, search field, confirm dialog, empty state or date format — six header shapes, seven back affordances, five date formats, five confirm patterns and, in places, no confirm at all. The re-judgement that opens this section is that none of that is a set of app-local bugs: the kit was optional and unenforced, each app was rebuilt in its own wave with its own copy tables and formatters, and `StatusLine` was unreachable from inside a `Modal`, so apps grew second feedback channels rather than fixing the one. [DESIGN.md](../DESIGN.md) already stated the target; the six rulings below are the ones the audit could not settle for itself, because each is a product choice and not a reading of the rulebook.

| Id | Current decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **D1 Trash** | **Empty trash everywhere.** Photos' live control is right, and Docs gets a real Empty trash behind the outlined-`--net` confirm. Docs' "cannot be emptied" copy goes. | One vault, one answer. The two surfaces were making opposite promises about the same rows, so a member reading either one was being told something false about the other. A capability that exists is not withheld from one room because that room shipped later. |
| **D2 Casing** | **Sentence case everywhere**, including Photos' menus. [#712](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/712)'s Title Case menu copy is superseded. | Casing is signage, and two registers in one app read as two products. The house rule is sentence case ([DESIGN.md](../DESIGN.md) §Copy) and Photos is the pattern-setter for the design system — a pattern-setter that diverges from the rule teaches the divergence. The one exception is `SectionBlock`'s uppercase micro-label, which is a typographic device and not copy (R-NY-3). |
| **D3 Editors** | **Autosave everywhere.** "Cancel" exists only before the first keystroke; after it, close = done. | Notes' silent discard (B6) is what the alternative costs: the shared copy already promised "Every change is saved as you write" while the editor threw the work away. Either the promise or the behaviour had to move, and the promise is the one the member believes. |
| **D4 Push vs sheet** | **Content pushes, choices sheet.** A destination the member reads is a pushed page with a real back target; a decision the member makes is an `OptionSheet`. | The two presentations were being chosen per screen by whoever built it, which is why the audit found seven back affordances. Tying the choice to what is on the surface makes it derivable rather than remembered. |
| **D5 Band** | The **editor hides the band**; **selection dims it** through leaf tokens and makes it non-interactive. Never live under two bars. | B8 is the failure: two bars at the foot with the band live under them, so one tap navigated away mid-selection and lost the selection. Dimming through leaf tokens rather than container opacity is the standing disabled contract — see the disabled rule in [DESIGN.md](../DESIGN.md). |
| **D6 Settings** | Settings is reachable from the **Home cover's trailing control** _and_ from **More**. | Invariant 1 (stem foot): the shell's own places are reachable from the shell's own root. Home had no entry at all (B15), and a second entry point is not duplication when one of them is the root the member starts from. |

### Deliberate non-goals (#1015)

- **No retheming.** No new hues, type roles or motion vocabulary. The constitution is not up for debate in this umbrella; the work is making the phone obey the one that exists.
- **No desktop or PWA seat.** The same grammar reaches those seats in a separate wave. Fixing one seat at a time is what makes the mobile gallery a usable gate.
- **No new features.** Only the product paths the audit found missing — Locker's unlock key, Tally's add-expense entry, Tasks' due and reminder fields — are restored, because a room with no way into it is not a consistency problem.

### Later rulings (#1015)

Appended as the umbrella's lanes reached them; each row is dated and does not rewrite the six above.

| Date | Ruling | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-10 | **A notebook is a place reached only by tapping Notebooks.** The Notes band offers no notebook tab, and `notes-band.test.ts`'s pin that "a filter is not a place" is superseded for notebooks only — a filter over the note list still is not one. | The band's five seats are the app's rooms; a notebook is one of an unbounded set, so putting one on the band makes the band a function of the member's data. Reaching it through Notebooks keeps the band fixed and still gives a notebook a real back target (D4). |
| 2026-09-10 | **Emptying the Docs trash is one vault command, `core.empty_document_trash`, that collapses every trashed document's grace window onto its own `deleted_at`.** The gateway's lifecycle sweep stays the only thing that destroys a document. | D1 asked for the control, and the vault had no door: `core.trash_document` refuses an already-trashed document and nothing could bring a purge date forward. Making the command a DATE rather than a second destruction path keeps the sweep's rent checks, authority revocations and provenance receipts as the single audited way a document leaves the vault. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-KIT-2 · An editor hides the band and carries its leave key in its own header.** S13 reduces to "the band's `current` is computed, never written down". | Two bars at the foot with a live band under them is D5's failure. An editor that keeps the band needs a rule for which bar owns the foot; an editor that hides it needs none. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-KIT-6 · `knip` is the root's full run at close**, not a lane's. | An unused export in one lane is usually another lane's not-yet-merged caller. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-6 · The Docs web-seat half of D1 stays** (the copy module is shared). | The two seats read one copy module, so a mobile-only fix would fork it. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-7 · `tests/inventory.json`'s TrashAsk row is refreshed at close**, not per lane. | It is one file that every lane touches; per-lane edits are pure merge conflict. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-4 · `viewer-toolbar-states.ts` extraction accepted.** | The toolbar's states were a switch inside a render; a table can be swept. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-5 · The Prioritise identifier sweep is Wave 3.** | It is a rename across trees two lanes were editing. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-6 · `subBase` is re-exported through `kit/theme`** (SHELL lane owns it). | A documented sub-base seam belongs to the theme, not to whichever component discovered it. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-7 · `SearchField` gains an `autoFocus` pass-through**; callers restore their own. | The kit field replaced three hand-rolled ones, two of which focused on mount. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-8 · Nearest-rung snaps accepted** where a literal was within a rung of the scale. | The alternative is a second scale, which the zero-debt gate forbids. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-9 · `PhotosHome` migrates to `AppPlace` in its own round.** | It is the largest screen in the tree and the pattern-setter; it does not ride along. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-10 · `PeopleConfirm` on `SheetRoom` with named titles accepted**; `ConfirmSheet` gains a title override at Wave 4 only if a third caller needs it. | One caller is not a contract. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-11 / R-SH-4 · The `screen-root` predicate is scoped at Wave 4** to files the app REGISTERS as screens. | 153 findings, ~96 of them leaf components. A rule that cries about a hundred non-problems is a rule nobody wires. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-12 · Retiring `locker-gate-field` accepted** ([#1002](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1002) removed the field). | The id outlived the control it named. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-13 · `selectedSentence` takes the SINGULAR noun**; every caller passes singular. | One pluraliser, at the one place that knows the count. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-B-14 · `PhotosBand`'s in-place destination switch stays**; band unification is not in this umbrella. | It is a navigation model change, not a consistency fix. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-1 · Automations stays a `SystemPlace`.** | It is a place in `places.ts` with no app mark; `AppPlace` would make an identity claim the product does not make. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-2 · `RoomError.detail` is kept, and never receives an exception string**; `lint-mobile-rooms` gains the `error-detail` rule at Wave 4. | A detail line is where a member learns what to do next. The fix is a rule that forbids the payload, not deleting the slot. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-3 · Section-level empties stay per-screen.** | A section's empty is about that section's rows; the room's empty is about the page. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-5 · `SheetRoom`'s `maxHeight: 85%` accepted**; the root checks it on device at the re-audit. | A sheet taller than the screen is worse than one capped a little low, and the cap is one number in one room. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-6 · `HomeRoom.head` is the permanent shape.** The cover's own title row is a leaf the room hosts, not a `trailing` action. | It carries the vault lockup, the Settings key (D6) and its own tests; folding it into `trailing` would flatten three things into one slot. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-SH-7 · A pending change that is not accepted says "Your vault did not accept this change. Try again."** | It was the gateway's own `reason` — an exception string wearing a sentence's clothes (S14). The states the kit has words for keep them; everything else gets one noun and one verb. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-8 · `DocumentViewer` handles its stage in the room, not in a wrapper.** | The stage is the viewer's body state; a wrapper around a room re-implements what `RoomBody` already is. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-9 · The selection OBJECT is the mode, not `count > 0`.** A room is in selection mode iff a caller passes `selection`. | An empty selection is still selection mode — the member entered it deliberately and has not picked yet. Deriving the mode from the count exits it under the member's finger. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-10 · `DocumentViewer` sits on the room header with a `colors.stage` media area.** | A viewer is a pushed page with a dark plate for the page image, not a chrome-free screen; the stage token exists for exactly that plate. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-11 / R-B-11 · The `screen-root` predicate landed at Wave 4** — a file is a screen iff it is registered as a route or is a `*Screen`/`*Home` frame. 153 findings → 56. | A rule that reports a hundred leaf components is a rule nobody wires into the gate. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-12 · `HISTORY_UNREADABLE` is deleted.** | No seat called it. A copy constant with no caller is not a contract, it is residue. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-13 · `PushedPage` gains `backTestID`.** | Two end-to-end flows select the back key by handle, and a negative asserted on copy passes forever the day the copy is re-worded. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-14 · The four Photos callers pass `selection` only in selection mode.** | The corollary of R-A-9 at the call sites: the object's presence is the mode, so it may not be passed unconditionally. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-15 · `surfaceWriteFailure` never interpolates `error.message`**; refusals reach the member through a typed channel. | S14: an exception string is engine vocabulary, and the one helper every app's write path funnels through was pasting it into member copy. |
| 2026-09-10 | **R-A-16 · `OptionSheet`'s iOS path honours `selectedId`.** | `ActionSheetIOS` was told the options and not which one was current, so the sheet opened with no mark on the live choice. |

Fixing the source removes the need for the filter. |

"Needs you" says what is on the page, a decision waiting on the member, which none of the retired nouns did. Naming it as a room is the fix; allowlisting it would be a ratchet nobody lowers. |

This supersedes R-NY-4's "the desktop keeps its own noun this round". | One destination wore two names across two seats, which is the divergence R-NY-4 ended on the phone. A notice pushed as if it were a decision sends the member to a queue that no longer holds it (R-NY-2). |
| 2026-09-11 | **R-NY-17 · `verify-native-state` checks drift, not absence.** [#1011](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1011) tracks the generated `ios/` and `android/` trees on purpose; the check asserts they match what prebuild generates from the app config. | A check that demanded the trees be absent contradicted the ruling that tracks them. What can go wrong with a tracked generated tree is drift from its source, so drift is what the check reads. |
| 2026-09-11 | **R-NY-18 · A phone reaches the gateway as itself.** A request the desktop tunnel forwards from a paired phone carries that phone's own EndpointId to the embedded gateway, proved by a stamp derived from the loopback bearer the phone never sees, and the gateway resolves that phone's own enrolment — never the host's. Host identity narrows to `isDirectHostRequest`; an unstamped forwarded hop is refused, not promoted. Pairing a phone creates its vault enrolment and revoking it tombstones that row, so revocation refuses every replica door, the Locker key door included, whichever store it was written to. | `deviceKeyFor` fell back to the host for any loopback request without a peer header, and every forwarder delivers a remote party to 127.0.0.1. So a paired phone WAS the owner of the box at the key door that hands back `K`, and revoking it changed nothing there, because nothing had ever named it. The old comment said tightening this would sever phone-link; it was severed already — the forwarder named nobody, so there was no phone to name. |
| 2026-09-11 | **R-NY-19 · The phone enrols into Locker from Locker.** "Enrol this phone" sits on the Locker wall; the phone reads the gateway's `seatLockerKey` flag, refuses the manual-URL lane, fetches `K` over the key door, checks the `vaultId`, and stores it only in the device keychain behind `requireAuthentication` and `WHEN_PASSCODE_SET_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`. It is not part of `pair()`. Desktop and web Locker stay honest refusals this round, with copy that does not claim another seat can reveal. | Pairing is a transport ceremony done once in Settings, often before Locker has been opened; the key to every secret in the vault should not ride along with it unasked. Asking on the wall puts the gesture where the thing being unlocked is, and over a channel the member's own enrolment already authenticated. The phone is the only seat with an OS-enforced presence prompt over the stored key, so it is the only seat that gets one this round. |

## v1 platform — Rust core, KMP shell, Electron seat, gateway anywhere (#1020)

Ruled 2026-09-12 by [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020), the umbrella that replaces v0's TypeScript monoculture with **one Rust core running as either a gateway or a seat, a Kotlin Multiplatform mobile shell with native views, and an Electron desktop seat over a sidecar**. Three things prompted it: mobile performance, because the phone runs SQL, the applier and every app query on Hermes's JS thread; tooling, because rule two is served in v0 by TypeScript-only tools and v1 is three languages; and surfaces, because only desktop and mobile matter, so the web PWA and every browser-only plane it forced into the seat engine retire. **v0 is treated as the executable specification** — its DDL, decisions, fixtures, Maestro flows, copy and tokens — and not as code to migrate.

This section is the umbrella's **wave 0 charter**: the rulings every later wave is planned against, the deliberate non-goals, and the questions left for the owner. Every ruling the lanes then made under the standing delegation is mirrored below, wave by wave — [wave 1](#wave-1-lane-rulings-1020), [wave 2](#wave-2-lane-rulings-1020) (with [lane V](#decisions--lane-v-1020) and [lane B2](#decisions--lane-b2-1020) in their own sections), [wave 3](#wave-3-lane-rulings-1020) (and [its close](#decisions--wave-3-close-lane-x3-1020)), [wave 4](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020) — closing with [the open questions as they now stand](#the-close-pass--the-open-questions-and-the-corrections-1020). **v0's rulings stay current for the v0 tree until wave 6 deletes it.** Every row below rules the **v1** tree, and where a row narrows or reverses a v0 ruling, the pointer in [Superseded decision pointers](#superseded-decision-pointers) says so and says which tree each answer governs. A ruling whose code does not exist yet names the wave that lands it.

**These rulings are in force.** Each row adopts the recommendation [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) argues for; they were recorded provisionally on 2026-09-12 and confirmed as written the same day by the owner's delegation ([R-1020-34](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)), which [**Q-1020-1**](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) below records. They are no longer provisional and no longer a release blocker; reversal is the owner's on [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020).

| Id | Current decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **R-1020-1** — the platform | **Rust for the shared core, Kotlin Multiplatform for the mobile shell with native UI, Electron for the desktop seat.** Compatibility with **v0 artifacts** — v0 seat files, v0 pair tickets, v0 npm gateway packages, `expo-updates` — is explicitly not a constraint, and neither is migration cost. Compatibility **between v1 releases** is a constraint and is [R-1020-12](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020). | The decision is **structural, not measured**, and [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) says so in its own words: the only handler timing on record (188 ms for Tally's dashboard at its 2,000-expense window, [receipt #922](../receipts/issue-922-snappier-blueprints.md)) was taken on a 4-core Node host over node-sqlite and says nothing about a phone. The answer is therefore not "v0 is too slow by N ms" but "data code stops running on a JS engine at all", and the numbers that hold the work honest are the absolute targets in [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) rather than a comparison against v0. |
| **R-1020-2** — one program, two roles, one binary | **One crate family, `centraid-core`, runs as the gateway (the vault's authority) or as a seat (full replica, applier, outbox, settlement, every app's queries and commands), and ships as one binary, `centraid`,** with `gateway`, `seat`, `pair`, `doctor`, `recover`, `export` and `native-host` subcommands. **The CLI is a client, not a privileged path**: `centraid devices revoke`, `centraid backup now` and every other admin verb send the same command messages an owner seat sends, over the local socket, and produce the same receipts. A headless gateway has no admin UI. | Two implementations of one applier is how v0 ended with two logs and two retention floors ([R-1014-1](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)); one crate makes the gateway and the seat the same code under a different role, which is also what makes [R-1020-30](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)'s simulation possible at all. The CLI clause is the same argument one rung down, from [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020): a privileged admin path is a second authorization surface to audit, and "revoke a lost phone from the desktop" should be the same command as from the terminal, with the same receipt. |
| **R-1020-3** (OQ3 adjacent) — transport, **provisional until wave 3** | **iroh QUIC is the transport and the product opens no listening TCP port by default.** All seat-to-gateway traffic is iroh under the device allowlist; media moves over iroh-blobs; a VPS needs no domain, certificate or reverse proxy. Two things this does **not** claim: hole-punching falls back to **relays reached over outbound TCP 443**, so the product depends on relay availability (n0's public relays by default, a self-hosted relay as a documented option, and a specified, tested failure posture when none is reachable); and **iOS background transfer is unresolved**, because an iroh connection does not survive app suspension and Apple's system-managed background transfers are HTTP only. The ruling is therefore **provisional until the wave 3 physical-iPhone experiment** decides between foreground plus `BGProcessingTask` drains only (v0's behaviour today, [mobile-offline.md](mobile-offline.md#background-work-and-push-privacy)) and an optional, off-by-default HTTPS blob door ([R-1020-15](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)). The per-state promise — foreground, backgrounded, locked, suspended, relaunched — is written down before wave 4. | "Zero ports" is the property that makes a gateway on rented hardware ordinary rather than an operations project, and it is worth keeping. What it is not is unconditional, and a transport ruling that hid the relay dependence would be the kind of half-truth [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s review round 1 caught: the fallback path is outbound TCP to somebody else's relay, and on iOS the whole question of who moves bytes while the app is suspended has no answer yet. Marking the ruling provisional and naming the experiment that closes it is what stops wave 4 from being built on an assumption nobody measured. |
| **R-1020-4** — two schema packages, two promises | **One protobuf schema workspace, `crates/api-proto`, in two packages with different compatibility promises.** `centraid.core.v1` (queries, commands, rows, change events, wire messages) is a **gateway compatibility commitment**; `centraid.screen.v1` (screen states and events) is a **shell-internal contract that may change every release**. buf enforces breaking-change rules per package — see [R-1020-12](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020). | One schema with one promise forces the weaker half to carry the stronger half's cost: a screen-state field rename would become a wire break, so screen shapes would ossify or the promise would quietly stop being kept. Two packages let the wire be strict where a member's paired phone depends on it and loose where only the shell reads it, and the split is declared in [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) rather than discovered per change. |
| **R-1020-5** — the five-function C ABI | **`open`, `call`, `next_event`, `free`, `close`, from cbindgen — five exported symbols, counted by a test in `core-ffi`.** No UniFFI, no Gobley, no typeshare. The contract lives in `crates/core-ffi/CONTRACT.md` and each clause has a test in wave 2: buffers returned by `call` and `next_event` are callee-allocated and released only through `free`, inputs borrowed for the call; `call` is reentrant across threads and two concurrent calls never block each other on the FFI itself; every request carries a request id and `Cancel{request_id}` cancels an in-flight unbounded operation while bounded reads are bounded instead of cancellable; `next_event` blocks on a **bounded** queue, coalescing change events per `(table, pk)` and dropping nothing, so a consumer that stops reading stalls sync after the queue fills — the correct backpressure, surfaced as a health event; `close` unblocks any thread in `next_event` with a terminal event and then releases the handle, and calls after it return a typed error; a Rust **panic never crosses the boundary** — every entry point is wrapped, the panic becomes an error response with a diagnostic id, and the handle is poisoned so the shell restarts the core deliberately. | A five-symbol surface is small enough to specify completely and to test clause by clause, which a generated binding layer is not — and the generators each add a build-time dependency across eight targets for ergonomics the KMP module does not need, since it speaks protobuf bytes either way. The clause list is the answer to [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s review round 1: an ABI whose ownership, threading, cancellation, backpressure, shutdown and panic behaviour are estimated rather than written down is where the unsafe bugs live, and the wave 2 binding spike measures the numbers rather than assuming them. |
| **R-1020-6** — mobile | **One KMP shared module** (screen state machines, navigation model, sync scheduling, notification plan, platform services behind expect/actual) with **SwiftUI and Jetpack Compose** views. Screen states and events are protobuf messages, so Swift binds through SwiftProtobuf without SKIE. **Compose Multiplatform is not used on iOS.** `mobile/shared/commonMain` has no platform import, enforced by Konsist. | The phone's problem in v0 is not that its views are written twice; it is that its state and data code run on Hermes. Sharing the state machines and rendering natively puts the shared half where the bugs are and leaves each platform's view layer to the toolkit its own OS ships. CMP on iOS would trade a known rendering path for a second runtime to debug on the surface [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) most wants to be boring. |
| **R-1020-7** (OQ6) — desktop is a seat | **Electron main spawns a seat sidecar process; the renderer stays a thin React client over a local socket with protobuf-es types.** The socket is only the renderer-to-sidecar hop; the sidecar pairs to the gateway anywhere over iroh. There is **no Node native module**. A seat runs **replicated** (default, full local copy, offline) or **thin** (no local copy, every call forwarded to the gateway and run under the caller's principal), with **one API common to both modes** — and the renderer **does** see availability, durability, pending-work and connectivity states. **The v0 React app UIs are carried over as presentation** ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ6, answered yes). | A sidecar keeps one core per device and one language for data code, and it is what makes a later host swap ([R-1020-18](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)) a drop-in rather than a rewrite. The mode-visible clause is not a feature: a thin seat that has lost its gateway has nothing to show and must say so, and a renderer blind to its own mode would draw an empty room as an empty vault. Carrying the v0 UIs costs wiring and buys every screen the product already accepted. |
| **R-1020-8** (OQ1) — plain SQLite | **No SQLCipher.** At-rest encryption is the platform's — iOS Data Protection, Android FBE, disk encryption on the gateway host — plus sealed columns for secrets. Key custody and the recovery design are wave 2 lane R **regardless of this answer**. | SQLCipher costs vendored OpenSSL across eight targets and never protected against the threat that actually matters once the gateway is on rented hardware, which is a host operator with disk or memory access — the threat [R-1020-9](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) answers properly. A database key that a running process holds in memory is not a defence against the person who owns the memory, and [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ1 says so. |
| **R-1020-9** (OQ8) — the trust premise | **The gateway host is trusted for data and blind for secrets.** Rows and blobs on the gateway are host-readable — the operator who can read disk or memory can read the vault, **and the product says so**. What changes against v0 is that **Locker secret cells become end-to-end sealed under a member key the gateway never holds**, unwrapped only on the seat that reveals: the gateway stores ciphertext, serves it to enrolled seats, and cannot read it. This narrows v0's L-export and household-placement rulings to seat-side operations. The ruling is wave 0; the Locker lane that ships it is **wave 4**, and until it merges this is the ruled premise and not a shipped property. [SECURITY.md](../SECURITY.md#the-gateway-on-rented-hardware--v1-trust-premise-1020) carries the threat model. | v0's premise is "the local gateway is L0-trusted", and a gateway on a VPS is not local — so the premise silently changed the moment "gateway anywhere" was ruled, which is exactly the drift [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s review round 2 named. Full end-to-end for **all** rows is refused deliberately: app logic runs on the gateway, so a blind gateway would be a dumb relay and would delete the design. Secrets are the one class where a blind host is worth its cost, and everything else is documented as host-readable rather than implied to be private. |
| **R-1020-10** — tooling follows the code | **Each language uses its first-class toolchain, and everything cross-cutting is a Rust `xtask` crate.** `cargo xtask gate` is the **only** CI entrypoint, with `local`, `pr`, `nightly` and `release` profiles and a feedback-time budget each — `local` under 2 minutes, `pr` under 15, `nightly` unbounded with device lanes, `release` with the restore drill and release smoke. Every failing gate uploads its artifact (trace, screenshot, Perfetto file) and prints the one line that says why. One Nix flake pins every toolchain but Xcode; a version file pins Xcode. tree-sitter is for structural rules only; semantic checks stay with each compiler and linter. **Compile time is the new Hermes**: clean `cargo check`, incremental `cargo check` after a one-line edit in an app crate, incremental `cargo nextest` for one crate, and the shared module's Kotlin/Native framework link are **down-only ledger entries**, as is the core library's size per ABI. | Rule two — never compromise on tooling — was served in v0 by tools that only understand TypeScript, and a cross-cutting layer that depends on one of three languages would be a layer two of them route around. The compile-time clause is the honest half: the reason TypeScript felt fast was the edit-run loop, and a large Rust workspace plus a Kotlin/Native link can lose it with every CI budget still green, so [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) ledgers the developer loop the same way the product's ceilings are ledgered. |
| **R-1020-11** (OQ7) — repository layout | **A new top-level tree in this repository** — `crates/`, `contracts/`, `mobile/`, `desktop/`, `extension/`, `design/`, `copy/`, `deploy/` — with **the v0 tree pinned as a read-only oracle until wave 6**. The only permitted edits to v0 are fixture-adapter changes under `packages/*/test/**` and `tests/**` that make v0 suites read `contracts/` files, listed in the receipt as they land. **From wave 1 v0's own gates stop running on PRs**; only the `contracts/` oracle suite runs against the pinned tree, nightly. v0's ledgers retire with v0; v1's seed from wave 1 measurements and ratchet from there. | Two trees in one repository is what lets every `contracts/` fixture be proved twice — once in Rust, once by the TypeScript implementation that is the specification — without a second checkout or a submodule. Keeping v0's CI on PRs would double every PR's cost for no signal (the v0 iOS lane alone is ~51 minutes), so [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) buys the oracle and declines the bill. |
| **R-1020-12** (OQ4) — the version window | **A gateway supports seats from the last N = 3 minor releases.** `open` and the pairing handshake exchange `{schema_version, min_supported}`; a seat outside the window gets a typed **`UpgradeRequired`** the shell renders, and a gateway older than the seat is allowed as long as the seat's `min_supported` admits it. `centraid.core.v1` is checked by `buf breaking` against **the PR base and every released tag inside the window**; `centraid.screen.v1` against the PR base only. Unknown fields are preserved and unknown message types are answered with `Unsupported{type}`, never dropped silently. **Database migrations are forward-only**, run by the core on `open`, held as fixtures in `contracts/migrations`, and a seat whose file is newer than its binary refuses to open with **`DowngradeRefused`** rather than guessing. A gateway upgrade that fails mid-migration restores from the pre-migration snapshot it takes first — the same path as the restore drill ([R-1020-29](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)). | A household upgrades its phones when the stores get around to it, so version skew in the field is the normal case and not the exception. Three minor releases is long enough that a member is never locked out by an upgrade they did not choose and short enough that the gateway is not carrying five wire shapes. Checking `buf breaking` against **released tags** rather than the previous commit is the part [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s review round 1 corrected: a chain of individually-compatible commits can still break the release a member is actually running. |
| **R-1020-13** (OQ5) — a local gateway | **Yes: a gateway on the laptop is the same binary installed as a service** — `centraid gateway install` on macOS (launchd), Windows (service) and Linux (systemd). | "Gateway anywhere" is worth nothing if the anywhere a member starts with — their own laptop, no server — is a different program. One binary with an install verb keeps the fresh-laptop path and the VPS path on the same code and the same receipts, which is what [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s inventory row for desktop service install asks for. |
| **R-1020-14** (OQ2) — push wake | **A content-free wake relay, built as a second route on the existing OAuth worker**: stateless, content-free, rate-limited per device token, opt-in per device. It lands in the **wave 4** notifications lane and **nothing in the core depends on it**. | A push relay is a service the product would have to run forever, so it belongs on a codebase and threat-model row that already exists rather than on a new one. Keeping the core independent of it is what makes the ruling cheap to reverse: if the answer becomes "no push in v1", the wave 4 lane is deleted and no other wave notices — see [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ2. |
| **R-1020-15** (OQ3) — the HTTPS blob door | **Prefer no door.** The product promise is "backup completes overnight while charging on Wi-Fi", through `BGProcessingTask`. The **wave 3** experiment measures assets per night on a physical iPhone, and the door is added **only if that number is below what a member would notice — the stated threshold is 500 assets per night**. If it ships it is **off by default** and is the only listener the product would ever have. The decision is made **after wave 3**, on the measurement. | Most photo backup already happens overnight on a charger, so the promise the recommendation keeps is the one members actually experience, and it costs no listener. Naming the threshold **before** the measurement is the point: a number chosen after the run is a number chosen to justify whatever the run produced, which is the failure mode [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) Amendment 3 fixed for OQ10 and the same discipline applies here. |
| **R-1020-16** (OQ9) — recognition placement | **Faces and OCR run on the device that holds the asset** (Vision, ML Kit), with a **gateway CPU fallback for assets no device holds**; **text and image embeddings run on the gateway through `ort` under a rate budget**. | A phone has a neural engine and the original bytes; a $5 VPS has neither. v0 runs CLIP, OCR and faces as gateway automations with native inference dependencies, which is fine on a laptop and slow on rented hardware, so [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ9 moves each capability to the host that can actually do it and keeps a fallback so an asset nobody holds is still enriched. |
| **R-1020-17** (OQ10) — screen contracts | **Protobuf through wave 3, then decided on evidence.** The integration proof builds the three screens as protobuf and the receipt records authoring friction (lines per screen, edits per change) and the double-serialisation cost. **Exit criterion, fixed before wave 3**: switch to Kotlin sealed classes + SKIE if any of **(a)** proto plus builder code for a screen exceeds 1.5× the lines of the equivalent Kotlin data classes, averaged over the three screens; **(b)** SwiftUI and Compose disagree on a shared state fixture more than once during wave 3; **(c)** Kotlin→Swift state delivery exceeds 1 ms at p95 on the reference iPhone at the 50k-asset library. If it switches, only `mobile/iosApp`'s wrapper changes. | Building three screens both ways is not affordable, so the choice has to be made on one implementation's evidence — and evidence collected without a criterion written first is sunk cost wearing a measurement's clothes, which is why [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) Amendment 3 fixed (a), (b) and (c) before the wave starts. Serialisation cost alone is not expected to trip (c); the benefit being paid for is one language-neutral fixture driving both Roborazzi and swift-snapshot-testing, and (b) is the test of whether it was earned. |
| **R-1020-18** (OQ11) — Electron, not Tauri | **Keep Electron for v1.** Tauri is a **v2** question. | The renderer UIs are reused and the packaging is known, so Electron costs nothing new this round. The sidecar boundary of [R-1020-7](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) is what makes the swap a later drop-in — Tauri's Rust main process could host the seat in-process — so deferring it forfeits nothing ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ11). |
| **R-1020-19** (OQ12) — the blob store | **Local filesystem only in v1.** An S3-compatible backend is a later proposal. The installer states the disk requirement up front and the storage screen reports it. | VPS disks are small, and the honest answer to that is to say the number before the member commits rather than to add a second storage backend with its own failure modes to v1's scope ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ12). |
| **R-1020-20** (OQ13) — the physical-device lane | **A self-hosted macOS runner with one iPhone and one Android attached, on nightly and release lanes only.** PR lanes run on simulator and emulator. Manual evidence is acceptable for the wave 3 transfer experiment itself. | The battery and transfer targets are exactly the numbers an emulator cannot produce, and a target with no lane behind it drifts unnoticed — which is what [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json)'s parked mobile rows already record. Keeping the lane off PRs is what makes it affordable ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ13). |
| **R-1020-21** (OQ14) — Windows | **Windows is a first-class desktop target in v1**, alongside macOS and Linux — the same three targets v0's electron-builder ships. The named pipe ([R-1020-26](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)) and the service install ([R-1020-13](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)) are the only Windows-specific code. | Dropping a platform v0 already ships would be a product regression dressed as scope control, and the Windows-specific surface is two named mechanisms rather than a port ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) OQ14). |
| **R-1020-22** — the extension | **The browser extension is MV3 over native messaging to the `centraid` binary**, which reaches the local seat. **No WASM and no iroh in a browser.** The native-messaging host manifest is registered by the desktop installer with an allowlist of the extension's ids. | v0's Companion carries an Iroh/WASM transport into the browser, which is a second network stack in the least controllable process the product runs in. Native messaging reaches the seat that already exists, over the socket whose authentication is [R-1020-26](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)'s, and deletes the whole browser transport plane ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) capability inventory). |
| **R-1020-23** — the artifact key law | **A prebuilt core artifact is keyed on the content digest of `crates/**` and `contracts/**`, the schema, the target triple, the feature set, the toolchain and the profile — never on `Cargo.lock` alone.** The binary embeds `{git_sha, digest, schema_version}`; **every shell reads it at `open` and refuses a mismatch** against its own expected digest, and a CI test installs a deliberately stale artifact and asserts the refusal. Symbol files (dSYM, NDK) are published beside every artifact so native crash reports symbolicate Rust frames without a third-party crash service. | This is the existing G-artifact-key law, restated for a new artifact: **a key names everything that is in the artifact**. Keying on `Cargo.lock` was an error [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s review round 1 caught and the amendment corrected — a source edit with no dependency change would have served a stale core, and the refusal-at-`open` clause is what turns a cache bug into a loud failure instead of a phantom one. |
| **R-1020-24** — app extensions | **iOS and Android app extensions never open the vault file and never start iroh.** A Share extension writes an inbox record in the app group that the main app's core ingests on next launch; Autofill reads a **sealed, main-app-maintained** index in the app group and reveals through the main app. | Share, Autofill and Widget extensions run in separate processes with small memory caps, so a second core in one of them would be a second writer to the vault and a second iroh endpoint on the same device — which breaks the one-writer rule the whole applier depends on. v0 already ships a Share extension in an app group, so the inbox shape is the one the product has ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) review round 2, gap 2). |
| **R-1020-25** — `open` and the network | **`open` never blocks on the network**: it opens the file, runs migrations and returns; the iroh endpoint starts on a core thread afterwards and connectivity arrives as an **event**. On phones the endpoint **idles when the app is backgrounded** and resumes on foreground or on a scheduled sync pass. | An `open` that waits on a radio is a launch a member watches, on the surface where launches are judged, and a QUIC connection held open behind a backgrounded app is a battery complaint with the product's name on it. The battery target in [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) measures exactly this ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) review round 2, gap 4). |
| **R-1020-26** — local socket authentication | **The sidecar socket is a Unix socket at mode 0600 in the user's runtime directory — a named pipe with a user-only DACL on Windows — and the sidecar verifies the connecting peer's credentials (`SO_PEERCRED` / `getpeereid` / pipe client process token) before answering.** Any other local process gets nothing; the extension's native-messaging host is launched by the browser under the same user and passes the same check. | The socket carries every command an owner seat can send, so "it is only on loopback" is not an authorization story — v0's own boundary section already says the **OS user boundary** is the primary local boundary, and a socket with no peer check is weaker than that boundary, not equal to it ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) review round 2, gap 3). |
| **R-1020-27** — online-only commands | **The schema marks commands `online_only`** — Locker reveal, assistant turns, exports — and **a seat refuses to queue them offline with a typed reason the shell renders**. Every other command is durable in the outbox. | v0 has `ONLINE_ONLY_ACTIONS` and it exists because queueing a reveal is a promise the seat cannot keep: the member would be told the act was saved and find out later that it never could have run. Declaring the class **in the schema** is what makes both shells render the same refusal without either of them keeping a list ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) review round 2, gap 8). |
| **R-1020-28** — media delivery | **Blobs never cross the ABI as bytes.** On phones the core **materialises** a blob into the app's private container and returns a **path**; a blob still being fetched is a growing file with a **`ready_bytes`** event, so a video can start before it lands. On desktop, Electron main registers a **`centraid://`** protocol handler that streams from the sidecar over the socket **with range support**, and the renderer never touches a port. **Authorization is the seat's**: a path or URL is minted **per request under the caller's principal** and expires. | Copying a 4 GiB video through a five-function ABI would put the whole file in the shell's address space for no reason, and handing a player a port would make the seat's authorization a URL anyone on the box could replay. A minted, expiring, principal-scoped handle is the smallest thing a player needs, and `ready_bytes` is what lets progressive playback exist at all ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) execution model). |
| **R-1020-29** — one snapshot, three uses | **The gateway's backup snapshot, a new seat's bootstrap, and the pre-migration safety copy are the same artifact**: a `VACUUM INTO` file plus WAL segments, content-addressed and moved over iroh-blobs. | v0 already takes the bootstrap snapshot as a physically sanitised `VACUUM INTO` copy ([R4](#one-vault-every-seat-996)); making it a content-addressed blob gives resumable, verified transfer for free and **removes two mechanisms** — three artifacts with three transfer paths is three places for a truncation bug to hide, and [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) collapses them into one. |
| **R-1020-30** — the sync proof | **Deterministic simulation is the primary sync proof, and it runs on every PR.** Because gateway and seat are one crate ([R-1020-2](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)), a `turmoil` simulation runs one gateway and N seats in a single process under a scripted network — partitions, reordering, duplication, latency, crashes, clock skew — with a fixed seed, and asserts convergence and receipt invariants after every schedule. **A failing seed is a reproducible test case** and stays in the suite. | This is the single largest correctness upgrade the platform change buys, and it was missing from the first draft of [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020). v0 could never run it across Bun, Hermes and a browser worker, so every replication bug in [#1014](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1014) had to be found by a human driving two simulators. One process, one seed, one schedule turns that class of bug into a test somebody can re-run. |
| **R-1020-31** — the execution model | **Rust owns every durable fact and every derived read; Kotlin owns screen state and navigation; Swift and Compose own nothing — they render and forward events.** A screen's state machine has exactly one owner (the KMP module) and one thread (the shell's core dispatcher), and the UI thread only ever receives a finished state message. **`call` must never be invoked from a UI thread**, asserted by a debug assertion in both actuals, and **a `call` budget runs in the `pr` profile**: any request that exceeds it fails the gate. Bounded reads run on the core dispatcher; unbounded work — sync, media, search indexing — runs on core-owned threads inside the Rust process and reports through events. | A synchronous ABI on a UI thread is a frozen app, and that risk is real rather than theoretical — so the answer is a rule a machine can hold (the dispatcher assertion) plus a number a gate can fail on (the `call` budget), not a convention. The ownership split is the same discipline: two owners for one screen's state is how v0's phone ended with a projection fork per app, and [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) names one owner per layer before any screen is written. |
| **R-1020-32** — recognition weights | **Model weights are fetched on first use and never bundled.** | Bundling four ABIs' worth of model weights into a store binary charges every member the download whether they enable recognition or not, and pins the model to the release. Fetching on first use is what lets a model swap re-derive the library with no new build, which is the property v0's derivation stamps already give ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) capability inventory). |
| **R-1020-33** — civil time | **Civil time is the vault's zone, never the host's.** A gateway on a VPS runs UTC, and every scheduled automation still fires at the member's local time. | This is v0's cron-timezone ruling ([cron-timezone.md](cron-timezone.md)) carried forward with the one fact that changes: in v0 the host was usually the member's own machine, so the host's zone was accidentally right. On rented hardware it is accidentally wrong, and a daily brief that arrives at 07:00 UTC is a defect with no error message ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) capability inventory). |
| **R-1020-34** — owner delegation for this umbrella | **For every later open question in [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020), the root adopts the recommendation it writes.** The question, the recommendation and its adoption are recorded in the umbrella receipt ([issue-1020-v1-platform.md](../receipts/issue-1020-v1-platform.md), in the `## Decisions` of the lane that raised it) and mirrored here at the next docs commit. Reversal is the owner's on the issue. The delegation does **not** extend past [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020), and it does not cover a security finding's safer-fix rule, which is always taken. | The owner's instruction on 2026-09-12: they would rather the run proceed on written recommendations than hold waves for a reply, and the receipt is where each one is read back. |

### Deliberate non-goals (#1020)

- **Any compatibility with v0 artifacts** — v0 seat files, v0 pair tickets, v0 npm gateway packages, `expo-updates`. v0 is the executable specification, not a migration source ([R-1020-1](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)).
- **Compose Multiplatform on iOS** ([R-1020-6](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)).
