- **A phone acting as gateway.** Every device, desktop included, is a seat.
- **The push-wake relay's design.** The route and its threat-model row are ruled ([R-1020-14](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)); the relay's own design is wave 4's and is open.
- **New product features.** v1 reaches v0's accepted surfaces and flows, and nothing else.
- **An S3-compatible blob store** ([R-1020-19](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)).
- **Tauri in v1** ([R-1020-18](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)).

### Open questions for the owner (#1020)

| Id | The question | Why it is open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Q-1020-1** | **Ruled 2026-09-12** — every **R-1020-1** … **R-1020-33** row above is confirmed as written by owner delegation ([R-1020-34](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)). | The rows were recorded provisionally because no owner reading had happened; the owner's instruction on [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)'s run was to proceed on the recommendations as written, so they are rulings by delegation rather than a release blocker, and reversal is the owner's on the issue. |
| **Q-1020-2** | **Ruled 2026-09-12** — `device-android-mid` is the **Samsung Galaxy A55 (2024, Exynos 1480, 8 GB)** and `device-iphone-oldest` is the **iPhone XR on iOS 17**, named by owner delegation ([R-1020-34](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)). The ceilings in [`tests/journeys.json`](../tests/journeys.json) stay parked as `_intended` until a run on those two phones exists. | The A55 is the mid-range Android the v1 targets are stated for, and the XR is the oldest iPhone that runs the iOS floor v1's SwiftUI surface needs and the cheapest, most common handset of that floor. Naming the model was the one input an agent could not supply; it does not make a number measured, so promoting a parked ceiling still waits on the hardware. |

### The lane rulings (#1020)

The rows above are the wave 0 charter. What follows is every ruling the umbrella's own lanes made under the standing delegation [R-1020-34](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) — options written, recommendation adopted — mirrored out of [`receipts/issue-1020-v1-platform.md`](../receipts/issue-1020-v1-platform.md) at the close pass so that a reader looking for a decision reads a decisions file rather than a 5,000-line receipt. Each row is the ruling **in its final form**: where a later lane narrowed an earlier one, the row carries the narrowed text and the table below names the chain. `D-1020-<lane><n>` names the lane that raised it; the receipt section for that lane carries the evidence, the demonstrated red and the falsification.

**Supersession chains inside the umbrella.** Five rulings were narrowed or replaced by a later lane's evidence. Each survives in its narrowed form; none was discarded.

| Earlier | Narrowed or replaced by | What moved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **D-1020-D1-7** — one content-addressed snapshot, **three** uses (backup base, seat bootstrap, pre-migration safety copy) | **D-1020-R8** (wave 2 lane R) | Built that way, a backup generation's base is the _sanitised_ seat snapshot, so a restore came back without `locker_key`, `access_device_secret`, the `blob_*` custody tables, `outbox_item`, `replica_invocation_commit` and the log itself — 29 tables absent, from a backup that reported success. The ruling is **narrowed to two uses**; a backup's base is `backup::base::build_backup_base`, a plain `VACUUM INTO` with nothing removed, and a test asserts the two differ in both directions. Not a flag on one function: the flag's wrong value hands a phone the credential bands. |
| **Lane B's reading** that `local`'s 2-minute budget covered a run after `cargo clean` | **D-1020-B2-1** (wave 2 lane B2) | The budget is the developer's feedback time after an edit, so it is scored on a **warm** tree, detected by every workspace member having a linked artifact in `target/debug/deps`. A cold run is charged to `coldLocalProfileSeconds` and fails where that key states no ceiling. Being cold is never what makes a slow gate green. |
| **D-1020-D1-10** — the paged door's vault-side hook, with the interface left to be agreed | **D-1020-D3-10 → D-1020-V1** (wave 2 lanes D3 and V) | The hook stands; what changed is where the keyset refusal lives and what it refuses. D3 refused any page over a nullable sort column; lane V's sweep of all nine `(from, sortColumn)` pairs against a real vault showed that would remove rows a member can see today, so the refusal moved to the **continuation** — the only thing that silently drops rows — with a syntactic `IS NOT NULL` in the handler's own predicate as the accepted escape. |
| **R-1020-17 / OQ10** — protobuf screen contracts through wave 3, then decided on evidence | **D-1020-X3** (wave 3 close) | Criterion (a) tripped at **1.72×**. The ruling is to keep protobuf until a device answers (b) and (c), with the number recorded so it cannot be re-litigated and a second trip flipping it to sealed classes + SKIE with no further debate. R-1020-17 stands as written; this is its evidence and its ruling. |
| **Lane D3's and lane D1's** "did not fix in v0, because v0 is the pinned oracle" | **R-1020-35** (owner, 2026-09-12) | A correctness bug the port surfaces is fixed at its source, v0 included, with a red-first test and a receipt line per oracle edit. See [Decisions — lane V](#decisions--lane-v-1020). |

### Wave 1 lane rulings (#1020)

Lanes A (`crates/ontology`, `contracts/`) and B (`crates/xtask`, the gate workflows).

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-A1** — the expected file version is a contract, not a constant | `Vault::open` reads its expected `user_version` from `contracts/schema/v0-registries.json`, embedded via `include_str!`, rather than from a hard-coded `7`. Options: a constant in Rust; a window read from the exported fixture. Adopted the window: `userVersion` (7, from the golden manifest) and `ladderUserVersion` (11, `VAULT_MIGRATIONS.length`) are both exported, `Vault::open` accepts the closed window between them, and it answers `DowngradeRefused` above the head and `UpgradeRequired` below the corpus. A fifth test asserts the two ends are **distinct**, so the window cannot collapse to a point and pass vacuously. |
| **D-1020-A2** — `replica_change` is an era exclusion of the corpus, never a registry entry | The pre-migration corpus holds 44 rows in a table that is in no registry and is dropped by v0's rung ten ([R-1014-1](#replication-offline-and-sharing-1014)). Options: add it to the ported `SNAPSHOT_EXCLUSIONS`; declare it in the tests as an era exclusion. Adopted the second — the library's exclusion list still names `replica_meta` and nothing else, and the allowance lives in the two test files with its reason. Consequence worth writing down: re-freezing that corpus with today's exclusion list yields 17 tables, not 16. |
| **D-1020-B1** — inherited red is named, never hidden | `gitleaks` and `osv-scanner` move onto the new PR gate with the rest **even though both are red on tree state that predates this work**. A PR gate that stops reporting because its target is red today is a weakening, and a gate whose first act is to widen its own allowlist has gated nothing: nothing was added to `.gitleaks.toml` or `osv-scanner.toml`, and both fixes are owner hand-offs. |

Four further lane B rulings, recorded without ids because each is one consequence of R-1020-10 rather than an open question: the `ci.yml` change is **two inseparable edits** (adding `schedule:` without extending the `changes` job's `all` output would install a lane that skips every path-gated job and reports green); `dependency-review` is its own job in `gate.yml` because it reads the PR's dependency diff through the API, keeping `pull-requests: write` off the job that compiles third-party crates; `scripts/lint-workflow-pins.mjs` rule 5 reads **one** `PR_ENTRY_POINT` constant and refuses `ci.yml` like any other file, because an allowlist of two is how "exactly one" becomes "a few"; and `ts-static` is scoped to the v1 tree rather than being a second run of v0's static gate under a different name.

### Wave 2 lane rulings (#1020)

Lanes D3 (`crates/apps/{kit,tally}`), C (`crates/{api-proto,protocol,net,centraid}`), D1 (`crates/vault`), R (custody, backup, recovery) and D2 (`crates/{seat,core,core-ffi,sim}`). Lane V's and lane B2's rulings have their own sections below.

**Lane D3 — the app kit.** `D-1020-D3-1` … `D-1020-D3-9` were adopted as the lane brief states them; the three later waves cite are **D3-5** (`invoke_key` is mandatory on every command), **D3-6** (parity fixtures are generated by executing v0, never typed) and **D3-7** (a number measured on `ci-linux-x64-4c` is reported as _projected_ provenance, never as a ceiling). Five rulings the brief did not settle:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-D3-10** — a keyset page is not continued over a nullable sort column | SQLite compares a row value with a NULL operand to **NULL, not true** (`SELECT (NULL,'pk-4') > ('','pk-3')` answers NULL), so v0's continuation drops rows in three of the four (direction, boundary) cases and says nothing. Options: reproduce v0; refuse the continuation; build a typed, versioned cursor that can carry NULL as its own value. Adopted the refusal, with the cursor filed as a finding — a wrong ledger is worse than a refusal, and the cursor is a wire-format change belonging to the protocol lane. Narrowed by [D-1020-V1](#decisions--lane-v-1020). |
| **D-1020-D3-11** — the parity fixture is canonicalised **rows**, not a `vault.db.gz` | `bootstrapVault` mints the vault, the owner party and the first device as UUIDv7 off the clock, so a database file is not byte-reproducible and the idempotency gate could never pass; a compressed database is also not a diff anyone reads. Adopted rows plus the committed DDL, which stays the one copy of the model. |
| **D-1020-D3-12** — a bound and a window report the size they can reach | `MAX_PAGE_ROWS` clamps a page to 500, so `pageSize × fanOutPages` names twice the rows it stops at whenever `pageSize` exceeds 500, and a window asked for as one page reads a quarter of itself. Options: restate v0's arithmetic and reproduce both bugs; report the reachable cap and walk a stated window. Adopted the second. **A cap that lies about its own size is worse than a smaller cap.** |
| **D-1020-D3-13** — each app crate is a **named** workspace member | `"crates/apps/*"` does not work: `crates/*` also matches `crates/apps`, cargo refuses a glob match with no manifest, and excluding `crates/apps` excludes the whole subtree — an explicitly listed member is the only thing that overrides its parent's exclusion. Naming them also keeps the count visible in the workspace file, which a glob hides. |
| **D-1020-D3-14** — the parity generator's epoch is 2099 and host-clock instants are tokenised | The vault has **two clocks**: a handler stamps `ctx.now`, which a proxy can hold still, while a command's conditions are SQL and read SQLite's own `strftime('now')`, which no proxy reaches. With a frozen clock in the past, `tally.delete_expense` writes a `purge_at` already expired and its own postcondition refuses it, so the trash case cannot be fixtured at all. Adopted: tokenise the clock-dependent columns and move the epoch past the host's. |

**Lane C — the schema workspace, the protocol and the endpoint.** `D-1020-C1` … `D-1020-C11` were adopted as briefed. Five refinements:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-C12** — an intent's and a command's `input` are `bytes` carrying canonical JSON | Options: a proto message per action; `google.protobuf.Any`; opaque bytes. Adopted opaque bytes, for three reasons in order of weight. The input **is** the hash preimage — `intentPayloadHash` hashes the canonical JSON and the gateway compares in constant time, and protobuf serialisation is explicitly not canonical (map order, default elision, unknown fields all vary by runtime), so a proto body could not carry a payload hash at all. The input schema is per action and lives in the vault, so a message per action would put `buf breaking` in the way of adding a command. And opaque bytes cannot be silently re-encoded, which is what holds the unknown-field promise. The cost is that the core validates JSON rather than getting it from the decoder, paid once in `crates/vault`. |
| **D-1020-C13** — prost 0.14 does not preserve unknown fields, so the invariant moves to the frame | Verified rather than assumed: there is no `preserve_unknown_fields` switch and no `unknown_fields` member in the vendored crate. Options: dynamic messages at the envelope; hand-rolled retention; hold the invariant one layer out. Adopted the third — nothing in the v1 plane relays a _decoded_ message, every payload that crosses a version boundary is `bytes`, and an unknown message type is answered `Unsupported{type_url}`. The residual gap is named: a **field** added in a future release is invisible to this build, and a middlebox that decoded and re-encoded would lose it. A test turns red if prost ever gains the feature, which would be a welcome red. |
| **D-1020-C14** — `idle()` closes the endpoint and `resume()` re-binds it | Options: a flag that only stops the accept loop; close and re-bind; wait for an iroh pause API. The flag leaves the relay connection and its keepalives in place, which is precisely the battery cost [R-1020-25](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) exists to remove, so the rule would have been a comment. Adopted close-and-re-bind: identity is the secret key, not the endpoint object, so `Endpoint::id()` is unchanged across a cycle and only the addresses move. A dial while idle is refused rather than silently re-binding. |
| **D-1020-C15** — `PairTicket` carries `direct_addrs` | With `RelayMode::Disabled` and no address-lookup service, an EndpointId alone has nothing to be dialled through, so a LAN-only deployment could not pair at all. Options: require a relay for pairing; a second out-of-band channel; the addresses in the ticket. Adopted the ticket — they are **hints and never authority**, because iroh's TLS handshake proves the endpoint id, so a tampered address reaches either the right gateway or nothing. |
| **D-1020-C16** — the `buf` step treats "the base carries no `.proto` files" as a pass whose line says so | `buf breaking` exits non-zero with `had no .proto files` when the base has no schema, which is this step's answer on the commit that introduces one. Options: fail (the schema's first commit is unmergeable); skip until `main` has the tree (a gate that does not run); classify the case from what buf printed. Adopted the third, read off the step's own artifact, and it stops happening the moment `main` carries the schema. |

**Lane D1 — the authority's file.** Eighteen rulings; the ones stated "as briefed" are listed for completeness because later lanes cite them by number.

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-D1-1** | v1's baseline is v0's **current** shape: re-cut with v0's own freezer at the ladder head, copied byte-identically into `contracts/`, and `vault-ddl.sql` regenerated from it. The rung-7 rendering is replaced, not kept beside — one baseline. |
| **D-1020-D1-2** | A v1 file carries `application_id = 0x43454E31` and a `user_version` counting v1's **own** migrations. `crates/ontology`'s 7…11 window is a different question — it is about reading two frozen **v0** files — and stays. |
| **D-1020-D1-3** | One session per replicated table, and `table_filter` stays unused. `rusqlite` does expose it. Options: one filtered session (~1 ms cheaper per commit); one per table. Adopted one per table, for a reason stronger than caution: `node:sqlite` accepted the same filter and silently ignored it, and the failure mode is a private table's rows reaching every seat, invisible until someone reads a log. A table that was never attached produces **no changes at all**, which a test sees. |
| **D-1020-D1-4** | The file's own row images keep v0's JSON spelling; number spelling is borrowed from `centraid_ontology::jsvalue::js_number_to_string` rather than re-derived, because a real `1.0` is JSON `1` and Rust's own formatting prints `1.0`. A differently-spelled image would make every row a finding for a difference that is not one. |
| **D-1020-D1-5** | The commit pair is the only writable connection, and `Vault::read` holds `PRAGMA query_only` — which turns "no caller writes through a read" into "SQLite refuses it". `Vault::connection` is `pub(crate)`. |
| **D-1020-D1-6** | Doors are functions. |
| **D-1020-D1-7** | One content-addressed snapshot, three uses — **narrowed to two by [D-1020-R8](#wave-2-lane-rulings-1020)**, see the chain table above. `Fault` is a runtime enum on the shipped builder rather than a `#[cfg(test)]` hook, because a gate that only exists in test builds proves nothing about the shipped one. |
| **D-1020-D1-8** | `DiskFull` is typed, and classified in `From<rusqlite::Error>` rather than in a helper each call site calls. The first run of the disk-full test showed why: the error arrived through a handler's own `?`, not through the log plane's helper, and came out as a generic `Sqlite`. **A classification a call site can forget is a classification the product renders wrong.** |
| **D-1020-D1-9** | Authority and the command plane as briefed. `AllowlistStore` is `crates/vault::devices` with `enrol` / `revoke` / `is_device_enrolled` / `live_devices`, because `crates/net` was not on the umbrella at this lane's rebase; lane C's trait is implemented over it in one block. |
| **D-1020-D1-10** | The paged door's vault-side hook: `Vault::page_raw`, `page::and_row_filters`, `page::apply_field_mask`. The interface agreed with D3 is that **the kit builds the statement text and the binds and calls `and_row_filters` with the decision it got from `evaluate_access`; the vault never sees a `PageQuery` and the kit never sees a `Connection`.** |
| **D-1020-D1-11** | The three gates land as `contracts/` fixtures. |
| **D-1020-D1-12** | `replicatedTables` and `replicaConstants` go in the registry fixture. `REPLICATED_TABLE_NAMES` is **not exported** by `private-tables.ts` and the v0 tree is pinned, so adding an `export` was not available. Options: re-type the 109 names in Rust; derive them from a fresh vault's tables ∩ `isReplicatedTable`, which only yields the names a vault happens to carry; read the array literal out of the source text and put every parsed name back through the exported predicate. Adopted the third — it transcribes rather than re-types, and a mis-parse fails loudly in the generator rather than shipping a short allow-list. |
| **D-1020-D1-13** | The baseline migration and its `contracts/` fixture are **one file**, reached by `include_str!`. Two copies of a 6,460-line DDL is two answers to one question, and a diff test is a gate against a problem that need not exist. |
| **D-1020-D1-14** | `core.add_party` refuses a reach scheme **by name** rather than dropping it. v0's input schema admits `email` and `tel`, routes them to `social.save_contact_channel`, and `core_party_identifier`'s own CHECK refuses them. Options: accept and silently ignore; accept and bind anyway (the whole command then fails with a constraint error); refuse with a sentence naming the command that takes them. Adopted the third — a dropped identifier means the app believes it bound an email and nothing did. |
| **D-1020-D1-15** | `core_entity_kind` is **derived** from the DDL, not transcribed: an entity kind is exactly a logical name whose table declares `FOREIGN KEY (<pk>) REFERENCES core_entity(entity_id)`. The schema already answers the question, and the test holds the derivation to v0's own 52 names in both directions. A transcribed list's drift shows up as a foreign-key failure on one app's first insert, months later. |
| **D-1020-D1-16** | `export-applier-oracle.ts` runs under `node --experimental-strip-types`, not `bun`. Not a preference: v0's vault package is built on `node:sqlite`, which Bun does not provide. |
| **D-1020-D1-17** | A snapshot build cannot exhaust SQLite pages, so the disk-full test aims at the copy's **file write**. Measured, capping `max_page_count` cannot work: every step after the copy only frees pages, and SQLite clamps the cap **up** to the current size. Options: drop the test; a privileged tiny `tmpfs`; redirect the copy to `/dev/full`, which accepts an open and fails every write with ENOSPC so SQLite reports a genuine `SQLITE_FULL`. Adopted the third — privilege-free, deterministic, and the same error code the log-insert test reaches by a different route. |
| **D-1020-D1-18** | `serde_json`'s `float_roundtrip` feature is **on**. Found by a property test: the default parser is fast and approximate and landed one ULP away. A log row's images are re-parsed on every apply, so a drifting REAL is a seat whose latitude, or exchange rate, is quietly not the gateway's. |

**Lane R — key custody, backup, the recovery kit and the drill.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-R1** — formats are moved, not ported | `crates/media` carries `cbsf` (v2) and `format` as moved v0 source with v0's tests. Options: re-implement from the census; move the source; depend on the v0 crate. The v0 crate is excluded from the workspace and retires in wave 6. A re-implementation is a chance to **silently re-key every vault**, because every constant and every info/AAD string is a format decision and the golden only catches the four vectors it carries. Adopted the move, with a header line per file naming the origin. |
| **D-1020-R2** — three key layers, three AADs, never one helper | `custody::{keystore, seal, locker_key}`, each with its own wire form and AAD, faithful to v0 including the parts that look like rough edges. One `encrypt(key, aad, value)` helper reads better and is how a ciphertext becomes movable between rows: the AADs name different things, and one helper invites a caller to pass the wrong one. Upheld and extended by [D-1020-L4](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020). |
| **D-1020-R3** — a recovery kit in a member's drawer must stay openable, so v1 reads a v0-made kit | The general rule is that no v1 code reads a v0 artefact, and it is right for seat files and pairing tickets, which are re-made in a ceremony the owner is present for. A kit is not like that: it is written once and opened years later, on the day everything else is gone. **An upgrade that quietly invalidated the last copy of a vault's keys would be the single worst thing this umbrella could ship.** Adopted as the one exception, scoped to this file format and to the **reader** only. |
| **D-1020-R4** — the acceptance test in its wave 2 form, with what it does not prove said out loud | Every seal/unseal function takes the key by value, and the gate opens the vault file and the whole `keys/` directory with `K` withheld. Options: leave the acceptance box to wave 4; land the test now in the shape the wave 4 key will satisfy. Adopted the second, and stated plainly: **the gateway still holds `K` until wave 4.** What the test proves is that a sealed cell depends on `K` and on nothing else on disk — the property that makes wave 4 a change of custody rather than a change of format. |
| **D-1020-R5** — one policy, deterministic nonces, `backup now` as a command through the core | `BackupPolicy` keeps v0's defaults exactly, including the two provider-shaped fields v1 has no back-end for (`storageClass`, `directToColdOriginals`). Dropping them makes a v0 policy row unreadable and re-inventing the names later is worse than two dormant fields now; carried dormant, with a note. |
| **D-1020-R6** — `recover` takes its password from a file, and a bad `--at` is exit 2 | `--password` puts the last copy of a vault's keys in the shell history and in every `ps` listing; a prompt cannot be scripted, and a restore is often run by a script at 3am. `--at` is validated **before anything is touched**, because a script that retries a refusal must not retry a typo. |
| **D-1020-R7** — the drill runs the real binary, and the seat's half is played by D1's applier | `crates/seat` was not on the umbrella at this lane's rebase. Options: wait for lane D2; mark the drill `#[ignore]`; land it with the applier and name the gap. Adopted the third. **What it costs, named:** the drill proves the replica re-bootstraps and converges and that the old cursor earns `RebootstrapRequired{epoch-mismatch}`; it does **not** prove the iroh pairing handshake runs again. |
| **D-1020-R8** — a backup's base copy is not the seat snapshot | See the chain table above. |
| **D-1020-R9** — `export` lands its custody half and says what it is missing | v0's portable export also carried the blob bundle, which needs the content store. Options: exit 3 until it exists; ship the bundle silently without blobs; ship the custody half and print, on stderr and in the JSON (`"carriesContentBlobs": false`), that content blobs are not in it. The second is a bundle an owner would trust with their photos. Adopted the third. It also requires `--password-file`: there is no unwrapped export path, not as a flag, not as a prompt. |
| **D-1020-R10** — the SQL stays in `crates/vault`, which shaped two APIs | `sql-confinement` allows SQL only under `crates/{ontology,vault,seat,search}` and `crates/apps/kit`, and `crates/centraid` is not one of them. Options: widen the rule for the CLI; put the SQL where the rule allows it. Widening a gate to go green is the one thing this repository forbids, and the confinement produced the better shape anyway — the drill exercises the real process, which a library call would not. |

**Lane D2 — the seat, the core, the ABI and the simulation.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-D2-1** | The seat is the same crate family as the gateway, and **the applier's SQL is the gateway's**: `centraid_vault::log::apply::{apply_row_sql, delete_row_sql}` render the statements and `crates/seat` calls them. v0 had two hand-transcribed copies of the same four rules and they drifted; one renderer with two callers cannot. |
| **D-1020-D2-2** | `core` is the message loop: four entry points and nothing else. `open` opens the file, runs migrations and **returns**; the endpoint is a separate call. `call` is synchronous from the caller's view with a debug assertion against the shell's named UI thread. `next_event` is bounded at 1024 and drops nothing. `close` unblocks every waiter and later calls are typed errors. |
| **D-1020-D2-3** | `core-ffi` is five symbols and a contract: ten clauses in `CONTRACT.md`, one test per clause through the C entry points, `nm` over the built `cdylib` as a second and different question, a committed cbindgen header whose regeneration is asserted to be a no-op, and the marshalling confined to one module so miri can run over it. |
| **D-1020-D2-4** | The simulation is the sync proof: one gateway host, N seat hosts, a seeded schedule, seven invariants after every run, 25 seeds in `pr` and 250 in `nightly`, every seed that ever failed replayed for ever. Every host is real except the network. The alternative — mocks — proves the mocks agree. |
| **D-1020-D2-5** | The failure matrix is red-first: five modes, ten tests, four demonstrated reds. Process death and duplicate delivery are proved in `crates/sim`, where a real host crashes and restarts over a real file. |
| **D-1020-D2-6** | The `call` budget is p95 over 200 calls of one bounded read against a down-only ledger, run by `pr`'s `call-budget` step with `--nocapture`, so a number trending towards its ceiling is visible in the gate's log rather than only pass-or-fail. |
| **D-1020-D2-7** | The binding spike's host half measures **JNA and not** the Foreign Function & Memory API: FFM is final in JDK 22 and Android's minimum is nowhere near it, so an Android shell will use JNA or JNI for years, and measuring the binding the product will ship is the point of a spike. |
| **D-1020-D2-8** | Status codes are prefixed on both sides (`CENTRAID_OK`). Options: bare Rust names plus a cbindgen rename annotation — tried, and cbindgen 0.29 does not honour `cbindgen:rename` on constants; two spellings, one per language — refused, two names for one constant drifts; one prefixed name on both sides — adopted. A C preprocessor has a single namespace and a header that `#define`d bare `OK` would collide on the first project that included both. |
| **D-1020-D2-9** | One mutex over the vault, in wave 2. The issue's "reads run concurrently under SQLite's own rules" needs a pool of read connections, which needs `PRAGMA` statements, which is SQL — and `sql-confinement` does not admit `crates/core`. Options: a reader pool in `crates/core` (**refused**, it puts SQL where the rule does not allow it); a reader pool inside `crates/vault` (the right answer, and another lane's file, so it is an owner hand-off); one mutex, every call serialised (**adopted for wave 2**). The cost is bounded and measured — `call` p95 is 0.47 ms against a 50 ms ceiling — and `CONTRACT.md` clause 3 states the honest version rather than claiming concurrency the vault does not have. |
| **D-1020-D2-10** | The sync driver is production code, not the simulation's: `crates/seat/src/sync.rs` is written over `LogSource` and `IntentSink`, `crates/sim` implements them over turmoil and a real seat implements them over `crates/net`. A driver in the test crate would have made every simulation bug a bug in code no phone runs. The two traits are **separate** because they fail independently and the remedies differ: a seat whose log source is down is _stale_, one whose intent sink is down is _blocked_, and one "connection" trait would make them the same state. |
| **D-1020-D2-11** | The simulation speaks **UDP, not TCP**. turmoil's TCP is refused twice over: production is iroh, which is QUIC over UDP, and a TCP simulation would prove convergence for a transport no seat has — hiding reordering, datagram loss and the absence of head-of-line blocking, the three things a sync loop must survive; and the product **may not open a listening TCP socket**, so a crate whose source reads `TcpListener::bind` is a crate somebody copies from. An in-process channel was refused too: turmoil's partitions and latency would not apply and the schedule would be decoration. The cost is this crate's own datagram framing, which keeps the `u32BE(len)` prefix even though a datagram already has a length, because a truncated datagram must be refused for the same reason a truncated stream frame is. |
| **D-1020-D2-12** | The sync driver is async; `Handle::call` is not. A sync **pass** is network I/O, so `pass` is `async` and the two traits return boxed futures; `call` is synchronous because a shell asking one question wants one answer and should not need a runtime in Swift. The SQLite work between the awaits stays synchronous, so the applier never holds a transaction across an await point — the rule that keeps a cancelled future from leaving one open. |

### Decisions — lane V (#1020)

**R-1020-35** — **a correctness bug the port surfaces is fixed at its source, v0 included.** The "v0's TypeScript tree is a pinned oracle" invariant of [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) admits a narrow, fixture-proven edit **for a wrong answer**, and the umbrella receipt lists every such edit together with the test that was red before it. Ruled by the owner on 2026-09-12 on [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020): _"if you are finding any correctness bugs in the process, fix them please!"_

It supersedes two things recorded one wave earlier, both of which asked for exactly this ruling: the lane D3 owner hand-off that put its first finding — a dashboard reading a quarter of its own declared ledger window and folding a balance over it — to the owner as options (a)/(b)/(c) with (b) recommended, and the sentence above that lane's findings list which said each one "did not fix in v0, because v0 is the pinned oracle". Both are answered the same way: the fix is taken. Lane D1's hand-off on the same footing — a replicated commit that refuses any INTEGER above 2^53 — is answered by it too.

What the ruling does **not** license: a refactor, a style edit, a performance change, or any behaviour change that is not a wrong answer. Every commit under it names the wrong answer it removes and the test that showed it, and a regenerated fixture whose bytes move says which case moved and why ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)). An oracle edit with no red test in front of it is out of scope: what makes the edit safe is the fixture, not the argument.

### Decisions — lane B2 (#1020)

**D-1020-B2** — **the v1 feedback-time ledgers were re-based once, for a Rust workspace, by owner ruling.** Ruled by the owner on 2026-09-12 on [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020): _"given that we're migrating away from ts to rust/kotlin, the earlier budget migh not hold...adjust accordingly!"_ The wave-1 numbers in [`contracts/ledgers/`](../contracts/ledgers) were seeded from a workspace of **one** crate over clap and serde_json; it now holds **nine** over iroh, quinn, tokio, prost and rusqlite-bundled, and wave 2b adds six more. An owner ruling is the only legitimate way a number in a down-only ledger rises, and this is that ruling, spent once. Every raised number quotes it, its measurement and its multiplier in the ledger's own `headroom`; three numbers were **kept** because their measurement already sat under a third of the ceiling. The ledgers are down-only again from this baseline ([toolchain.md](toolchain.md#v1-cargo-xtask-gate-1020)).

**D-1020-B2-1** — **the `local` budget is scored on the tree the edit-run loop runs on: a warm one.** 120 s is the developer's feedback time after an edit, not the first build after a clone — on the reference container the two are 24.0 s and 1394.6 s. `cargo xtask gate` decides by asking whether **every** workspace member has a _linked_ artifact (a `.rlib` or an extensionless executable) in `target/debug/deps`; an `.rmeta` from a previous `cargo check` does not count, so a checked-only tree cannot buy a warm budget while its `test` step still has to compile and link everything, and a _stale_ incremental tree does read as warm on purpose, because that is the tree the loop runs on. A cold `local` run is charged against the new `coldLocalProfileSeconds` ceiling and fails when that key states none: being cold is never what makes a slow gate green. Only `local` has a cold ceiling — every other profile runs in CI on a runner that has never seen the workspace, so cold is its normal case and its budget holds cold or it is not a budget. The options considered were a marker file (gameable), "`target/debug` exists" (satisfied by a 5 s `cargo check`) and a mandatory `--cold` flag (an honesty system); artifact presence is the only one that cannot be satisfied without having done the work ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)). This supersedes lane B's reading that `local`'s 2 minutes covered a post-`cargo clean` run, and answers lane C's owner hand-off 2.

**D-1020-B2-2** — **a re-based number is measurement × a stated multiplier, never a chosen round number.** Each key was measured on the reference container at nine crates and its ceiling set from that measurement: multiplier 3 by default, tightened to **2** for the three large cold numbers (clean check, cold `local`, release build) because 3× of a number that size is a ceiling no plausible regression reaches, and a budget nobody can miss is a log; `pr` took **1.06** — the next round number above its measurement and no further — because it is the profile the rung table binds. `incrementalCheckSeconds` (10 s) is the one number whose meaning is the promise itself and it was **kept**, at 0.6 s measured; if a measurement ever forces it up, what is owed is a structural fix (mold or lld for the link, sccache for the dependency half), not a bigger number. The alternative — storing the measurement as a second ledgered number — was rejected for the reason the ledgers already state: a ceiling pinned to today's workspace fails the first time a crate lands, and the author learns to widen ledgers ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)).

**D-1020-B2-3** — **`pr` stays Rust-only at the rung-2 wall clock; Kotlin gets its own row.** `pr` rose 900 s → 1500 s, which is what its cold measurement (1420.1 s) needs and no more, recorded against #892's rung table ([G-lane-rules](#the-pr-gate-loop-892), 15/45/90/300 minutes): CI restores a Cargo cache, so the warm run (575.8 s) is the p95 the rung budgets and 1420 s is the cache-miss tail this ceiling must not be exceeded by. [`gate.yml`](../.github/workflows/gate.yml)'s job timeout rose to 40 minutes to sit above it, because a run the runner kills cannot report the budget it is scored against. Kotlin/Gradle JVM tests are **not** charged to `pr`: `gate-budgets.json` gains a `mobile-jvm` profile and `compile-time.json` a `kotlinNativeLinkSeconds` key, both **null**, both for wave 3 lane E to measure and set. A null is not a number and cannot be ratcheted, so this is a placeholder rather than a widen, and `cargo xtask gate --profile mobile-jvm` refuses with a line naming that lane instead of passing a profile with no steps ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)).

**D-1020-B2-4** — **the ratchet code stays exactly as strict; the exception is the ruling, not a mechanism.** No `approvedDeviation`, waiver or override path was added to `crates/xtask/src/ledger.rs`, and `a_risen_number_is_a_finding` still fails a raise against an existing base copy. The re-base passed the `ledgers` step for one reason, stated plainly: `origin/main` carries no `contracts/ledgers/` yet, so the base copy is `None` and every entry reads as new. Once [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) merges these numbers are the baseline and only fall — and the next lane that wants one raised needs an owner ruling, not a flag.

**D-1020-B2-5** — **the `secrets` step scans the repository, not its build output.** Under the owner's correctness ruling ([R-1020-35](#decisions--lane-v-1020)), lane C's owner hand-off 1: `gitleaks detect --no-git` gives the step its working-tree coverage and also walks `target/`, where a built Rust workspace reports PEM headers from `pem-rfc7468`/`pkcs8` doc strings inside `.rmeta` files. gitleaks 8.30 has no `--exclude-path` and no `.gitignore` support, and its one exclusion mechanism is the config allowlist — the file this step exists to keep honest. So the step keeps **one** unfiltered scan and classifies gitleaks' own JSON report afterwards: a finding is dropped only where `git check-ignore` matches the file **and** `git ls-files` does not track it, which is git's answer to "is this a file we wrote?" rather than an allowlist of secret patterns. Both counts are printed, `.gitleaks.toml` is untouched, and a tracked file is never dropped. The alternative — one `gitleaks dir` invocation per non-ignored top-level entry — was measured at 46.5 s against 2.7 s, because gitleaks compiles its ruleset per process, and buys nothing the report filter does not ([#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020)).

### Wave 3 lane rulings (#1020)

Lanes G (`deploy/`, the CI lanes, the prebuilt core), F (`desktop/`) and E (`mobile/`). Wave 3's own close, lane X3, has its own section below.

**Lane G — the deploy tree, the CI lanes, the prebuilt core and the release smoke.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-G1** — `deploy/` is the one home; the v0 image moves with history and keeps shipping | Options: rewrite the root `Dockerfile` into the v1 image in place; add a v1 image beside an untouched root `Dockerfile`; `git mv` the v0 image to `deploy/docker/gateway-v0.Dockerfile` and add `deploy/docker/Dockerfile` as the v1 one. Adopted the third. The first would have broken two shipping workflows, which is deleting a v0 lane by another name; the second leaves the deploy tree half-populated. A re-home, not a deletion. |
| **D-1020-G2** — the key and the stamp are two mechanisms, and the exact rule is written down | `cargo xtask artifact-key`'s five inputs, with `contracts/ledgers/` excluded and `Cargo.lock` reduced to its resolved graph, are in the module doc and in [release.md](release.md#the-v1-prebuilt-core-triples-keys-identity-symbols). The stamp lives in `crates/centraid` and **not** `crates/core` because lane D2 had not landed when it was written; the module names the move rather than implying it, and the JSON field names are already `gitSha`/`digest`/`schemaVersion` so the KMP assertion has a contract to write against today. |
| **D-1020-G3** — a CI-shape gate moves into the gate that replaced the workflow; it is never dropped | The advisory register reads **both** `contracts/ledgers/advisory.json` and `tests/inventory.json#advisory`, so there is exactly one row per step between the two files and neither carries a copy of the other's. Considered and rejected: duplicating the two v0 rows into the v1 ledger (two copies of one row drift), and leaving the rule in v0 only (it would come off pull requests with `ci.yml`). |
| **D-1020-G4** — a device lane is a loud skip with a runner contract, and the contract lives in the runner | `CENTRAID_DEVICE_RUNNER` flips `device-lanes` from skipping to **requiring** `xcrun devicectl list devices` (a physical iPhone — not `simctl`, which lists simulators) and `adb devices`. A runner that claims the label and has no phone goes **red**: that is an infrastructure fault, not a skip. `--lane <name>` narrows a run to one cell, and a name matching nothing is an error rather than an empty run that would report PASS over zero steps. |
| **D-1020-G5** — the release profile's four steps, and `vps-smoke`'s device-less form | `restore-drill` + `artifact-identity` + `prebuilt-core-required` + `vps-smoke`. The old placeholder test was replaced by `no_step_in_the_release_profile_is_a_stub`, which greps the gate source for the stub sentence — the rule was never about the message, it was that **the profile cannot pass vacuously**. |
| **D-1020-G6** — the flake stays honest without a fabricated pin | Options: install nix with a SHA-pinned third-party action; install it from a pinned release URL; do neither and check what is checkable. This repository has no pin for either installer shape, and **inventing one in the file whose whole subject is pinning** is the worst available outcome. Adopted the third: the `flake` job asserts that `flake.nix` reads the channel out of `rust-toolchain.toml` and carries no second literal of it, and states in the run summary that the flake has never been evaluated. The first evaluation is an owner hand-off. |
| **D-1020-G7** — the edit-run loop carries line tables, not full DWARF | Measured: **16 GB** under `debug/`, of which 7.7 GB was 144 test and bin executables. `[profile.dev]`/`[profile.test]` at `debug = "line-tables-only"` with `split-debuginfo = "unpacked"`, and `[profile.dev.package."*"] debug = false`, take the same set to **0.50 GB over 39 executables**, largest 43.1 MB. A backtrace still names file and line; `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=2 cargo test -p <crate>` restores the rest for one command. **The directory total does not fall until a clean** — cargo does not garbage-collect artifacts from a previous profile — so the honest number is the per-artifact one. |
| **D-1020-G8** — two optimised profiles, and the second reason is the one that survives | `release` (what a pull request builds and `release-build` scores) and `dist` = `release` + `lto = "thin"` + `codegen-units = 1` (what a tag publishes). Measured cold and contended: LTO + one codegen unit put `cargo build --workspace --release` at 1039 s against 379 s for the profile as it stands. The split was first made against the old 600 s ceiling; lane B2 has since re-based that to 1400 s, so 1039 s would now fit — and **the split stays on the argument that survives the number**: it spends ~70 % of the `pr` budget on inlining only the published artifact consumes. What it costs is stated too: the binary a pull request builds is not bit-identical to the one a tag ships, so `vps-smoke` smokes `release` and the `dist` artifact is proved by the prebuilt-core lane, which builds it, stamps it and reads the stamp back **out of the binary** before publishing. |
| **D-1020-G9** — the WAL capture tick runs in the gateway and its pending tail is on disk | Options: an in-process buffer (`centraid backup now` is a separate process and could not see it); the CLI captures the tail itself (a second capturer with its own idea of where the last segment ended); the gateway appends one line per tick to `<data-dir>/wal/pending.jsonl` and writes the sealed bytes to the blob store, and `backup now` retires it **after** the manifest naming those segments is written. Adopted the third. A crash between the blob write and the index append leaves an orphan blob rather than a manifest pointing at a blob that is not there — a blob nothing points at is garbage; the other way round is a generation that cannot be replayed. |
| **D-1020-G10** — a redemption waits for the peer's close | `IrohConnection::closed()` before the ticket is burned, so a redemption that the peer never received does not consume a one-time ticket. |

**Lane F — the desktop seat.** `D-1020-F1` … `D-1020-F8` were adopted as briefed; nine rulings the lane had to make:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-F9** — the seat socket carries **two** channels in one frame, and the local one is not in `centraid.core.v1` | A frame is `u32BE(len) ‖ body` with the first body byte a channel tag: `0x00` is a `centraid.core.v1.Envelope` byte-identical to what crosses iroh, `0x01` is one UTF-8 JSON local message. Adding the local messages to `centraid.core.v1` was refused because every one of them names something that cannot exist on a remote wire (a peer uid, a byte offset into a file this process can see, a capability token for a child on this machine), and that package is under a FILE promise to seats that update on their own schedule; a second protobuf package was refused because it needs a generator in the desktop build for three message types. What it costs is that a reader has to know about the tag, which `desktop/README.md` states. |
| **D-1020-F10** — the renderer sees JSON, not protobuf-es, and `desktop/**` is outside the bun workspace | Neither `@bufbuild/protobuf` nor `@bufbuild/protoc-gen-es` is installed, and adding them means editing the root `package.json` and `bun.lock` — shared files, and a codegen step in a tree whose whole point is that it needs none. Options: install both and generate; hand-roll a protobuf decoder in TS (two copies of a wire format); main speaks the local channel only, the core channel is protobuf and is exercised from Rust, and the JSON shapes are pinned from both sides. Adopted the third. Consequence: `desktop/**` and `extension/**` are not bun-workspace members and not rows in the repository-wide vitest project list — that list drives the v0 coverage run scored against `tests/floors.json`, and adding a tree to it moves coverage numbers for reasons unrelated to the oracle it measures. The v1 gate runs both suites by name. |
| **D-1020-F11** — reads are **named** statements from a catalogue the sidecar holds, never composed by the caller | Options: carry v0's shape and let the renderer send a `PageQuery` — refused, the renderer is the part of this product that runs app-authored JSX, so a read it can compose is a read an app can compose, and the peer check says _who_ rather than _what_; a per-statement message type — a protocol change per app; a name, resolved against the app crates' own statement functions. Adopted the third, pinned in `contracts/desktop/socket-catalogue.json`, which `centraid seat --print-catalogue` must reproduce byte for byte. |
| **D-1020-F12** — "not yet" is its own outcome, and the HTTP answer is a retryable 503 | A range inside a declared total whose bytes have not landed is not unsatisfiable and is not satisfiable either. Answering 416 was refused and this is the whole point: **a media element that gets a 416 stops asking, permanently.** Holding the request open was refused because a stalled transfer would hold a media request forever. Adopted: the seat waits a bounded 5 s on the writer, answers the prefix short if any of it is there, and otherwise answers `still-arriving`, rendered as `503` with `Retry-After: 1` and `Content-Range: bytes */<total>`. `416` is reserved for a range past a **settled** size. |
| **D-1020-F13** — one resolved range becomes as many frames as it takes, and the seat reports the range it resolved | `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` is 262,144 and the local channel base64-encodes bytes, so one frame carries at most 128 KiB of blob. Answering with that one window and an honest `Content-Length` would tell a `<video>` the file is 128 KiB long — which it believes, once, forever. So `BlobBytes` carries `req_end`, the last byte of the range the **sidecar** resolved before the frame ceiling clamped it, and the response body is a stream that pulls the rest. The shell cannot re-derive `req_end`, because the `Range` grammar lives only in the sidecar. |
| **D-1020-F14** — a per-turn capability token is minted by the shell, is single-use, and is clamped to ten minutes | Admitting any local process of this uid was refused: uid says _who_, not _which program_, and a browser-launched host runs as the member too. A long-lived token in a file is a bearer on disk. Adopted: the renderer asks, the seat mints 32 bytes of `/dev/urandom` as hex, and the token is **removed from the live map on redemption** — so a token read out of a child's `/proc/<pid>/environ` by a second child of the same uid buys nothing. A token minted for `native-host` does not admit `mcp`, and a child may not mint. |
| **D-1020-F15** — the Windows named-pipe door is an owner hand-off, and `centraid seat --socket` refuses to run there | Writing the DACL unobserved was refused: **a DACL that was written but never seen to refuse anybody is a claim, not a protection**, and this container has no Windows machine. Opening an unprotected pipe with a warning was refused. Adopted: exit 3 with the hand-off named in the message, so a Windows build fails loudly rather than serving a vault over a pipe nobody checked. |
| **D-1020-F16** — mounting the v0 app-inline components is deferred, and the parity claim is not deferred with it | Those components take v0's `@centraid/client` context — a gateway HTTP client, an SSE change feed, and the design-token provider — so mounting them is adapting _that context_, not a data source. Options: mount them and stub the context (a stubbed change feed is a screen that silently never updates); port the components (a second copy of eight apps); ship the screens over the same named reads and keep the parity claim by comparing the fold against the generated fixture field by field. Adopted the third, named as a hand-off in `desktop/README.md`. |
| **D-1020-F17** — the e2e's fixture video is committed, not generated | The claim is about serving a blob whose bytes are still arriving; a test that first has to encode a video depends on an encoder the runner may not have. The generator is committed beside the file so it is reproducible, and nothing in CI runs it. |

**Lane E — the KMP mobile shell.** `D-1020-E0` (Gradle for `mobile/`, over the Kotlin Toolchain and Amper, because AGP + XCFramework + SPM integration is mature there) and `D-1020-E2` … `D-1020-E8` were adopted as briefed; `D-1020-E4` is cited by [client-keying.md](client-keying.md) as the rule that makes a placeholder `(gatewayId, vaultId)` unrepresentable rather than merely forbidden. Eight rulings the lane had to make:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-E0-1** — the toolchain is current stable, not the container's | Gradle 9.7.1 via the committed wrapper with `distributionSha256Sum`, Kotlin 2.4.20, AGP 9.4.0, Wire 7.0.1, Kotest 6.2.5, Turbine 1.2.1, kover 0.9.9, Konsist 0.17.3, JNA 5.19.1, coroutines 1.11.0. The container's preinstalled Gradle 8.14.3 / Kotlin 2.0.21 printed _"Deprecated Gradle features were used … incompatible with Gradle 9.0"_ on the first run. Adopted current stable with `org.gradle.warning.mode=fail`, on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-12, amending D-1020-E0. **Its cost is that a Gradle or plugin upgrade reds here before anywhere else, which is the point.** |
| **D-1020-E1a** — the Android target is configured by **name**, not through typed accessors | Declaring AGP in `plugins {}` with `apply false` resolves it from `google()` on every configuration of this build including on machines with no SDK — a download to reach a failure. A precompiled convention plugin in an included build is the mature answer and doubles the setup. Adopted: the root `buildscript` classpath conditionally, and the `androidLibrary` extension configured by name with `withGroovyBuilder`, which is not type-checked here — with the trade written into the build file, because that block is proved by the owner hand-off and nowhere else, and a silently-skipped Android target would have been worse. |
| **D-1020-E1b** — cinterop commonization **on**, klib cross-compilation **off** on Linux | `iosMain` needs commonization to see the one `centraid` klib across three iOS targets — it is what makes the iOS actual a single file — but processing a cinterop needs an Apple toolchain, and with cross-compilation on, a Linux `:core:compileKotlinJvm` **fails** rather than simply not building iOS. Adopted: keep commonization and set `kotlin.native.enableKlibsCrossCompilation=false`, with the macOS hand-off passing `-P…=true`. |
| **D-1020-E3a** — every screen message goes in the existing `screen.proto`, one file | `crates/api-proto/build.rs` carries a named list of every `.proto` and a test asserts the list and the directory agree; both are lane C's files. For a shell-internal package with a PR-base-only compatibility promise, one file costs nothing and keeps the change inside the boundary. |
| **D-1020-E5a** — `.xcode-version` stays `16.4`, unchanged, and the confirmation is the hand-off | Raising it on a machine that can neither run `xcodebuild` nor link Kotlin/Native is a guess dressed as a decision. Adopted: confirm the existing pin and name the exact command that settles it. If Kotlin/Native refuses that Xcode, the refusal names the version it wants and that number goes in the file. |
| **D-1020-E6a** — the emitter splits `NativeColors` into colours and effects | Three of `packages/design`'s 54 `NativeColors` entries are CSS `box-shadow` strings and five more are `rgba()` functions. Emitting them as opaque strings makes `colors` a map a `Color(Long)` cannot consume; inventing a native elevation grammar is a design decision this lane does not get to make. Adopted: parse the `rgba()` colours into channels, split the three shadows into a named `effects: Map<String, String>`, and file the design question. |
| **D-1020-E7a** — the accessibility lint is a source scan over both surfaces, not Konsist | Compose's `contentDescription` is a call argument and Konsist reads declarations; the Swift half has no Kotlin AST at all. Adopted one regex scanner that strips comments, asserts its own file count, and fails with the file and the call site — and its advertised failure mode, a false positive somebody fixes rather than a false negative nobody sees, **fired immediately** and was fixed by measuring over code instead of over comments. |
| **D-1020-E7b** — kover excludes generated code from its report | The Wire and design-emitter packages are four times the hand-written source; including them put line coverage at **41.2 %** against a 70 % floor and made the number a measurement of two code generators. Lowering the floor to fit is weakening a gate. Adopted the exclusion, stated in the build file and in `mobile/README.md`; coverage after it is 86.8 % of lines. |

### Decisions — wave 3 close, lane X3 (#1020)

**D-1020-X3** — **open question 10's criterion (a) has tripped, and the ruling is to keep protobuf until a device answers (b) and (c).** Ruled 2026-09-12 under the standing delegation ([R-1020-34](#decisions--lane-v-1020)), over [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020) open question 10 and its **Amendment 3** exit criterion.

_The evidence._ Criterion (a) is "proto plus builder code for a screen exceeds 1.5× the lines of the equivalent Kotlin data classes, averaged over the three screens". Measured by wave 3 lane E against compiled throwaway sealed classes with the same fields, the same nesting and the same three-state content union, with Wire's generated output excluded from both sides: **1.72×** — Tally list 1.47×, Photos grid 2.00×, Notes editor 1.68×. The count understates protobuf's cost, because it excludes the nineteen `.textproto`/`.bin` fixture pairs, their manifest and the `buf convert` step that keeps them current, none of which sealed classes would need. Criteria (b) — a SwiftUI/Compose disagreement on a shared state fixture more than once — and (c) — Kotlin→Swift delivery over 1 ms at p95 on the reference iPhone at 50,000 assets — are both device measurements and **neither has been taken**: there is no Xcode, no simulator and no device on the machine wave 3 ran on.

The issue's exit criterion says switch if **any** of the three trips. One has.

_The options._ (i) **Switch now**: lane E's successor rewrites the three screen contracts as Kotlin sealed classes with SKIE in wave 5, and only `mobile/iosApp`'s wrapper changes on the Swift side. (ii) **Keep protobuf until (b) and (c) are measured on a device**, then decide on all three. (i) honours the criterion as written and stops paying a cost that is already known. Against it: (a) was written expecting to be the _weakest_ of the three signals, and the thing being paid for is not line count — it is one language-neutral fixture driving both Roborazzi and swift-snapshot-testing, which is exactly what (b) tests and what has not been tested. Switching on the one criterion that does not measure the benefit would be deciding the question with the evidence the machine happened to be able to produce.

_Adopted: (ii), with a hard date and no second debate._ The **device run decides**, and the number is recorded here so it cannot be re-litigated from memory: 1.72× is over the threshold and is not in dispute. A **second** trip — on (b) or on (c) — flips the answer to sealed classes + SKIE with no further discussion, and (a) counts as the first trip. If the owner's iOS session produces (b) and (c) both clean, protobuf stays and 1.72× is the price of the shared fixture, stated. If the session does not happen by the time wave 5 opens the screen contracts for any other reason, (i) is taken then: a criterion that trips and is never acted on is a criterion that was decoration, and the whole point of fixing it before wave 3 was that evidence would decide rather than sunk cost.

_What this does not change._ R-1020-17 stands as written; this is its evidence and its ruling, not a supersession. The commands that produce (b) and (c) are in [`mobile/README.md`](../mobile/README.md)'s hand-off table, and the four evidence file names are fixed in `mobile/maestro/ios-transfer-experiment.md`.

### Wave 4 lane rulings (#1020)

One lane per app plus `crates/assist`, `crates/automations` and `extension/`. The connectors lane was put on the **back burner by the owner on 2026-09-12** and is not in this wave; `sync_*` token custody is untouched and stays host-readable by design ([R-1020-9](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020)).

**Lane assist — the assistant plane.** `D-1020-AS1` … `D-1020-AS7` were adopted as briefed; **D-1020-AS2** is the one later lanes build on: there is **no MCP listener** — `centraid mcp` is a stdio child that connects to the local seat socket with a per-turn token, which is what [D-1020-F14](#wave-3-lane-rulings-1020) mints. Three refinements:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-AS8** — the ledger band needed no migration | Options: write `contracts/migrations/002_ledger.sql`; discover that the band is already on **rung one** of the baseline — it is, because the baseline was exported from a founded v0 vault — and hold the baseline to `ledger.ts`'s shape with assertions instead. Adopted the second. A second migration creating tables the baseline already has is either a no-op or a divergence, and the real risk is the opposite one: a future migration silently dropping a trigger or widening a CHECK. So the schema module is names and closed vocabularies — 14 tables, 2 indices, 8 triggers, 1 view, 1 FTS virtual table — and a test fails if any of them leaves. |
| **D-1020-AS9** — the ACP session fixtures are a live fake `Agent`, not recorded `.jsonl` | Options: record frames from v0 and replay them in Rust; ship `fake-acp-harness`, a real ACP `Agent` binary with four modes, and run the client half against it over a real pipe. Adopted the second. A replayed frame log tests the mapper and _asserts the framing it was recorded with_; it cannot fail when `initialize` stops negotiating, when notifications arrive out of order, or when the framing changes under a version bump — and those are the failures that break a turn. The cost is named: **no recorded v0 wire bytes are committed**, so a divergence between v0's frames and the crate's would not be caught here. |
| **D-1020-AS10** — `preflight` probes the harness CLI | Options: keep the `LaunchPlan` signature and special-case the two adapter kinds inside `run`; change `VersionProbe::version` to take the CLI path and the environment explicitly. Adopted the second, because the first puts a `match kind` outside the registry — exactly what D-1020-AS1 forbids — while the second makes the type carry the distinction [harnesses.md](harnesses.md) states. |

**Lane Photos — the library as a projection.** `D-1020-P1` … `D-1020-P8` were adopted as briefed and are recorded against their tests in `crates/apps/photos/README.md`; **D-1020-P5** keeps the **Places facet inside Photos** (there is no `crates/apps/places`: a location is a phrase before it is a pin, and in a shared context the relative rung is skipped because "5.2 km NW of Home" hands a stranger a bearing to the member's house). Four further rulings:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-P9** — the bundle is one canonicalisation pass over one vault, and the host clock is one flat parenthesised token | A token per file makes cross-file comparison impossible. Numbering host instants chronologically was **built first and rejected as not idempotent**: two rows share a millisecond in one run and not the next, the distinct count moves, and every later number shifts. Adopted one flat token, parenthesised because `(` sorts below every digit under BINARY collation and the host clock is below the 2099 epoch — so an undated asset still rides the end of a newest-first page, where the real instant put it. An angle-bracketed token sorts _above_ the digits and moved those assets to the front of every page. Cost: no ordering among host instants, which the reading statement breaks on a UUIDv7 primary key, and a test proves that tie-break is v0's order rather than assuming it. |
| **D-1020-P10** — the keyset case's cursor is the newest dated asset's `taken_at`, not the page's own `tail` | `tail` is `captured_at ?? created_at` of the last live row and three of the seed's assets carry no `captured_at`, so the tail is a host instant and feeding it back would make the case's _input_ unreproducible. Options: drop the page-boundary case; give the undated assets a capture time, distorting v0's corpus; cursor at a dated instant mid-roll. Adopted the third — a real keyset read at a real boundary, at an instant the fixture owns. |
| **D-1020-P12** — `representations` lands in `crates/apps/photos`, not in the kit | A `contracts/handoff/photos/kit.patch` is a patch no test in this lane could run against; the kit is another lane's file this slot. A fixture-proven module in the app is worth more than a patch nobody has applied, with the lift named as a hand-off. |
| **D-1020-P13** — the generator is split at the line ceiling rather than the ceiling raised | `oxlint`'s `max-lines` (625) refused the 844-line generator. Options: an inline disable; raise the rule; split. Adopted the split, into what a case and a row set **are**, which handler at which input, and the run. **Never weaken policy to go green.** |

**Lane X3 — the cross-lane fixes wave 3 surfaced.** See [Decisions — wave 3 close, lane X3](#decisions--wave-3-close-lane-x3-1020) above for `D-1020-X3`; the same lane's four other rulings:

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-X3-2** — ids are minted from the injected clock, not persisted as a sequence | Options: persist the counter in `core_vault.settings_json` or a `vault_ids` row under the commit that consumed it; mint UUIDv7 from the injected `Clock` with a per-open random suffix. The first makes every id mint a write that has to be transactional with the commit that used it, and a crash between the two either loses ids or reissues them; it also gives the sequence a schema and therefore a migration. Adopted the second — no state at all, matching what v0's `bootstrapVault` already does, and deterministic for the fixtures and the simulation **by injection rather than by default**. |
| **D-1020-X3-3** — the owner-facing sentence is built from the error code alone | Options: a `sentence` field each raising layer fills; derive it from `code` in one function and let no caller pass text. The first is one careless `format!` away from the bug returning, and the bug is **a member reading SQL**. Adopted the second: `sentence_for_code` is the only writer, `detail` keeps the predicate for the audit trail, and a sweep asserts no code's sentence is SQL-shaped. |
| **D-1020-X3-4** — the handshake's identity is checked at `open`, against a digest the caller states | Options: the shell reads `Hello.identity` after `open` and decides; the caller states `expectedIdentity` in the `open` config and the core refuses a mismatch before a handle exists. The first leaves a window in which a shell has a working handle onto a core it would have rejected. Adopted the second, with the first still possible because `Hello` carries the identity either way. |
| **D-1020-X3-5** — no second `call` ceiling was written | Lane E's brief proposed a 3,600 µs ledger ceiling for the JVM binding; E's own report then asserted the JVM path against the **same 50 ms** ceiling the Rust side is held to, on the grounds that a binding needing a relaxed ceiling is a binding the product cannot ship. Writing 3,600 µs would have created a second, looser ceiling for the same operation. **Nothing written**, and the reason recorded. |

**Lane Tally-finish — `crates/design`, the 29 cases, the 23 commands, `copy/`.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-T1** — `crates/design` is one lowering of `packages/design`, generated, never retyped | Options: retype the three functions in Rust and test them against hand-written cases; emit a corpus from the real TypeScript and assert every row; drop the presentation fields from the fixture. The third means editing a generated fixture and the first means two hue wheels that each look right. Adopted the corpus — the real `partyHueKey`/`partyHueValue`/`identityInitials` and Tally's `figureTone` over 252 hostile rows. Lane E's emitter was **extended, not duplicated**: one command still writes every artifact. |
| **D-1020-T2** — the 29 cases are compared whole | One stated exception: a `recurring` row's `preview` and `next_start` are v0 civil-time answers the schedule lane owns. They are named in one constant and asserted **present** in the fixture, so the deferral cannot widen quietly and cannot hide a regression in the ported fields. |
| **D-1020-T2a** — the expenses projection is v0's thirteen columns exactly, and that is a finding rather than a choice | v0's `loadTally` selects thirteen columns, leaving `settlement_currency`, `original_amount_minor`, `original_currency` and the four `rate_*` columns undefined in every row the dashboard folds — so a JPY expense is labelled with the vault's base money and `rateSuggestions` can never produce a row. Options: select the columns in the port (silently right, uncomparable); reproduce v0 and file the finding. **Reproduced.** |
| **D-1020-T3** — the 23 commands are the stubs made real, in the vault | v0's preconditions and postconditions as `CommandCondition`s bound to `:ctx_now`, D1's idempotency and risk, two command-level `confirm`s kept separate from the manifest's seven confirmations, and `online_only` reproducing v0's Tally list exactly (one command). |
| **D-1020-T3b** — three commands refuse rather than fake | `tally.add_receipt_expense` needs the staged-blob, content and enrichment planes, and the occurrence halves of the two recurrence commands need the civil-time expander. Options: write the expense and drop the photo; write a minimal RRULE expander; refuse with a sentence naming the lane that owns the plane. **Refused** — a member who believes their receipt was filed is worse than a refusal, and a second recurrence engine is the drift [ONT-25](#one-vault-every-seat-996) was filed for. |
| **D-1020-T3c** — effects are proven by replay, not by typing rows | `commands.json` records v0's own 20-step script with every minted id as a reference to the step that produced it, and the Rust test replays it and compares table for table, modulo ids and host instants, because two runs mint different ids and nothing in the product depends on which. |
| **D-1020-T3d** — the app's real `Commands` door is behind a `vault-door` feature | Off by default, so an edit in `crates/apps/tally` rebuilds one crate and the in-memory test impl still serves the unit tests. |
| **D-1020-T5** — `copy/<app>.json` has exactly one writer | The leaves are read as **text**, not imported: some are import-free by design and the rest would pull a `.tsx` app frame into a build script. Route ids travel with the sentences and `crates/design`'s `copy::route_gaps` names either gap, because a route id in one side and not the other renders a silent empty string. |
| **D-1020-T6** — the eight client-side models stay unported | They are form arithmetic and surface state, not the ledger. The one that was a bug — a module-level line-id counter — was already fixed in v0 by lane V, and the Rust port mints from `Ids`. |
| **D-1020-T7** — the match pairing fold is extracted and asserted over constructed rows | `matches` is the one answer of the eight no generated fixture can reach: v0's seed holds one `core_transaction` row, a bucket of one proposes nothing, and a second account with a second transaction is the finance plane's writer rather than any of Tally's 23 commands. Hand-written rows in a generated fixture are forbidden and would not be v0's answer either, so the fold was **extracted** as a pure function over the rows the two statements returned, and every refusal is a named assertion. |

**Lane automations — `crates/automations`.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-AU1** — the spine keeps its one injection point, and the watch list becomes structural | Dispatch: inlining the harness call is what a straightforward port does and deletes the boundary; a closure per fire; **adopted** `&dyn centraid_assist::turn::Dispatch` behind a forwarding newtype, so every dispatch passes the consent question in one place. The loop guard: port the eleven-name list (what the census predicts forgetting); a name list plus a test that it covers the band (the same, with a reminder); **adopted** a `Watchable` newtype that cannot be constructed from a string, resolved against the ontology registry. Under it the ledger band has no registered entity at all, so a table added to it is refused the day it is created. |
| **D-1020-AU2** — cron resolves in the vault's zone; tier 3 is deleted | Options: port all three tiers and document the VPS hazard (a schedule silently wrong for every member outside UTC); keep tier 3 but read the zone from an env var (the wrong answer behind an environment nobody reads); **adopted** two tiers and a typed `ZoneUnset` surfaced as a system signal, with `jiff`'s `tz-system` feature **off** so the host's zone is unreachable rather than merely unused, and `tzdb-bundle-always` so the answer does not depend on a host's `tzdata`. Carries [R-1020-33](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020). |
| **D-1020-AU3** — the enrichment gate, verbatim, and the name said out loud | "Resource mode" names nothing in the tree; the module is `fire::enrich_gate` and its header says so. The rule, the lanes and `seal_model_turns` are v0's. **An omitted lane reads as `gateway`**, written down in `EnrichLane::default()`, because assuming the cheaper lane would be assuming consent. |
| **D-1020-AU3-1** — the third execution site is a **fourth tier value**, not a re-meaning | `off(0) < on-device(1) < sealed-gateway(2) < gateway(3)`, with `device` read forward as `sealed-gateway` and never written back. Declined: re-meaning `device`, which changes a stored consent without asking; and splitting the axis into `tier × site`, the cleanest model and the worst migration — four combinations of which one is nonsense, and a second comparison in every gate. |
| **D-1020-AU3-2** — an unreachable holder is **parked, not failed** | It writes no `enrich_target_failure` row: the target is parked by the _absence_ of a lease. Without the test that says so, the first incident report is "my library stopped being recognised" and the failure table is where the answer hides. |
| **D-1020-AU3-3** — the cursor still advances past a parked target | One frontier, so the progress line has one number, plus a parked set re-examined out of band. A cursor that waited on one device would stop recognition for the whole library. |
| **D-1020-AU3-4** — the stamp's `site`/`device_id`, `EnrichAsk` and `enrich.submit_derivation` are root-integrated | They move `centraid.core.v1`'s wire, a new command and the shell's own service together, and none of the three is one lane's. Named so the vocabulary is not mistaken for a shipped device lane. |
| **D-1020-AU4** — one execution boundary, and the models behind a trait | Options: `ort` sessions in this crate, which makes it unbuildable without weights and its tests dependent on a download; a stub returning fixed output, which reads green and proves nothing; **adopted** a `Model` trait with `FakeModel` driving the same handler code, and each real session an owner hand-off with its exact command. `NoNetwork` proves the "reported, never thrown" property through every line of `ensure` except the socket, and `ctx.fetch` answers a typed `NotAvailable` naming the connectors plane rather than being half-built. |
| **D-1020-AU5** — webhook ingress without a listener | Options: a `blob-door`-style feature-gated listener, which re-introduces the surface the invariant exists to keep off a member's machine for a trigger kind no bundled recipe uses; polling a relay the OAuth worker holds, which is right for connectors and connectors are on the back burner, so it would be a rail with nothing at either end; **adopted** a seat delivery (`centraid automations deliver`, or the gateway's dialled iroh endpoint from a paired phone). The listener is recorded as the future option **under the same flag as the HTTPS blob door**, so it can only ever land where the invariant already makes an exception. |
| **D-1020-AU6** — steering and anchors | Steering is the `ctx.delegate` rail at `failover: fire-boundary`, and a failover rung **carries no manifest provider pin** — a model id from one vendor means nothing to another. A consent refusal is not failed over, because every rung is a provider and the member's answer was about providers. `collapse_scopes` **refuses** a non-rectangular set rather than granting the cross pairs. |
| **D-1020-AU7** — signals are values, and the wire arm is a hand-off | Options: add a `SignalEvent` arm to `centraid.core.v1` from this lane, a shared file two lanes were building against; squeeze signals through `HealthEvent`, making a member-facing fact wear a queue sample's shape; **adopted** a typed `Signal` with a `SignalSink` trait, and the proto arm as a hand-off patch with its demonstrated red. The crate logs **nothing** instead of signalling. |
| **D-1020-AU8** — the manifest parser is v0's real shape, not a reduced one | The first port modelled `{id, name, enabled, instructions, triggers}`; generating the parity fixture showed v0 requires `name`, `prompt` and a `generated` block and refuses four **couplings** a shape check cannot see — a condition or data trigger needs a `vault` block, an event trigger needs a bound connection of its kind, `requires.secrets` is connector-only, and a connector needs somewhere to stage. Under the reduced shape all four would have been discovered by a member whose automation type-checked and did nothing. |

**Lane Schedule — Agenda, Tasks, and the one recurrence engine.** Two apps over one `schedule_*` plane and one 16-command schema, so they landed as one lane.

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-S1** — one rrule parser, three call shapes | `assert_supported` at write boundaries, `inspect` for surfaces that report, and `expand` returning **nothing** on an unsupported rule rather than a plausible-but-wrong series. v0 has three implementations of the same subset in three files; this is one, and a generated corpus of 244 cases is what proves the three shapes agree. One deliberate strengthening: `schedule.propose_event` calls `assert_supported` where v0 only prefix-checks. |
| **D-1020-S2** — the DST policy is shared with cron | Nonexistent wall time is skipped; an overlapping wall time occurs **once, at the earlier instant**; an exception keys off the unmodified original occurrence. One implementation, called by both cron and recurrence, so the three sentences in [cron-timezone.md](cron-timezone.md) cannot drift apart between the two halves. |
| **D-1020-S3** — the occurrence key is the **wall clock**, storage-enforced | `original_start_local` is named in exactly one place in the tree and every reader goes through `expand`. The proof is a number: a daily 09:00 series in `Asia/Kolkata` across a US spring-forward has 18 occurrences and one skip on the boundary day. Keyed on the wall clock the skip matches and **17** remain; keyed on the resolved instant it matches nothing and **18** remain — an occurrence the member deleted, back on the calendar. That is drift ONT-25 made into a number. |
| **D-1020-S4** — `upcoming` reads two windows | Non-recurring events from before `from` for multi-day spans, plus recurring anchors under their own cap; expand; re-apply the true lower bound; `MAX_TOTAL_INSTANCES` checked **mid-expansion**. |
