| **D-1020-S5** — the board | Open tasks due-first, then priority desc, then title, then primary key; subtasks nested; **an unfinished child of a finished parent is promoted to a root** rather than disappearing with it; the 50 most recently closed as the logbook; `truncated` is the open page's own cursor. Nullable sort columns take [D-1020-PE5](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)'s adopted order, nulls last then primary key. **No module-level ceiling was added above `board.limit`**, because v0 has none and a refusal v0 lacks is a port bug rather than a hardening. |
| **D-1020-S6** — the two destructive verbs stay ungated, **as a question** | The brief recommended adding manifest confirmations to `cancel-event` and `delete`. **They were not added.** A manifest confirmation is a product decision with a UI consequence, and the lane's evidence for one was "v0 does not have it", which is an observation and not a finding. The honest output is the question, and it is in [release/v1-handoffs.md](release/v1-handoffs.md#the-owner-questions). |
| **D-1020-S7** — task lifecycle is a **vault operation**, not the app's | `complete`, `reopen`, `cancel`, the recurrence rollover and the successor's link inheritance live in `crates/vault::operations::task_lifecycle`, because Notes' _send to tasks_ and the extension's `capture:task` reach them without going through the app. This makes drift ONT-27 — "completion is one operation" — true structurally rather than by convention, and it is what closes [D-1020-PE9](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)'s refusal with a real owner. |
| **D-1020-S8** — one zone source, and it moved **down** | `FireZone` was the automations crate's and recurrence needs the same zone. It is now `crates/vault::time::zone`, re-exported so `crates/automations::cron`'s public API is unchanged and its 10,320 cron parity cases stayed green. `FireZone::resolve(trigger_tz, vault_zone)` takes **two** tiers and no third argument: the deletion of tier 3 is expressed in the signature, and `jiff` without `tz-system` makes the host zone structurally unreachable rather than merely unused. |
| **D-1020-S9** — the app crates depend on `centraid-vault` for `time` | `crates/vault::time` holds no SQL and could have lived in the kit; it lives in the vault because `task_lifecycle` needs it beside the operations, and a second copy in the kit is exactly the duplication S1 and S3 exist to prevent. |

**Lane Docs — the drive, the share fold, and the `core` schema's document half.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-DC1** — identity is the `core.document` wrapper | Options: key a drive row by content id (v0's wire shape makes it tempting, since the row carries both); key it by the wrapper. Adopted the wrapper: two documents may legitimately share bytes ([#352](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/352)), and a content-keyed row merges two members' unrelated files. Proved as a property over _n_ copies rather than a case at two, because a port that special-cased two would still be wrong at three. |
| **D-1020-DC2** — the fold is one module with **nine** named windows | Options: inline the share joins into each of the two callers, as v0 almost does; one module. Adopted one module, with `SHARE_WINDOWS` as data so a tenth window cannot appear unnamed. `via` is `document` or `folder` and never a guess; `delivered_at IS NOT NULL` is the only thing that makes a member `current`; a denial is `Reading::Denied`, and an empty vector is "shared with nobody". |
| **D-1020-DC3** — the `core` schema's document half, and eight named absences | Options: land all 27 of v0's `core.*` commands to hold the whole schema; land the ones Docs invokes plus the recognition writer. Adopted the second — nineteen. "A lane takes a whole schema for its slot" is about not having two writers of one file at once; it is not an instruction to write commands no consumer exercises. A test asserts the absences by name. |
| **D-1020-DC4** — bytes ride a door the app crate does not hold | Options: a `centraid://` request type in `crates/core`'s surface; a trait in the app crate. Adopted the trait, because promoting the three verbs touches two other lanes' files — filed as a hand-off. The never-inline list is lane F's, carried unchanged and refused **at the request** rather than at the surface. **No type is not permission**: a document whose representation this vault cannot read is offered, never embedded. |
| **D-1020-DC5** — the seed is a generator, not a port of the script | It reads no clock and holds no path: the bodies are declared facts and `now` is a civil date the caller states, so two runs of one date write the same rows. |
| **D-1020-DC6** — the polymorphic reference, re-judged rather than cited | Docs' reads **do** depend on `access_provenance`'s `(entity_type, entity_id)` and the share plane's `(subject_type, subject_id)`, and both are load-bearing: the share fold matches **per subject type, never by id alone**, because a document id and a folder concept id are different namespaces. What the re-judgement finds is not the polymorphism but its consequence — `access_provenance` has no foreign key onto `core_entity`, so it is not an entity, so the gateway's paged door cannot serve it at all. **The pair is fine. The band's absence from the entity registry is not.** |
| **D-1020-DC7** — there is no `core.add_content_item`, and the ceiling is not 1 MiB | The content item is minted inside `add_document`, `edit_document` and `replace_document_content`. 1 MiB is `core.content_item`'s **replica** rule about when a seat stops carrying a body's value; what a command enforces is `INLINE_BODY_BUDGET_BYTES`, **64 KiB** of decoded text, sixteen times tighter, because a text body cannot redirect to the CAS at all — the FTS feed decodes it in-transaction and a trigger cannot do I/O. The `data:`-URI door has its own ceiling of 360,000 characters. All three are ported with their own predicates. |
| **D-1020-DC8** — the inline binary path is a receipted refusal, **per input shape** | Claiming a staged sha is pure row work and v0 says so, so the whole `staged_sha` path and the whole `text/*` path are real and a scanned PDF files, versions and restores for real; only bytes pasted inline as base64 that this vault does not already hold refuse — as a **precondition** with its own predicate, so the refusal is receipted with an owner-facing sentence rather than thrown. Bytes already held are exempt, because the mint dedupes before it would spill. |
| **D-1020-DC9** — `enrich_request` rows are not written from `core.*` | `enrich.*` is the automations lane's schema; writing another lane's table from this one is how two writers of one plane happen. Hand-off. |
| **D-1020-DC10** — the declared drive window is walked to its stated size | v0 asks for its 2,000-row window as one page and `MAX_PAGE_ROWS` clamps it to 500, so a drive answers **500 of 7,600 live documents** and discards the cursor that says there are more. A list is where a clamp is defensible, but **the clamp has to be the one the caller asked for and not one the page contract imposed behind it**; `core_tag.tagged_at` is `NOT NULL`, so the keyset walk is continuable. Photos takes one page because its own sort column is nullable and a walk there would silently drop every NULL. This is a behaviour divergence from the oracle **at volume** that the parity fixture cannot reach — its corpus is six documents — and it is named for that reason. |

**Lane Locker — the member key the gateway never holds.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-L1** — founding is on a seat | `MemberKeyCustody` has `on_seat` and `with_store` and **no gateway constructor**; the vault learns that a generation exists and when, never the key. Options: the gateway mints and hands out (the door this wave deletes); the first seat mints and the others adopt; a KDF over the passphrase with no stored key at all, which makes rotation a re-derivation every seat must agree on at the same instant and loses the recovery kit. Adopted the second. |
| **D-1020-L2** — the key door is deleted **structurally**, in three places | `Verb` loses `Reveal`; `SealedSubject::new` refuses `locker`; `core::api::reveal` is role-typed. Options: a runtime check in the reveal handler; a feature flag; make the request unrepresentable. **A check is a line somebody deletes.** Adopted the third, and the gate test falsifies it against a gateway that still has the door. |
| **D-1020-L3** — the seat is the unlock boundary | `SESSION_TIMEOUT_MS` 5 min, `REVEAL_WINDOW_MS` 30 s, `PASSPHRASE_MINIMUM` 12, `WRAP_ITERATIONS` 600,000 — v0's exact numbers. A `Session` **locks as a side effect** of being asked too late, so a caller cannot hold a stale handle. |
| **D-1020-L4** — three key layers, three AADs, never one helper | [D-1020-R2](#wave-2-lane-rulings-1020) upheld: `seal` binds `table.column:rowid`, `locker_key` binds `rowId‖keyId`, `member_key` binds `vaultId‖keyId‖deviceId`. |
| **D-1020-L5** — the gate is a **search**, not an assertion | Five planted plaintexts are hunted across every sealed table through the paged door, eight command answers, the seat snapshot compressed **and** inflated, the backup base, and the vault file with its WAL and SHM — plus a non-vacuity assertion that the paged door _did_ serve `lk1:`. Asserting the reveal API refuses, and asserting no file holds a key, are its two weakest legs and are kept as named tests beside it. |
| **D-1020-L6** — a derivation that needs plaintext answers **addresses** | `locker.watchtower` answers the rows to score and `locker.totp_code` answers an address plus a receipt; the folds live where a seat calls them with what it unwrapped. Keeping v0's shapes and inventing the values is **a security screen that invents an all-clear** — the worst failure this app has. |
| **D-1020-L7** — `locker.export` writes the receipt and the seat writes the file | The manifest's two dead fields go with it, asserted as **exactly** two edits against v0's `app.json` byte for byte. |
| **D-1020-L8** — rotation is retire-before-insert, gated both ends | The precondition and the postcondition both walk `LOCKER_ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` rather than a list, so a sealed column added to the registry is covered without anybody remembering. |
| **D-1020-L9** — the seat mints the id of any row that carries a secret | The AAD binds ciphertext to its row id, so a gateway-minted id would mean the gateway chose what the ciphertext is bound to. |
| **D-1020-L10** — the origin spec is promoted, not copied | `contracts/origin-matching-v1.json`, 24 vectors, asserted byte-identical against the extension's copy and run through all three implementations. `psl`'s version number is the list's date; a bump re-runs the spec in both trees. |
| **D-1020-L11** — the member-key envelope uses HKDF over a one-time transfer secret, not a public-key box | Options: an X25519 sealed box, which adds an asymmetric primitive for one message; reusing the device ed25519 keys, which needs an ed25519→X25519 conversion and re-keys the format golden; HKDF over a transfer secret the two seats already share through pairing. Adopted the third. |
| **D-1020-L12** — three v0 findings are recorded, not fixed | Under [R-1020-35](#decisions--lane-v-1020)'s "anything wider is a finding", each asserted so a fix turns the test red rather than passing silently. |

**Lane extension — the Companion over native messaging.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-X1** — the extension is not a seat and can never become one | No vault, no `K`, no unwrap, asserted over the sources **and the built tree**: no `WebAssembly`/`wasm-unsafe-eval`, no `iroh`, no `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`WebSocket`, no `indexedDB`, no `crypto.subtle` key operation. `digest` and `getRandomValues` are allowed and named. The lint strips comments first, because a lint that matched its own explanation would be a lint somebody fixes by explaining less. |
| **D-1020-X2** — eighteen methods, one table, and the census says seventeen | `handleCompanionRequest` has **eighteen** `case` arms. The generator asserts the count, the Rust enum is asserted against the fixture, and the shipped table is asserted against the same file. An unknown name is refused with a typed frame naming it — **before** the capability check, because "that method does not exist" tells the extension to update where `no-capability` would send a member to fix something that is not wrong. |
| **D-1020-X3 (extension)** — chunking, and an honest stop | `stage:begin/chunk/end`, 512 KiB of raw bytes per frame, a content-addressed handle at the end. The handle is real; **claiming it into `blob_staging` is the byte door lane Docs handed off**, so `stage:end` answers `claimed: false, pending: "bytes-door"` and the capture carries the sha. Having the host write the staging row itself was refused — it holds no vault and would be a second writer; refusing large captures is a capture button that works on short pages. |
| **D-1020-X4** — the badge is pushed, and the alarm is kept | The staleness contract is **"live while the port is open, at most one minute otherwise"**, stated in `extension/README.md` and asserted against the constant. Push-only was tempting and wrong: a push needs an open port and MV3 closes workers whenever it likes, so it trades a bounded staleness for an unbounded one. |
| **D-1020-X5** — the extension id is a release artifact, and a missing one is `null` | `contracts/extension/ids.json` carries Firefox's author-chosen id (pinned, and the lint asserts the manifest agrees) and `null` with a reason for both Chrome channels. A placeholder id was drafted and **deleted**: `native-host install` refuses an empty allowlist precisely so a host manifest cannot exist without a real id, and a fixture carrying an invented one reads as settled when it is not. |
| **D-1020-X6** — the seat is where the fill happens, and the order is match → receipt → plaintext | `reveal_for_fill` reads the row, matches the page origin against **the row's own stored policy**, checks the session, writes the reveal receipt, and only then calls the seat's `fill_grant` — whose own origin match is the second wall. A first draft wrote a placeholder receipt id inside the grant's closure and the real receipt afterwards; that inverted the order and is the bug this note exists to record. `locker_unlock` and `locker_enrol` are **renderer-only** at the seat: _a door that can raise the passphrase prompt is a door that can be used to phish it_, and the browser is the caller that sentence is about. |
| **D-1020-X7** — one port, closed when idle | Lane F's port-per-request had a good reason (a native port holds a process). Two facts moved it: the seat's capability token is **single-use**, so one port per request means the shell minting a token per keystroke; and a pushed badge needs an open port. Adopted a long-lived port with a 30-second idle close — lane F's property, bounded by **use** rather than by request count. |
| **D-1020-X8** — origin matching moved to the seat; the extension normalises only | v0 bundles `tldts` and the Public Suffix List into the extension: a third copy of a security policy, with its own staleness, in the least trusted process. A third copy could only ever disagree, and a disagreement shows up as a login the picker offers and the seat then refuses. What stays in the extension is eligibility and `URL`-level normalisation. Consequence, and it is the good one: **the Companion now bundles no third-party code at all.** |
| **D-1020-X9** — a new local-channel message is additive and does not bump `LOCAL_PROTOCOL_VERSION` | The constant's own rule is _bumped when a message changes shape_; four added variants change none. A client that never sends them is unaffected, and a host that sends one to an older seat gets `Unsupported` with a sentence. |
| **D-1020-X10** — `blocking-count` is four named reads, windowed at the badge's own cap | v0's four sources as catalogue statements, `limit` 100 — because the badge renders at most `99`, so a hundredth row is already "99+" and a wider read would answer a number nothing shows. `capped` is reported separately rather than folded into the count. |
| **D-1020-X11** — `extension-unit` runs the type programs and the lint, not a second vitest | The Companion's tests are in `desktop-unit`'s project; duplicating them would be a second thing to keep green for six seconds a run. What `extension-unit` adds is what that project cannot: `tsc` over the shipped tree with `types: []`, and a lint about two files **outside** this tree. `extension-e2e` is `nightly` because an extension needs a full Chromium and therefore a display. |

**Lane Notes — the FTS door, the six queries, and the history graph the schema still permits.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-N1** — text search is its own crate, and Locker's absence from it is structural | Options: a `search` module inside `crates/vault`, which makes every app that searches depend on the whole vault; a fold inside `crates/apps/notes`, which puts a table of physical column names where nothing checks them against the sealed registry; **adopted** `crates/search`, a door with a `Search` trait. The crate earns its existence by being **the place the check can run**: `assert_no_sealed_column` at construction, a second check of each domain's live index columns, and `SearchError::NotADomain` for anything outside the seven — never an empty page, because an empty page reads as "no matches", a different claim about the vault. `sql-confinement`'s allowlist gains `crates/search` for the same reason it holds `crates/vault`. |
| **D-1020-N1a** — a search page is the kit's page | `SearchRequest::page` is `PageRequest`: required `limit`, no default, keyset continuation, `probe_limit = window + 1`. The consequence that mattered: the label filter and the caller's `excluded_ids` are **SQL predicates**, not post-filters, because dropping rows after the probe was counted makes `next` lie in both directions. |
| **D-1020-N2** — the one-graph rung is proposed here and landed by the root | [#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R20(a) deleted the content→content `revises` graph from every writer and drift ONT-22 is closed **in code**; the **schema still permits all of it**. Four rows nothing forbids: a self-parent; a parent belonging to another object; an `UPDATE` that re-points `parent_revision_id`; a `core_link` whose relation is `revises`. Options: leave it reader-enforced — but **ten** call sites now carry the same defence, and the honest answer every one gives is a refusal that leaves the member with no history at all; fix it in the readers, which is ten copies of a repair; **adopted** propose the rung with its tests and let the root land it. `contracts/migrations/002_revisions.sql` makes each unrepresentable and **refuses the migration** rather than silently dropping a row that already violates it. The cycle closes by construction, not by search: a revision is insert-only, so a fresh row cannot close a loop, and A→B→A needs a second statement that moves a parent, which the immutability trigger refuses. |
| **D-1020-N3** — the library asymmetry is the contract, not an oversight | A People-journal entry is a `knowledge.note` carrying a marker concept, **absent from the library, the trash shelf, the tag chips, `search` and the powerbox — and reachable by id** ([#834](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/834)). Spelled as the **absence of a filter**: `load_note` does no journal read at all. Re-judged rather than cited: a port that unified the two would either leak journal entries onto the notes shelf or break the People screen that opens one, so the asymmetry stays, held as a test in both directions so nobody tidies it. |
| **D-1020-N4** — the inline body budget is 64 KiB, and it is a receipted precondition | Not the 1 MiB replica ceiling. A body over it was first an `Err` from the handler, which is a crash a member cannot act on; it is now a named precondition, so the refusal carries the predicate that refused it. |
| **D-1020-N5** — the editor contract is answered by the handler, not by a second implementation | The screen fixtures describe one note; the kit seeds exactly that note and the test asserts the `note` query's own answer against the fixture. A test that restated the fixture's fields would pass while the query drifted. |
| **D-1020-N6** — the command count is a per-schema table, not a total | So two lanes landing schemas in the same wave do not fight over one number. |
| **D-1020-N7** — the link relations are seeded at found time | `core.link_entities` refused `references` in the port because the Rust bootstrap seeded no SKOS vocabulary at all — a refusal that read as a policy decision and was an absence. `SEED_RELATIONS` is the seven v0 seeds ([#272](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/272)); `revises` is **not** among them, and that omission is D-1020-N2's fourth guard made true today. |
| **D-1020-N8** — the year-3 axis reports what this box can prove and names what it cannot | The typing-latency target is a round trip through the **editor's state machine on a reference device**, and that machine is Kotlin with no Rust twin. What is measured here is the half that is Rust; the Kotlin half is an owner hand-off. |
| **D-1020-N9** — the card fold is the app's and the card decision is the gateway's | A `[[wikilink]]` compiles to a `core.link` and the shelf draws the far end; **resolvable-if-linked** is the consent rule (a live link authorises rendering the far end even with no read scope, and a ref with neither is `denied` **per ref** rather than a failed screen). Resolving cards inside the app would be the app deciding consent; resolving them in the gateway would be the gateway folding presentation. Adopted a `CardDoor` trait, with an owner impl and a no-cards impl, so neither half guesses at the other. |
| **D-1020-N10** — a created note's `updated_at` is the vault's clock, fixed at the v0 source | Under [R-1020-35](#decisions--lane-v-1020). |

**Lane People — the largest action surface.**

| Id | The ruling, the options, and what was adopted |
| --- | --- |
| **D-1020-PE1** — three denials, three readings, one type | Options: `Option<T>` per reading, as the wire shape suggests; one `ReadState<T> = Loading \| Denied \| Ready(T)` per reading. Adopted the second. **The census's sentence is not true of v0**: `queries/person.ts` catches **one** of the three and puts the rest in one `Promise.all` under one `try`, so revoking `tally.obligation` — a scope over another app's table — answers `{person: null}` and a member who uninstalled Tally loses their grandfather's birthday. The port makes all three independent, and the fact lives **inside** the state: a denied sharing read has no `linked` and no `vault_count` to draw a chip on, where v0 ships `linked: null` **and** `vault_count: 0` on the same row. The fourth candidate is stated rather than modelled — revoking `social.contact_channel` denies the whole sheet, here as in v0 — and is an owner question. |
| **D-1020-PE2** — `merge_party` is exhaustive **by construction**, not by registry | Options: port v0's `ENTITY_POINTERS` list; generate the sweep from the live schema. Adopted the generator, and the first thing it found is that **v0 forgot** a column. Sixty columns, three kinds; the one column the engine cannot see is named in code with the reason it cannot, so a second such column is a compile-time edit rather than a silent gap. Collision policies are data: `sum` where a share is a number and adds, `demote` where a flag claims primacy, `drop-duplicate`, plus `degenerate` and `revoke` — a standing answer is **dated shut, never deleted**. |
| **D-1020-PE3** — the test-to-action ratio is fixed here | Every command has a fixture case, and each of the fourteen `once` commands has an idempotency replay. Six v0 test files against 29 actions is the worst ratio in the tree; the port's answer is 61 scripted steps of which 15 are **refusals on purpose**, because a script of only happy paths cannot tell a port that reproduces the gates from one that has none. |
| **D-1020-PE4** — `people.limit` is 20–10,000 and v0 stops at 9,999 | Options: reproduce the clamp; honour the declared maximum and lower the port's window for comparison. Adopted the second — v0's clamp is a look-ahead row the same file no longer takes, so the number is a fossil. The parity mapping lowers the port's window onto v0's so the comparison stays exact and the divergence is in a receipt rather than in a changed fixture. Why the number is 10,000 at all is an owner question. |
| **D-1020-PE5** — one nullable sort column, and it is the trash shelf | Options: leave the shelf's order and rely on the window never being continued; re-order every statement nulls-last defensively. Adopted the first, **with the enumeration as a test**. The kit's door reads the DDL rather than the `where`, so a predicate that makes a column non-null does not persuade it; the shelf is safe because `TRASH_ROWS == MAX_PAGE_ROWS`, and a window of 501 refuses. Where such a column must genuinely be continued the adopted order is **nulls last, then by primary key** — stated so the next lane has an answer rather than a choice. |
| **D-1020-PE6** — three manifest confirmations, and one command-level park | The manifest's `confirmation` is the **dispatching surface's** gate; exactly one of the twenty-nine commands carries `confirm: true`, and it is `core.merge_party` — a **non-owner park**, because an irreversible fold of one person into another is not a thing an assistant does on a member's behalf. Neither gate substitutes for the other; a port that collapses them loses the park. |
| **D-1020-PE7** — the birthday rail is civil time, in the vault's zone | v0's `daysUntilMonthDay` reads the **host's** local midnight, so a gateway on a VPS running UTC and a member in UTC−07:00 disagree for seven hours of every day and the _birthday, daily brief_ notification fires a day early or late depending on which machine answered. The ported module has **no clock in it at all**, so the wrong one cannot be read. **29 February clamps to 28 February in a common year**, and the fixture carries a leap-day birthday because a rail without one cannot show the clamp. |
| **D-1020-PE8** — a command's name and its owner schema must agree | v0 names three commands `people.*_contact_channel` and declares `ownerSchema: "social"` on all three. The gateway authorises by owner schema and the caller types the name, so the two halves answer to two different schemas. The registry refuses the split by construction; they are `people`-owned here, and the manifest's three narrow `social` act scopes are consequently dead weight. |
| **D-1020-PE9** — the recurrence rollover is not People's to own | `people.complete_task` denies a task carrying an `rrule` with a sentence naming `schedule.organize_task`. Reproducing it here would re-introduce exactly the drift ([#996](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/996) R21, ONT-27) that took People's own `toggle_task` away: a repeating task completed from People never got its next occurrence, because only Tasks' code knew how to spawn one. |
| **D-1020-PE10** — which parity lists are compared in order, and which as sets | Options: sort the fixture; compare six lists as sets and name them. Adopted the second — **editing a generated fixture by hand is not available and re-ordering the generator's output would hide the fact**. A list whose statement orders by a minted id is ordered by the canonicaliser's first-appearance tokens, not by the vault. Six lists are named in the test; every other list is compared in order. The set's sort key is a **key-sorted normal form** rather than a printed value — see [traps/serde-json-preserve-order.md](traps/serde-json-preserve-order.md). |

### The close pass — the open questions, and the corrections (#1020)

Written at the umbrella's close pass. The receipt is the evidence layer and this section is the state: where the two disagree, the receipt is what happened and this is what is current.

**The fourteen open questions of [#1020](https://github.com/srikanth235/centraid/issues/1020), each with its answer as it now stands.** Q-1020-1 ruled all fourteen recommendations as written; where a wave then produced evidence, the row says so and names it.

| # | Question | Where it stands |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | SQLCipher, or plain SQLite plus sealed columns? | **Ruled and shipped.** [R-1020-8](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020): plain SQLite. Custody landed in wave 2 lane R (`crates/vault::custody`, three layers, three AADs) and the member key in wave 4 lane Locker. |
| 2 | Push wake: none, or a content-free relay? | **Ruled, not built.** [R-1020-14](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020): a second route on the existing OAuth worker, in the wave 4 notifications lane — which has not run. Nothing in the core depends on it, which is what makes the ruling cheap to reverse. |
| 3 | An off-by-default HTTPS blob door? | **Open on evidence that does not exist yet.** [R-1020-15](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) prefers no door at a stated threshold of 500 assets per night. The measurement is the **iOS transfer experiment**, which needs a physical iPhone; the protocol, the five states, the four evidence file names and the decision table are written (`mobile/maestro/ios-transfer-experiment.md`) and the run is owner hand-off 1 of lane E. |
| 4 | Version window N | **Ruled and shipped.** N = 3; the handshake, `UpgradeRequired` and `DowngradeRefused` are in `crates/ontology` and `crates/core` with tests at both window ends and a test that the ends are distinct. |
| 5 | A local gateway on the desktop | **Ruled and shipped.** [R-1020-13](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020); `centraid gateway install` writes the unit and never enables it. The three-OS install is wave 5. |
| 6 | Carry the v0 React app UIs on desktop | **Ruled yes, deferred with its reason.** [D-1020-F16](#wave-3-lane-rulings-1020): mounting `app-inline` means adapting v0's `@centraid/client` context, not a data source. The desktop seat ships its screens over the same named reads and keeps the parity claim; the mount is a hand-off in `desktop/README.md`. |
| 7 | Repository layout | **Ruled and shipped.** [R-1020-11](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020); v0 is pinned as the oracle, its gates are off pull requests since wave 1, and wave 6 deletes it. The retirement commit is prepared on `claude/1020-retire-v0` and is **not merged** — see the receipt's close section for what gates it. |
| 8 | The VPS trust premise | **Ruled and shipped.** [R-1020-9](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020); wave 4 lane Locker ships the member key and deletes the reveal door structurally. Two boundaries are **explicitly outside** what the gate proves and are stated in [SECURITY.md](../SECURITY.md#the-gateway-on-rented-hardware--v1-trust-premise-1020): a host with root reading `K` out of an unlocked seat's process memory, and connector tokens, which stay host-readable by design. |
| 9 | Recognition placement | **Ruled, and one rung of it is vocabulary rather than a device lane.** [R-1020-16](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) and [D-1020-AU3-1](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020): a fourth tier value, `off < on-device < sealed-gateway < gateway`. The device half — the stamp's `site`/`device_id`, `EnrichAsk`, `enrich.submit_derivation` — is named as root-integration scope and is not built. |
| 10 | Protobuf screen contracts, or sealed classes + SKIE? | **One criterion tripped; the ruling holds until a device answers the other two.** [D-1020-X3](#decisions--wave-3-close-lane-x3-1020): criterion (a) measured **1.72×**, and (b) and (c) are device measurements nobody has taken. A second trip flips it with no further debate. |
| 11 | Tauri instead of Electron | **Ruled: not v1.** [R-1020-18](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020). The sidecar boundary is what keeps it cheap, and wave 3 lane F built exactly that boundary. |
| 12 | Blob store backend | **Ruled: local filesystem only.** [R-1020-19](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020). |
| 13 | The physical-device lane | **Ruled, and the lane exists with nothing attached.** [R-1020-20](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) and [D-1020-G4](#wave-3-lane-rulings-1020): four device cells that **fail** on a runner claiming `CENTRAID_DEVICE_RUNNER` with no phone, and skip loudly otherwise. The runner is an owner hand-off. |
| 14 | Windows as a first-class desktop target | **Ruled yes; one door is unbuilt and refuses rather than pretending.** [R-1020-21](#v1-platform--rust-core-kmp-shell-electron-seat-gateway-anywhere-1020) and [D-1020-F15](#wave-3-lane-rulings-1020): `centraid seat --socket` exits 3 on Windows until the named-pipe DACL is written **and observed to refuse somebody**. |

**Corrections to the plan, and to the census the waves were briefed from.** Recorded because a number in a brief that nobody corrects becomes a number in a doc.

- **The Companion answers eighteen methods, not seventeen** ([D-1020-X2](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)). The generator asserts the count against `handleCompanionRequest`'s own `case` arms, so a future reader gets the number from the code.
- **The share fold has nine bounded windows, not eight** ([D-1020-DC2](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)). The miscount came from reading a declaration line as a window; `SHARE_WINDOWS` names all nine and a test is the count.
- **The `core` schema's idempotency split does not add up in the census** — `27 (17 / 6 once / 2 retry-safe)` sums to 25. The `once` arm is **eight**.
- **Places is a facet of Photos, not an app** ([D-1020-P5](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)). There is no `crates/apps/places` and there should not be one: the phrase logic is the load-bearing half and it is Photos' own.
- **The `schedule` schema carries four confirm-gated commands, not three.** Census A0 read one of the two files v0 declares them in. The port matches v0 exactly; the census row is the thing that was wrong, and the number is used as an exit check.
- **`queries/person.ts` makes one sharing read deny independently, not three** ([D-1020-PE1](#wave-4-lane-rulings-1020)). The census sentence describes the app's intent, which is why the port was written to the intent rather than from the sentence.
- **Two rulings share the id `D-1020-X3`** — wave 3's close ruled open question 10 under it, and wave 4's extension lane used it for the staging chunk protocol. Both are recorded above; the extension one is disambiguated as `D-1020-X3 (extension)`. Named rather than silently renumbered, because a ruling id that moves is a citation that breaks.
